## §0 — Grounding

This demo exists to prove three capabilities that `onnxruntime-genai` does not have:

1. **Continuous batching** — new requests join an in-flight decode batch instead of waiting for it to drain.
2. **Paged KV cache** — KV memory is allocated in fixed-size blocks that can be shared and reclaimed.
3. **Prefix caching** — a shared prompt prefix is computed once and reused.

**The demo is a PURE CLIENT.** Static HTML + vanilla ES modules. **No build step, no `npm install`, no bundler.** At most one pinned CDN library with SRI. The server serves it; the demo never loads a model, never manages a process, never shells out.

> **🔑 SERVER TOPOLOGY — RULED (Lead): TWO SERVERS, one per model, both modes shown honestly.**
> `/v1/status` is a **node-level** contract — its own docs say *"All values are model-agnostic; `node_id` names this node, never a model"* (`routes/mod.rs:110-116`) — and it has **no model dimension**. Multi-model mode gives each model its **own `EngineDriver`** (`state.rs:376`), so on one server `/v1/status` could only report one engine's numbers or blend two, leaving the visitor unable to tell which model's KV stats they were seeing. **That is precisely the ambiguity AC6 exists to prevent, and it would have been invisible on screen.** Two origins keeps `/v1/status` unambiguous by construction and requires zero additional server work. **The mode switch doubles as the server switch.**

### §0.1 — Verified constraints (read before designing anything)

| Constraint | Evidence | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| `/v1/status` **hardcodes 10 of 13 fields to zero** | handler at `routes/admin.rs:41-86`; `routes/mod.rs:110-142` is only the **struct definition** | **Trace every field to a handler that computes it, not a struct that declares it.** This distinction is the whole project. |
| Genuinely measured today | `queue_depth`, `active_sessions`, `healthy`, `node_id` (`admin.rs:45-61`) | Everything else needs plumbing or an em-dash. |
| No CORS layer, no static-file serving in the router | `lib.rs` (absent); `tower-http` not a declared dep | Demo must be served **same-origin**. Non-negotiable — see §2. |
| `max_batch` hardcoded to 4 | `server/src/state.rs:25 DEFAULT_MAX_BATCH`, no CLI flag | `batch_utilization` has no real denominator until `--max-batch` is surfaced. **Keep it** — watching occupancy climb 1→2→3→4 and pin is the batching proof. Above ~4 the slider demonstrates **queueing**, which is still honest — but the narration must say which is which. |
| Preemption is **structurally disabled** on the batching path | `batched.rs:757` hardcodes `PreemptionPolicy::Disabled`; documented as structural at `:713-717` | A preemption counter in Mode A is correct and **permanently zero**. Mode A's swimlane is **4-state, by design and forever.** |
| `vram-limit` admin endpoint is **dead** | `state.rs:152` hardcodes `allow_runtime_override:false` (`config.rs:602`), no CLI/config path — a live server returns **403**; and `crates/onnx-genai-engine/src/engine/governor.rs:171` **[cited as :167 — DRIFTED +4]**'s `TODO` **is** the eviction step | **Cannot create memory pressure.** ~~Use the honest fallback: raise concurrency + prompt length until the pool genuinely fills.~~ **🔴 STRUCK — CONCURRENCY IS INERT (both driver loops run generation INLINE, `driver.rs:147-149`, so concurrent requests QUEUE and never overlap; the pool never sees simultaneous sequences). PROMPT LENGTH still works. Use the replacement procedure — REPEATED PREFIXES ACROSS SEQUENTIAL REQUESTS — in AC163, and size the pool small (AC-capacity) so real pressure is reachable. Watch `hot_evictions`, NOT `allocation_failures`, which stays ZERO under maximum pressure.** |

## §1 — The Story

A visitor lands on the page knowing nothing. In **sixty seconds** they should be able to say, in their own words, what continuous batching is and why it matters. That is the bar.

The narrative arc:

1. **"Here is a request."** One request, one stream of tokens. Establish the unit.
2. **"Here are eight requests."** On a static-batching engine they queue. On ours they interleave. **Show both.**
3. **"Here is the memory."** The paged KV block table — blocks filling, being shared, being freed.
4. **"Here is what it cost."** Honest numbers, honest gaps.

5. **“And here is why you cannot have all of it at once.”** — **THE THESIS. This is the most technically interesting thing we have to say, and it is the one a visitor cannot get from any vendor's benchmark page.**

> ### 🔴 THE CENTRAL FINDING: ONE ARCHITECTURAL FACT, EXPRESSING ITSELF THREE TIMES
> **Continuous batching and paged KV DO NOT COMPOSE.** The paged KV cache owns **both** the page table **and** the prefix trie, so a path that bypasses the cache bypasses **both** — *one cause, two symptoms* (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §5.6). The batched path additionally owns its KV in **physical rows that cannot be swapped out and resumed in place**, so preemption is **structurally impossible** there (`crates/onnx-genai-engine/src/batched.rs:759`, `:757`).
> **The same fact independently broke three separate stories:**
> | Story | How it broke | Where the truth lives |
> |---|---|---|
> | **Prefix cache** | `prefix_cache_hit_len` is a hardcoded literal `0` on the batching path (`batched.rs:262`, `:486`) | Real on the **dynamic** server |
> | **Preemption** | `PreemptionPolicy::Disabled`, structural not default (`batched.rs:757`) | Possible only on the **dynamic** server |
> | **Memory pressure** | no paged pool exists to fill on the batching path; the vram knob evicts nothing (`byte_budget.rs:180-190`) | Real on the **dynamic** server |
>
> **THAT IS NOT THREE PIECES OF BAD LUCK. IT IS ONE ARCHITECTURAL FACT WE HIT THREE TIMES FROM THREE DIRECTIONS — and each time, the code said the same thing.**
> **The epistemics are the point: we did not read this in a design document. We tried to build three features, and the engine refused three times for the same reason.** That is why the demo runs **two servers** — not as a workaround, but because **the split IS the finding**, and a single page claiming all five capabilities at once would be advertising something no configuration of this engine can produce.
> **A visitor who leaves understanding ONLY this — that these capabilities are mutually exclusive, and WHY — has gotten more from the demo than any throughput number could give them.** Every honest em-dash on the page is this fact, made visible.

**The hero number is a COUNT, not a ratio: four sequences decoded in one forward pass, against one for onnxruntime-genai.** The visitor verifies it by counting rows on screen. See AC50 for why the previous `2.46x` headline was **withdrawn by its author** — it moved 10.8% from background load alone, and there were always two defensible ratios in the same paragraph.

**The demo drives 100% of its own load.** There is no background traffic, no synthetic worker, no hidden client. Everything on screen was caused by something the visitor did — that is what makes the numbers trustworthy and the swimlane client-observable.

> **We never simulate the static-batching baseline.** If we cannot measure it, we do not draw it. A fabricated "before" number is the single fastest way to destroy the credibility of a true "after" number.

## §2 — Scenarios (two-mode)

| Mode | Server / model | Scenarios | Panels that are LIVE | Panels that are `not-applicable` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Mode A** | scatter | **A — Continuous batching** | batch occupancy, swimlanes, queue depth, throughput | KV block table, prefix cache |
| **Mode B** | dynamic | **B — Prefix caching**, **C — Memory pressure** | KV block table, prefix cache, preemption | batch occupancy |

> **🔴 IT IS A DETECTED CAPABILITY PROFILE, NOT A MODE TOGGLE — ratified. THE VISITOR CANNOT CHOOSE; THE LOADED MODEL DECIDES.** So **there is no toggle to build, and building one would MISREPRESENT WHAT THE VISITOR CONTROLS.** The page **detects** the profile it is attached to and adapts. Less UI work and more honest at the same time.
> Consequences, all binding:
> - **🔒 CORRECTED (supersedes the "scenarios are filtered out" line I wrote earlier — that is now WRONG, do not build it): ALL THREE SCENARIO TABS ARE ALWAYS VISIBLE.** The two rulings are not exclusive once ordered correctly: **THE SWITCHER CHOOSES THE ORIGIN; DETECTION THEN DECIDES WHAT MOUNTS AGAINST WHATEVER ANSWERS.** Selecting a scenario points the page at that scenario's configured origin; on arrival the page **detects** the profile and mounts only what it genuinely supports. **A visitor must be able to reach all three scenarios** — filtering them out would hide the existence of half the product.
> - **⚠️ LIFECYCLE CHANGE — `meta.requires` IS RE-EVALUATED ON ORIGIN SWITCH, NOT ONCE AT PAGE LOAD.** @bb2ee824 @c8d9a40e: build for re-evaluation.
> - **Capability is DETECTED FROM THE SERVER, NEVER ASSUMED FROM A HARDCODED SCENARIO MAP.** This is the load-bearing half of the designer's insight and it survives intact: **a visitor pointing this at THEIR OWN server would otherwise get a lying dashboard.** That is the real reason for detection — not our two servers, but everyone else's one.
> - **Inactive panels collapse into ONE group card**, not one notice per panel. **Six identical capability notices is the wall-of-zeros failure in a politer typeface.**
> - **Profile banner** states BOTH halves in one sentence — e.g. *"static-cache profile · continuous batching LIVE · paged KV bypassed by design"*. Informational styling, **never a warning colour**. Naming both halves is what stops a visitor concluding something is broken.
> - **Profile S's KV panel is "Static KV decode rows"** — same grid component at `capacity = max_batch`, **different noun. Never call rows "pages"**; that re-imports the exact confusion we removed.

**Nothing is ever structurally pinned at zero.** Each scenario renders only the panels genuinely live on its own server. The emptiness in the right-hand column is **correct, by design, and is the central truth the demo teaches** — see the `not-applicable` state in §3.1.

> **🔒 FINAL: TWO SERVERS. One-server was confirmed technically viable and then REJECTED on evidence — the reasoning is non-obvious and worth knowing.**
> `metrics.rs:88` is a **process-wide `static REGISTRY`** with **no model dimension**: `prefix_cache_hits`/`lookups`, `batch_size`, `pending`, `active_sessions`, `ttft`, `e2e` and rejections are **all blended across models**. Adding a model dimension means refactoring a deliberately allocation-free static **on the hot path**, endangering AC33.
> **`static` means PER-PROCESS. Two servers = two processes = two registries — so the blending problem does not get mitigated, IT CEASES TO EXIST.** Every global counter becomes per-model for free, with zero hot-path risk. **Two servers costs two README lines and two origins in a config object; one server costs ~3 lines per endpoint plus a permanently blended prefix hit rate plus a silent-misattribution trap.**
> **Launch rules (binding):** each server uses **`--model <DIRECTORY>`**. **NEVER `--models-dir`** (loads 20+ subdirs eagerly — many GB, very long startup). **NEVER set `--max-loaded-models`** (default unlimited; setting it risks evicting a model mid-demo). **`models/` lives in the MAIN checkout** — paths must point at `/Users/justinc/Documents/GitHub/onnx-genai/models/`, not the worktree.
> **AC47 SURVIVES AS DEFENCE IN DEPTH**, explicitly kept by the Lead: two servers makes attribution unambiguous, but an echoed model id makes every response **self-describing to anyone curling it**, and costs nothing.

<!-- superseded analysis retained for reasoning history -->
> **🔑 ONE-SERVER WAS CONFIRMED VIABLE (now rejected — see above) — verified from source by @d7cf9b84: each model gets its own `Engine` and its own `EngineDriver` thread (`state.rs:370-376`), so the scatter model batches while the dynamic model does paged-KV work **concurrently in one process. There is no global mode.**
> **Use `--models-config` (`cli.rs:50`), NOT `--models-dir`** — `from_models_dir` (`models_config.rs:121-151`) eagerly loads **every** valid model subdir and `models/` has 20+.
> **If adopted, this dissolves the CORS blocker entirely** — one origin, no navigation required, and the scenario switcher becomes a pure UI control.
>
> **But read what the trade actually is, because it is not free.** Two servers made **AC42 (no cross-mode bleed) STRUCTURALLY IMPOSSIBLE TO VIOLATE** — the wrong model's numbers were not reachable from the page. **One server makes cross-mode bleed a matter of DISCIPLINE instead of shape**, and it fails *silently*: `registry.resolve("")` falls through to `default_id` (`registry.rs:329-341`), and the default is `specs[0]` after an **alphabetical** sort (`models_config.rs:151`, verified). ~~**`qwen2.5-0.5b` sorts before `qwen2.5-0.5b-scatter-v2`, so the DYNAMIC model wins the default and the batch panels would describe the WRONG MODEL during the batching scenario — with plausible numbers and no error.**~~ **🔒 CORRECTED IN PLACE (D131): THE ALPHABETICAL SORT LIVES IN `from_models_dir` ONLY, AND `run-demo.sh:233-247` LAUNCHES WITH `--model` + `--model-id qwen-scatter` / `qwen-dynamic` — ONE MODEL PER PROCESS, SO `specs[0]` IS THE ONLY MODEL AND `resolve("")` IS UNAMBIGUOUS. THE ALPHABETICAL TRAP CANNOT FIRE ON THE SHIPPING PATH; THE IDs `qwen2.5-0.5b*` ARE NOT THE SERVED IDs EITHER. THE ARGUMENT SURVIVES WITHOUT IT — SEE *"A CORRECT DEFAULT IS STILL AN UNLABELLED ONE."*** 
> **This is precisely the failure class we keep saying must be enforced by API shape rather than developer care. AC47 IS THE PRICE OF THE ONE-SERVER UPGRADE — adopt them together or not at all.** With `?model=` plus an echoed model id, one server is strictly better than two. Without it, two servers is safer.

> **⚠️ DO NOT HARDCODE PORTS.** Build against a **configurable per-scenario origin**. **The UI is identical either way** — a config line, not an architecture. Reference values today: scatter `:8123`, dynamic `:8124`.

> ### 🔴 CORS CONSTRAINT — THE MODE SWITCH MUST BE A NAVIGATION, NOT A FETCH SWAP. Verified against a live server.
> There is **zero CORS support anywhere in `crates/onnx-genai-server/src/`** — no `CorsLayer`, no `Access-Control-*` header — and **`tower-http` is not a declared dependency** of the server crate. A different port is a **different origin**, so a page served by server A calling `fetch()` on server B has its response **blocked by the browser**.
>
> **Measured, not inferred** (live server on `:8137`, 2026-07-29):
> ```
> $ curl -i -H "Origin: http://127.0.0.1:8123" http://127.0.0.1:8137/v1/status
> HTTP/1.1 200 OK
> content-type: application/json
> ```
> **Zero `access-control-*` headers.** And the preflight is worse than absent:
> ```
> $ curl -i -X OPTIONS -H "Origin: ..." -H "Access-Control-Request-Method: GET" .../v1/status
> HTTP/1.1 405 Method Not Allowed
> ```
> **Read what that `200` means, because it is the trap.** The server answers *fine* — it does not know or care about origins. **The browser fetches the response and then refuses to let JavaScript read it.** So it works in curl, in every unit test, and in Node — and throws an opaque `TypeError: Failed to fetch` the first time it runs in a browser against two servers, with no stack trace pointing at the cause.
>
> **This would fail in the most misleading way available to us.** A CORS block is indistinguishable from an unreachable server at the `fetch` layer, so our missing-server state would tell a visitor *"not detected — run `<command>`"* about a server **that is already running**, and the copy-paste fix would not fix it. Confidently worded, actively false, unactionable — **inside the error state we designed specifically to be trustworthy.**
>
> **RESOLUTION (preserves the two-server ruling, needs no server change): the mode switch performs a real page NAVIGATION to the other origin.** Every fetch is then **same-origin by construction**, CORS never arises, and we add no dependency. **Both servers must therefore serve `GET /demo` — see AC39.** This is also the only construction that satisfies the Architect's invariant **I6 ("same-origin only, no CORS ever")** while keeping two servers.

### Scenario A — Continuous batching (Mode A)
The visitor raises concurrency and watches occupancy climb **1→2→3→4** and pin. Swimlanes show decode segments **advancing in lockstep across concurrent requests** — that lockstep IS the proof of continuous batching, and it is fully client-observable.

**Static always runs first** (@0837fdf9), so continuous never benefits from a warm cache. The conservative ordering **disadvantages our own headline number**; careful readers notice that kind of thing.

### Scenario B — Prefix caching (Mode B)
**Empirically verified by me** on the dynamic model (prebuilt binary, `:8134`): `prefix_cache_hits` **0→1**, `hit_rate` **0.5**, end-to-end **1.53s → 1.22s**. The payoff is real and measured, not projected.

> **🔴 BINDING — THE HIT RATE MUST BE MEASURED CLIENT-SIDE VIA TTFT DELTA, AND MUST NEVER BE READ FROM `/v1/status`.** `prefix_cache_hits` / `_lookups` are **process-global statics with no model dimension** (`metrics.rs:88`), and `_lookups` increments on **every completed generation** with no predicate (`metrics.rs:130-132`). **Under one server, scatter traffic inflates the denominator of the dynamic model's hit rate** — so the number would be wrong *because of activity in a different scenario*, and it would still look entirely plausible.
> Note this cuts **both** ways and the one-server upgrade makes it worse, not better: my own verified 0→1 measurement was taken on an **isolated** server. **The same counter under mixed traffic is not the same measurement.** TTFT delta is what we actually claim — *the second request is faster* — and it is measured on the thing the visitor can feel.

### Scenario C — Memory pressure (Mode B)
**Do NOT build this around a VRAM-limit slider that claims to EVICT.** The endpoint lowers the ceiling and **never touches usage**: `ByteBudget::reconfigure` (`scheduler/src/byte_budget.rs:180-190`) sets `state.limit` and leaves `state.used` alone — and **the repo's own test is named `reconfigure_lower_reports_overage_without_evicting`** (`byte_budget.rs:301-312`). *The test name is the answer.* The governor computes `overage_bytes` and an ordered `eviction_order` (`crates/onnx-genai-scheduler/src/governor.rs:158-167`), but there are **zero consumers anywhere outside that file**, and our driver discards the outcome entirely (`driver.rs:735-739`). **Drag the slider down and the grid sits inert.**

**RULED 🔴 THE HONEST FRAMING IS "THE POOL STOPS ACCEPTING", NOT "THE POOL GETS RECLAIMED" — and @d7cf9b84's version is the better SCENARIO, not merely the surviving one.** Lower the limit **first**, *then* send requests: `used > limit` means the next allocation is refused, producing **genuine `allocation_failures`, admission stalls, and visible queueing**, with no need to fill a huge pool first.
> **Why this is an upgrade and not a consolation prize:** eviction is an *internal housekeeping* event — a visitor must take our word for what the animation means. **Backpressure is externally observable**: it propagates out to admission and the visitor *feels* it in their own requests slowing down. **We are trading an animation the visitor must trust for a consequence they can verify.** That is the same trade this document keeps making.

**🔴 BUILD SCENARIO C ON `allocation_failures`, AND RUN THE DEMO WITH A SMALL KV BUDGET.** Per @e00032a4: `allocations`, `allocation_failures`, `frees`, `hot_evictions`, `prefix_evictions` are **real today** (`PageStats`, `page_table.rs:564-576`), as are `filled_slots`/`slot_capacity` (`:588-590`) — which show **fragmentation, the actual cost of paging** — and `shared` (`:594`). **`allocation_failures` is the honest "pool under real pressure" signal.**
> **Capacity is a PRODUCT decision, not just a payload one.** Measured **14,612 pages** on qwen2.5-0.5b: three arrays that long is ~250 KB/poll ≈ **1 MB/s at 4 Hz**, and **nobody can read 14,612 cells.** Run the demo with a **small KV budget so capacity lands in the low hundreds**: **a small pool visibly fills, shares and evicts within the span of a demo; a large pool never fills at all.** This is the honest replacement for the dead `vram-limit` knob — **we are not shrinking the pool to fake pressure, we are sizing it so real pressure is reachable in sixty seconds.**

Real pressure, real requests, no privileged endpoint, **no `simulated` badge.**

> Scenario C deliberately drives the server into memory pressure, **so slow and dropped polls are the EXPECTED behaviour there.** The single most dramatic moment of the demo is also the moment we are most likely to present stale numbers as live ones. See AC45.

---

## §3 — Dashboard Metrics & The Honesty Rules

### §3.1 — The Field shape — 🔒 **FINAL AND RATIFIED. FIVE STATES + THREE ATTRIBUTION KEYS. BUILD THIS AND NOTHING ELSE.**

```js
{ value,
  state: 'measured' | 'pending' | 'stale' | 'unavailable' | 'not-applicable',
  source,          // 'server'|'client'|'derived'|'estimated'|'simulated' — badge class
  endpoint,        // '/v1/status' — curl-able, for the audit
  server,          // 'scatter'|'dynamic' — WHICH ENGINE
  classification,  // sub-reason, ONLY when state === 'unavailable'
  unit, label, reason, observedAtMs, derivedFrom }
```

**🔴 `origin` IS A RETIRED WORD. It meant an ENDPOINT PATH in one ratified broadcast and a SERVER in another, so it is poisoned — no amount of care makes it safe.** Three keys, never merged.

**✅ `server` IS THE KEY THAT MAKES AC41/AC42 TESTABLE AT ALL.** An endpoint path is **byte-identical on both servers**, so without `server` there is literally no key answering *“which engine produced this number?”* — the two-server demo's central honesty requirement.
> **⚠️ AND THE DETAIL THAT WOULD HAVE QUIETLY BROKEN EVERYTHING: `server` IS SET ON CLIENT-MEASURED FIELDS TOO.** The browser measured TTFT — **but it measured it AGAINST AN ENGINE**: `{ source:'client', endpoint:null, server:'scatter' }`. **Miss this and EVERY HERO METRIC IS THE UNATTRIBUTED ONE**, because our hero metrics are exactly the client-measured ones. *(@0837fdf9.)*

**📝 MY FOUR-STATE CONCESSION IS OVERTAKEN — recorded rather than quietly overwritten, because several people built against it.** I conceded five→four when `classification` looked like a cheaper axis. **The Lead overruled it on a FRAMING argument that changed the design rather than the label:** `not-applicable` is **a teaching surface, not an error state**, and **the demo's single most interesting finding**. Burying it in a secondary field means **a panel author must dig to discover it, so it renders apologetically by default.** @0837fdf9 accepted the same reasoning and retired their own `BYPASSED` classification for it. **`classification` survives as the sub-reason for `unavailable` ONLY** (`DOCUMENTED_ZERO` vs `NOT_PLUMBED`).

**🔒 `measured`, NOT `ok`** — the Lead yielded: **a reviewer seeing `state:'ok', value:0` reads “ok” as “fine” and is tempted to HIDE it, whereas `measured` makes a genuine zero obviously load-bearing.** That is a safety property, not a naming preference. **`state` means “do we have a real value from its stated source” — it does NOT claim the source was an instrument;** `source` remains the sole answer to where it came from. **`observedAtMs`, never `at`** — on a project about honest units, an ambiguously-united timestamp is a bug generator.

**🔴 `not-applicable` IS CONTAGIOUS THROUGH DERIVATION.** If ANY input to a derived field is `not-applicable`, the result is `not-applicable` — **NOT `unavailable`** — because the reason is architectural and stays architectural downstream. **`numericValueOf` must treat it as valueless and return `null`, never a number**: a bare `null` coerces to `0` under `+` and `<`, **which is exactly how a fabricated zero reaches the screen.**

**🔴 `renderField` MUST THROW ON AN UNKNOWN STATE. A FALL-THROUGH THAT DEFAULTS TO “RENDER IT AS MEASURED” IS THE MOST DANGEROUS LINE IN THE CODEBASE** (`telemetry-field.js:296-303`) — it converts every future enum addition into a silent fabrication.

**THE FIVE TREATMENTS — none render identically (that was the Lead's test, and it passed). Scalars and charts differ:**

| state | scalar | chart | voice |
|---|---|---|---|
| `measured` | `41`, full contrast — **a genuine `0` is a stark `0`** | normal line | none needed |
| `pending` | `···` — **never blank** | faint baseline + `AWAITING DATA` | “waiting” |
| `stale` | `41 · 12s old` — **age in words** | line **stops**, no forward fill | “this may no longer be true” |
| `unavailable` | `—` + dashed underline + `cursor:help` | 45° hatch + `NOT MEASURABLE YET`, **no line at all** | **apologetic**, hover names the file |
| `not-applicable` | `—` + **an ON-SCREEN CAPTION** | hatch + caption naming the execution path | **not apologetic** — architectural fact, ends with how to see the real number |

> **🔴 `not-applicable` IS THE ONLY STATE WHOSE EXPLANATION RENDERS ON SCREEN RATHER THAN BEHIND A HOVER — BECAUSE A FACT NOBODY HOVERS OVER IS A FACT NOBODY LEARNS, AND THIS IS THE ONE WE MOST WANT READ.** Mutual exclusivity is our best finding; it does not get hidden behind an interaction. *That* is what “first-class, not a footnote” means at field scale.

> **`pending` IS `···`, NEVER BLANK — two independent reasons.** (1) **A blank cell is indistinguishable from a layout gap, an unwritten panel, or a field we forgot**, so blank quietly borrows the semantics of *unavailable*. (2) **LAYOUT SHIFT: the cell is empty, a number appears, the row reflows — on EVERY first load, exactly when the visitor forms their impression.** `···` reserves the slot width so first paint doesn't jump.

> **⚠️ HATCH IN CHARTS, EM-DASH FOR SCALARS — and the reason is the sharpest statement of our thesis yet: A FLAT LINE AT ZERO IMPLIES DURATION** — *“we watched for 60 seconds and it stayed at zero.”* **That is a STRONGER and MORE SPECIFIC false claim than a scalar zero, because it fabricates a TIME SERIES THAT WAS NEVER OBSERVED.** And **gaps are NEVER bridged: interpolation fabricates the most convincing false data there is — the kind that looks continuous.** A hatched *scalar*, conversely, reads as a rendering bug. *(@0837fdf9.)*

> **🏅 CONTRAST IS AN HONESTY REQUIREMENT, NOT AN A11Y CHECKBOX.** @0837fdf9 shipped `--og-unavail-fg` at **3.23:1** and `--og-pending-fg` at **2.49:1** — below AA — then measured their own committed tokens and caught it. Their reasoning is binding: **the em-dash is the ONE GLYPH a visitor MUST notice — it is the entire mechanism by which we admit we don't know something. At 3.23:1 on a projector at the back of a room, some visitors simply don't see the hole and read the panel as COMPLETE.** An honesty bug in the costume of visual restraint. **AC25 (a11y) and AC6 (honesty) are the same requirement here: A TRUTH RENDERED ILLEGIBLY HAS NOT BEEN TOLD.** Fixed at 4.52 / 4.51:1, hue preserved.

> **`not-applicable` is the STATE that carries this project's central insight.** Under two-mode it is the *normal* case, not an exception: on the scatter server the KV and prefix panels are **correctly** empty. `reason` should read like *"this server runs continuous batching, which bypasses the paged KV allocator entirely."*
> **🔴 `not-applicable` IS UNIMPLEMENTABLE WITHOUT `(field, profile)` PROVENANCE KEYING — THEY ARE ONE CHANGE, NOT TWO.** `prefix_cache_hits` is `measured` on the dynamic server and `not-applicable` on the scatter server. **Same field, opposite provenance, decided by which model loaded.** A table keyed on field name alone **cannot express that and fails toward the wrong answer on precisely the server we demo.** @0837fdf9 asked me to carry this to the Lead — **it is already ruled and already broadcast as the top front-end priority. @bb2ee824: it is not pending anyone. Build it.**

> **On `pending` (ratified, quoted verbatim because it is the cleanest statement of the rule):** *pending resolves on its own; unavailable never will.* **TELLING A VISITOR TO WAIT FOR A NUMBER THAT IS NEVER COMING IS ITS OWN SMALL DISHONESTY.** So a measurable-but-unsampled field starts `pending`; a documented zero renders `unavailable` **from the very first frame, before any poll.**

> **🔴 BINDING — `stale` MUST render a visible AGE SUFFIX (`41 · 12s old`), not merely a colour shift.** Colour alone fails grayscale and colourblindness, and **a 12-second-old number presented as current is the same class of error as a fabricated one.** Ceiling rules in AC45.

**Derivation is contagious in both directions**: any `unavailable` input → `unavailable` result; any `pending` input → `pending` result.

### §3.2 — THE THREE KINDS OF ZERO (binding)

| Kind | What it is | Treatment | Citation |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Measured zero** | The server computed it and it is genuinely 0 | **The number, full contrast.** No apology | `prefix_cache_hits: 0` |
| **Placeholder** | A hardcoded stub the server never computes | **Em-dash** + hover naming the stub | `/v1/status.tokens_per_second` is literally `0.0` at `routes/admin.rs:57-69` |
| **Not-applicable** | Structurally meaningless on this path | **Explained** — the teaching surface | `prefix_cache_hit_len` hardcoded `0` at `batched.rs:262`/`:486`; `prefix_cache_lookups` increments with no predicate at `metrics.rs:130-132` — **it would read 5 with the prefix cache deleted** |

> **🔴 A FOURTH KIND, AND IT DEFEATS OUR STRONGEST INSTINCT: THE FABRICATED VALUE THAT *MOVES*.**
> ~~`GET /v1/models` returns **`created: now_unix()`** (`routes/admin.rs:29`, verified) — **the CURRENT TIME on every call.** It is not a creation date; it **ticks live**,~~ **🟢 STRUCK — FALSE AT HEAD, AND MEASURED FALSE, NOT JUST RE-READ. `e556b7f4` replaced it with `directory_mtime_secs(&status.path)` (`admin.rs:58`), returning `Option<u64>` — omitted when unknown rather than guessed. @e00032a4 called `/v1/models` twice three seconds apart and got an IDENTICAL `created`. THE MODEL CARD MAY SHOW `created`. See AC147/AC172.** ~~ on an **ungated endpoint that works with no flags at all**.
> **Every heuristic we have relies on motion as evidence of liveness.** A stuck number looks suspicious; a moving one looks alive. **This is a hardcoded fabrication that passes that test perfectly** — it would sail past a reviewer skimming for stubs, because the thing they're scanning for is *a value that never changes.* It even survives the AC44 audit's instincts: **it is not a literal `0` anyone can grep.**
> **BINDING: the model card MUST NOT display `created` until this is resolved.** Ruling pending with the Lead. **Rule generalised: a value changing is NOT evidence it is measured. Ask what would have to happen in the ENGINE for this number to move — and if the answer is "nothing," it is a clock, not a measurement.** *(Found by @d7cf9b84; verified independently.)*

> **Fabrication has TWO directions.** An unmeasured stark `0%` is a lie; **so is an em-dash over a real zero.** The second is the one we are more likely to commit, because it feels safe.
>
> **A real measurement of the WRONG QUANTITY is more dangerous than a stub.** A stub is greppable; a correct number under a wrong name looks perfect forever. Naming traps verified: `vram.used` is scheduler KV byte-budget accounting (`crates/onnx-genai-scheduler/src/governor.rs:548,554`), **not device VRAM**; `host_ram.used` is a whole-machine OS query; `active_sessions` counts persistent `X-Session-Id` sessions and therefore shows **0** during 4 concurrent requests.

> **🔴 THE AVAILABILITY SET IS DYNAMIC, NOT MERELY STALE-PRONE — SO THE SERVER MUST COMPUTE IT.** The same field is available or unavailable **depending on which model is loaded**, because batching and paged KV are mutually exclusive. **A hardcoded client-side list would be wrong HALF THE TIME, not just eventually.** Required: unmeasurable → **`null`, never `0`**, plus a top-level `unavailable: { "dotted.path": "human reason" }` computed **per-request from the live engine mode.** Same conclusion as AC46, reached from a second direction.

> **🟢 SOURCE PREFERENCE: `/metrics` IS HEALTHY AND TRUSTWORTHY — PREFER IT WHEREVER IT HAS THE NUMBER.** Confirmed against a running server: TTFT histogram and token counters are all live. By contrast `/v1/status` returns `tokens_per_second: 0.0` and `batch_utilization: 0.0` (stubs) and `/v1/debug/kv` returns the **literal string `"unavailable"`**. **Bind to the endpoint that already computes the value rather than waiting for the one that declares it.**

> **⚠️ The "distrust `/v1/status`" rule is TIME-BOUNDED.** The hardcoded `0.0` fields are being fixed at their source. After telemetry lands, `kv_usage` / `tokens_per_second` / `batch_utilization` become genuinely measured. **Bind according to `provenance-audit.md`, not according to a remembered warning** — that warning expires when the audit says a field is measured. See AC44 and AC46.

### §3.3 — Chart rules (@0837fdf9, ratified)

- **Never a flat line at zero for an unmeasured series.** Use a 45° hatch well captioned `NOT MEASURABLE YET`. Unlike a `0` in a table, **a flat line also implies DURATION** — it claims *"we watched this for sixty seconds and it was zero the whole time."* **A fabricated chart lies about time as well as value.**
- **Gaps are never bridged.** Interpolation fabricates the most convincing kind of false data — the kind that looks continuous.
- **Colour never carries meaning alone.** Every sequence is a triple: colour + fill pattern + shape marker. Palette is Okabe-Ito. **Gate: screenshot any panel in grayscale; if it loses meaning, it fails.**
- A/B comparison is **stacked with one locked X scale**, not side-by-side — that makes the comparison about **extent**, which is the real result.

---

## §4 — Interaction Model

- **One fetch loop.** Panels subscribe; **panels never fetch and never `setInterval`.** (Architect invariant I4.)
- ~~Poll cadence **4 Hz** for live series.~~ **🔴 AMENDED — 4 Hz IS UNSAFE AS AN UNCONDITIONAL CADENCE. `/v1/resources` MEASURED AT 2.49 s / 3.03 s ON THE FIRST CALL AFTER GENERATION BEGINS (idle control: 1.6-8.8 ms). A 250 ms timer against a 2.5 s endpoint stacks ~10 in-flight requests. SEE AC173: single-flight, and the page must render DATA AGE.**
- Panel contract: `export const meta = { id, title, group, span, cadence, defaultOpen, acronyms }` and `export default function mount(rootElement, telemetryStore) → { unmount(), describe() }`. Both returned methods required. `unmount()` must be **total** (AC22 is won or lost there); `describe()` returns a plain-English sentence powering the canvas `aria-label` (AC28) and the table view.
- **Panels degrade per-panel, not globally.** Each declares the engine mode it needs.
- **The two BLOCKING FAILURE STATES are built FIRST**, not as a finishing touch — they are what a first-time visitor most often sees, and building them early stops our own devs from debugging a blank dashboard.
- **Highest-value optional interaction:** hover a swimlane → that sequence's KV blocks light up, everything else dims. Bidirectional. It makes "sequence" and "memory" one concept instead of two panels.

### §4.1 — The swimlane (client-observed)
Because the demo drives 100% of its own load, the browser knows from its **own** request streams: sent, first SSE chunk (= TTFT), each subsequent chunk, stream end.

- **A browser CANNOT distinguish `queued` from `prefill`.** It sees only *sent → first token*. **That collapses to ONE pre-first-token segment**, labelled `queued (client-observed)` since it bundles network + admission time. `queue_wait_ms` may appear as an **optional server-badged sub-segment, NEVER INFERRED.**
- **Client bars have TWO segments, not four.** Drawing a queue/prefill split from client timing would be invisible fabrication.
- Every segment carries its **source class**; client-observed segments get the **client** badge.
- It reads **only** the client's own streams — never `sessions[].state`, which is a useless literal `"unknown"` (`admin.rs:77`).
- **`preempted` is Mode B only.** See §0.1.
- Static wave dead-time is hatched and explicitly labelled *head-of-line blocking · 2.4 s* — **naming it is what turns a chart into an argument.**

---

## §5 — The Quality Bar (50 acceptance criteria)

### Ratified rulings this list encodes
| Ruling | Source |
|---|---|
| Two servers (Option D); scenario switcher **is** the server switcher | Lead 🔒 |
| Field enum is **FIVE** states incl. `not-applicable`; `origin` retired for `source`/`endpoint`/`server` | Lead 🔒 **FINAL** — PM's four-state concession overtaken |
| Prefix-cache **panel** ships unconditionally; only the **scenario** is cuttable | Lead 🔒 |
| Swimlane is **client-observed** — cut, then **reversed**; it is IN | Lead ↩️ |
| `decode_backend` replaces execution provider | Lead 🔒 |
| Static-batching baseline is **never simulated** | PM, unchallenged |
| Panel is **"Paged KV block table"**, never "paged attention" | PM, unchallenged (AC9) |

### Demo shell & delivery
- [ ] **AC1** Static HTML + vanilla ES modules. **Zero build step, no `npm install`, no bundler.** At most one pinned CDN lib with SRI.
- [ ] **AC2** Server serves it same-origin at `GET /demo/` (**ungated — no flag needed for the PAGE; the PANELS need `--enable-debug-endpoints`, see AC62**). Documented in `README.md`. Zero CORS configuration required from the user. *(Superseded in detail by AC39.)*
- [ ] **AC3** No TypeScript toolchain, no transpile, no watch process.
- [ ] **AC4** Works in current Chrome, Firefox and Safari. No polyfills.
- [ ] **AC5** Total page weight under 500 KB excluding fonts.

### Honesty (the core of this document)
- [ ] **AC6** Every displayed number traces to a real server counter, a client-side measurement, or a clearly-labeled derivation. **Zero hardcoded values. Auditable by grep.**
- [ ] **AC7** Every value carries **provenance** — a source badge and a hover explaining where it came from.
- [ ] **AC8** Absence is rendered as absence. **An em-dash is never a zero and a zero is never an em-dash.**
- [ ] **AC9** The panel is titled **"Paged KV block table"** — never "paged attention". This is **permanent visible UI text, not a tooltip.**
  **🔴 AC9b — THE CANONICAL HONESTY STRING, CORRECTED. VERBATIM, WORD FOR WORD, EVERYWHERE IT APPEARS (block-table caption, §4.6 honesty footer, README).**
  > *“This page visualizes the paged KV **allocator** — real block allocation, copy-on-write sharing, tiering, and LRU eviction of cached prefixes. A separate VRAM-budget governor computes an eviction order that nothing consumes yet. Paged-attention kernels are not yet implemented in this runtime; attention still runs over materialized KV. We show what exists.”*
  **The previous wording claimed “… tiering, and EVICTION.” That was FALSE and I had not caught it.** Verified by me at source: `eviction_order` is produced at `crates/onnx-genai-scheduler/src/governor.rs:166`/`:508` and **referenced by NOTHING outside that file except its own tests** (`:778`, `:852`); `driver.rs:735-739` discards the outcome; `ByteBudget::reconfigure` never touches `used`, and **the repo's own test is named `reconfigure_lower_reports_overage_without_evicting`** (`byte_budget.rs:301`). The allocator **computes** an eviction order; **nothing acts on it.**
  **🔴 WHY THIS IS THE MOST EXPENSIVE ERROR AVAILABLE ON THE PAGE — and it is not because of its size.** That sentence is **the one place we ask a skeptical expert to trust us**; its entire job is *“we tell you what isn't built.”* **An overclaim INSIDE the honesty disclaimer doesn't cost us one feature — it costs us the disclaimer.** An expert who catches us overstating in the very sentence where we are being scrupulous **rightly discounts everything else on the page**, and they will catch it, because **it is a grep.**
  **🔴 CORRECTED AGAIN — SEE AC132. THE CLAUSE *“so no eviction occurs”* WAS FALSE AND SHIPPED IN THE DISCLAIMER FOR OVER AN HOUR WHILE THIS SAME DOCUMENT VERIFIED THE OPPOSITE AT AC—§*eviction is real*. The surviving governor finding is TRUE and NARROWER THAN “the allocator”.** The repaired clause is **specific, checkable, and names two subsystems instead of collapsing them.** Vague honesty reads as marketing; **precise honesty reads as competence.** *(Caught by @0837fdf9.)*
  **🔒 AND IT IS AC44-VERIFIABLE:** every clause maps to a file:line, so a reviewer can falsify it in one grep — which is exactly the property a credibility sentence must have.
- [ ] **AC10** No simulated, projected, or extrapolated series anywhere. **The static baseline is measured or it is absent.**
- [ ] **AC11** Derived values name their inputs (`derivedFrom`).
- [ ] **AC12** Units are always displayed and always correct.

### Visualization
- [ ] **AC13** Swimlane timeline, **client-observed**, per §4.1 — one pre-first-token segment, source-badged, `preempted` in Mode B only.
- [ ] **AC14** Batch-occupancy sparkline sampled at 4 Hz against a real denominator.
- [ ] **AC15 — ⚠️ PER-CELL OWNERSHIP IS NOT SATISFIABLE BY SERVER PLUMBING ALONE; DESIGN IT TO BE CUT CLEANLY.** `SequenceUsage` is built at `page_table.rs:867-875` as `{ sequence, pages: pages.len(), tokens, shared }` — **the `Vec<PageId>` is consumed into a length, so block identity is destroyed.** The raw map (`page_table.rs:619-620`) holds the IDs but sits behind `Engine.kv_cache`, a **private field** (`engine/mod.rs:166`) the server crate cannot reach. Fixing it is ~10 lines **in `crates/onnx-genai-engine`** — ruling requested from @12e42da8.
  **Degraded form, which must still be a good panel:** capacity, in-use, free, shared count, refcount histogram, and per-sequence block **counts** — but **no per-cell ownership and no stable block identity between frames.** Note what dies with it: the hover-linked swimlane↔KV highlight (§4) needs stable identity, so **it falls WITH per-cell ownership, not separately.**
  **🔴 CADENCE RULING (product call, so @0837fdf9 and @d7cf9b84 need not negotiate it): THE GRID DOES NOT POLL AT 4 Hz.** 4096 blocks × 4 Hz is ~1 MB/s of JSON and lands directly in AC33's overhead budget. **Gauges and sparklines need 4 Hz because the eye reads them as MOTION; the block grid is read as a STATE you inspect, not a flow you watch.** Grid at **1 Hz** (or on-change) is indistinguishable to a visitor and ~4x cheaper. **If cadence must be traded against fidelity, keep the blocks and slow the refresh.**
  **Wire shape (ratified, `telemetry-contract.md` §6):** three parallel arrays — `blocks[]` (owner-or-`null`), `block_fill[]`, `block_refs[]` — plus a **sparse `shared_blocks` map** for `refs > 1`. Flat arrays diff cheaply at 4 Hz; **the sparse map preserves copy-on-write multi-owner truth that flat arrays alone destroy.** `blocks[i] === null` means free/never-owned. A **mandatory `window: {start, count}`** is part of the contract.
  Baseline rendering: 64 → 4096 blocks with three density modes and a **mandatory utilization ribbon**. At 4096 the refcount numeral and dog-ear are **dropped rather than rendered illegibly** — a 3px dog-ear is a spurious significant figure in visual form.
- [ ] **AC16** Every cell state is a **shape** (free = empty+dotted, partial = fill height, shared = ring + dog-ear, demoted = strike). **Colour only ever answers *which sequence*, never *what state*.**
- [ ] **AC17 — THE PANEL SHIPS UNCONDITIONALLY; ITS *STATE* IS PER-MODE. This reconciles the Lead's ruling with @0837fdf9's retraction — they do not conflict.** The panel is **always present** (never hidden, never suppressed to make a number look better). What changes per mode is the **state it renders**:
  - **Mode B (dynamic/paged):** a **genuine measurement** — renders the number at full contrast, **including a real `0.0 %`** if that is the truth.
> **⚠️ `preempted` ON MODE A — STATE RECONCILIATION.** The ruling that `preempted` must render as `not-applicable` (never as a lane that silently never fires) is **substantively correct and binding**: **a lane that never shows a state is indistinguishable from a bug.** But it cites the retired five-state enum. **RESOLVED: it IS `state: 'not-applicable'`, exactly as ruled** — the five-state enum is final and `not-applicable` renders with an ON-SCREEN caption, not a hover. `batched.rs:757` hardcodes `PreemptionPolicy::Disabled`, structural per `:713-717`. **Mode A's swimlane is 4-state by design and forever.**

  - **Mode A (scatter/batching):** **`state: 'not-applicable'` 🔒 FINAL.** ✅ **The five-vs-four question is closed in favour of five, and I was overruled on the AXIS while being upheld on the SUBSTANCE:** it must never read as an apology. **A dedicated state carries that better than a sub-field**, because a panel author cannot miss it. Prefix caching lives in the paged KV manager, which the batching path bypasses **by construction**; hover cites `batched.rs:262`/`:486`, where the hit length is a **hardcoded literal `0`**.
  **Why the distinction is load-bearing here specifically:** this is the single panel where a visitor is most likely to conclude *"their prefix cache is broken"* — and it is the panel where the true answer is the most interesting thing we have to say.
- [ ] **AC18** A/B comparison is stacked on one locked X scale; static runs first.
  **🔴 AND THE LOCKED SHARED X SCALE IS AN INVITATION TO A COMPARISON THAT IS CONFOUNDED. THIS IS MY AC AND THE RISK IS MINE.** @fc8b5d97 measured the two arms four ways (`3405e477`, `perf-baseline.md` §11) and the result is decisive:
  ```
  SCATTER  qwen2.5-0.5b-scatter-v2  ->  batching ENGAGES,  peak 4 rows in flight
  DYNAMIC  qwen2.5-0.5b             ->  batching ABSENT,   peak 1 row
  ```
  **⚖️ FIRST, THE RULING, BECAUSE @fc8b5d97 CORRECTLY DECLINED TO MAKE IT AND IT IS A DEMO-COMPOSITION DECISION RATHER THAN A SERVER BUG: THE COMPOSITION IS *CORRECT AND DELIBERATE*, AND IT MUST NOT BE "FIXED".** The two arms are supposed to differ in which capability is live — that IS the architecture. A static/scatter cache lets rows share one buffer, so continuous batching runs and the page table is bypassed; a dynamic cache grows per sequence, so KV pages and continuous batching cannot. **The panes demonstrate DIFFERENT capabilities, and the shipped labels already say so** — `launch-command.js` carries `label: 'static-cache server'` / `demonstrates: 'continuous batching'` and `label: 'dynamic-model server'` / `demonstrates: 'paged KV block allocation'`, with a comment saying the field exists to state the purpose *"in the visitor's terms rather than the engine's."* **That is careful work and it is already right.**
  **⛔ WHAT IS *NOT* RIGHT IS THIS AC. A LOCKED SHARED X SCALE IS THE UNIVERSAL VISUAL GRAMMAR FOR *THESE TWO SERIES ARE COMMENSURABLE*, AND THESE TWO SERIES ARE NOT.** Any throughput, latency, occupancy or scheduling delta read across the panes is confounded by the presence or absence of the headline feature itself. **I specified an affordance that says "compare these" for two things that must not be compared — and I specified it before anyone had measured that the arms differ in batching at all.**
  **✅ BINDING, AND IT COSTS NO LAYOUT CHANGE: (a) the stacked panes keep the locked X scale — it is correct for reading *time* across them, which is its actual job; (b) **no caption, legend, header or footer may present a cross-pane DELTA of any quantity**; (c) the comparison the page invites is stated in words at the top of the pair — **"same request stream, two cache strategies, two different capabilities"** — not "A vs B"; (d) **if any number ever appears that is derived from both panes, it is a defect by construction, not a design choice to review.**
  **🔑 THE RULE, AND IT IS THE CAPTION RULE IN A NEW REGISTER — A LAYOUT IS A CLAIM: PUTTING TWO SERIES ON ONE AXIS ASSERTS THEY ARE COMMENSURABLE, AS LOUDLY AS A SENTENCE WOULD, AND NOTHING IN OUR HONESTY LAYER AUDITS LAYOUT.** Every guard we own reads strings. **A confound expressed in geometry is invisible to all of them** — and @fc8b5d97 found this one with a stopwatch and four arms, not with a grep. *(Their fourth arm is why it is believable: a fix that always published `1` would produce their third row identically. **A correction needs a case it could have failed, or it is a coincidence with good manners.**)*
- [ ] **AC19** Dead-time is hatched **and named** in words.
- [ ] **AC20** **No metric is displayed that cannot move — AND NO METRIC IS TRUSTED MERELY BECAUSE IT DOES.** A correctly-implemented counter that is structurally pinned is not shipped as a live number.
  **🔴 SECOND CLAUSE ADDED PER @0837fdf9's D126, AND IT IS THE MORE IMPORTANT HALF: AS ORIGINALLY WRITTEN, AC20 CERTIFIED ITS OWN MIRROR IMAGE.** `created: now_unix()` **changes on every single call and is pure fabrication — it PASSED AC20 PERFECTLY.** Every visual honesty mechanism on this page uses **absence of motion** as the cue for doubt, **so a fabrication that ticks is invisible to all of them and reads as MORE trustworthy than a real value that happens to be constant.**
  **BINDING (AC136): movement is NECESSARY AND NOT SUFFICIENT. No absolute server-supplied timestamp is ever rendered; times appear only as ages against `observedAtMs`, the one clock whose provenance we can state.**
  **THIS TEXT LIVES INSIDE AC20 ON PURPOSE — @0837fdf9's reason is exact: AC20 IS THE ONE PEOPLE GREP FOR, so the counter-example must be where the rule is, not in a distant AC that the grep will never reach.**

### Robustness
- [ ] **AC21** Zero failed requests in DevTools during a clean run.
- [ ] **AC22** `unmount()` is **total** — no leaked timers, listeners, observers or animation frames. Mount/unmount 100× leaks nothing.
- [ ] **AC23** No unhandled promise rejections.
- [ ] **AC24** The page survives the server being killed mid-run and recovers when it returns.
- [ ] **AC25** Two blocking failure states (server unreachable; wrong/missing model) are **explicit, actionable, and correct** — never generic.
- [ ] **AC26** No console errors or warnings in a clean run.

### Accessibility
- [ ] **AC27** **Grayscale gate:** screenshot any panel in grayscale; if it loses meaning, it fails.
- [ ] **AC28** Every canvas has a meaningful `aria-label` from `describe()`, plus a **view-as-table** alternative.
- [ ] **AC29** Composite widgets are **ONE tab stop with a roving cursor.** A 4096-cell grid as 4096 tab stops would be the worst a11y failure available to us.
- [ ] **AC30** Visible focus on every interactive element; respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Server plumbing
- [ ] **AC31 — ⚠️ REFRAMED: THIS IS A DEPENDENCY, NOT A PERFORMANCE TARGET.** `GET /v1/status` returns the full JSON contract in a single response in **<10 ms at 32 concurrent sequences** — and this is currently **STRUCTURALLY IMPOSSIBLE, not merely slow.** Verified at `server/src/driver.rs:592`: inside the continuous-batch loop, `command => deferred.push_back(command)` parks **every non-Generate command**, and the loop exits only at `manager.is_idle()`. A telemetry snapshot is a non-Generate command, **so a 250 ms poll blocks for the entire generation.**
  **It is worse than staleness, and I verified this second-order effect myself.** The deferred queue is not drained until `run_static_batch_until_idle` **returns** (`driver.rs:520`, `:533`) — i.e. **after the batch is idle.** So the parked snapshots are answered **against an empty engine** and report **occupancy 0, queue 0.** They do not return stale numbers; **they return the OPPOSITE of the event they were sampling.** A viewer watching our headline proof would see the dashboard **flatline to zero precisely while four sequences decode**, then recover — and would reasonably conclude the batching claim is false.
  **The dashboard goes blind during exactly the activity it exists to display, and in the most incriminating direction available.** AC31 therefore **passes or fails on the inline-snapshot fix** (~40 lines, 5 call sites, including the pipeline arm at `:480-484`, which must reply `Err` rather than drop the oneshot and hang; @e00032a4 verified the borrow is safe — `continuous_batch_manager(&self)` is immutable and `page_usage`/`page_stats` take `&self`). **Without it AC31 cannot be met by tuning, caching, or polling faster — only by changing where the snapshot is served.** If the fix is descoped, AC31 is not "missed" — **Scenario A's central claim becomes unevidenced**, and §9 applies. *(Found by @d7cf9b84, routed by @c0de4c2e; idle-read effect verified by me.)*
- [ ] **AC32** `PageUsage` / `PageStats` extended to carry per-block data for AC15. Both take immutable borrows today; `PageUsage.sequences[]` is counts-only and `references` is a histogram — **both need extending.**
- [ ] **AC33** **Telemetry overhead < 2%**, measured as a **matched A/B** on the same binary and machine: telemetry-collecting vs not, 4 Hz polling active, **≥3 runs per arm**, at a generation length where the instrument can actually resolve the effect. **"Inconclusive" is not a pass.** *(At 128 tokens CV was 4.95% and two runs of the IDENTICAL binary differed by 1.98% — the criterion was untestable; 512-token generations give CV 1.81%.)* **🟢 BASELINE CAPTURED (@fc8b5d97, clean tree, `qwen2.5-0.5b-scatter-v2`, CPU EP, `max_batch=4`):** single-request decode **median 33.415 tok/s** (n=15, CV 1.98%); 4-concurrent aggregate **82.130 tok/s** (**ratio 2.46x — RETAINED HERE AS RAW MEASUREMENT, WITHDRAWN AS A HEADLINE BY AC50; see there for the ±10.8% ambient swing and the second, unshipped 1.57x wall ratio derivable from this same paragraph**); 4-concurrent wall **52.506 tok/s**; TTFT **median 2141 ms**. **Method is BINDING (`perf-baseline.md` §5): 512-token generations · 15 samples per arm · compare in BLOCKS · **use the ARCHIVED HARNESS rather than writing your own.** Pass thresholds: ≥32.75 tok/s single, ≥51.46 tok/s concurrent-wall, reported with n / CV / 95% CI — NEVER a bare percentage.** 🔒 **SUPERSEDED SIGN-OFF PROCEDURE — THE PERISHABLE-BASELINE MODEL IS DEAD, AND WITH IT ALL SCHEDULE PRESSURE ON THIS PROJECT.** @fc8b5d97 re-ran the **BYTE-IDENTICAL preserved binary** 75 minutes later and got **30.151 vs 33.415 tok/s — a −9.8% swing from BACKGROUND LOAD ALONE, 5× our 2% threshold.** So *“record the conditions precisely enough to reproduce them later”* **IS NOT ACHIEVABLE ON THIS MACHINE**: the perturbing variable is other agents' workloads, which we can neither record nor reinstate. **A later after-run compared against today's numbers would be measuring the CREW'S ACTIVITY, not our telemetry — and we would have reported a 10% regression that does not exist, or masked a real one, with total confidence either way.**
  **BINDING PROTOCOL: both binaries run BACK-TO-BACK IN ONE SESSION, alternating BLOCKS OF 5, to ≥15 samples per arm, at 512-token generations. Machine state then CANCELS OUT instead of being recorded and hoped about.** Clean binary preserved and hash-verified at `clean-binary/onnx-genai-server-clean-d49d3c8`.
  **🔴 AND BOTH ARMS POLL AT 4 Hz — THIS CLAUSE PREVIOUSLY CONTRADICTED ITSELF AND I AM CORRECTING MY OWN TEXT.** It demanded the same workload in both halves while specifying 4 Hz polling only for the after-run. **Taken literally that compares a NO-POLLING before against a POLLING after and charges the polling load to telemetry.** The measured error is small (+1.45% median, SEM ±1.53%, indistinguishable from zero) — **but it biases in the PERMISSIVE direction, and for a criterion whose entire purpose is catching regressions, that is the wrong way to be wrong.** *(Caught by @12e42da8 against my wording.)*
  **AND THE REUSABLE NEGATIVE RESULT: interleaved PAIRED A/B DOUBLES the variance of the delta**, because the noise here is per-request jitter rather than drift — pair only when the drift you are cancelling exceeds the jitter you are doubling.
  *(Historical, now moot:)* **THREE ARMS, NOT TWO — and the third is only capturable BEFORE instrumentation lands.** A naive before/after would compute `delta = instrumentation cost + 4 Hz polling load`, and **polling alone could exceed 2%**, producing a **false FAIL of the telemetry criterion or masking a real regression.** Required arms: (1) clean tree, no polling; (2) **clean tree WITH a 4 Hz poller hitting `/metrics`, `/v1/status`, `/v1/debug/kv`, `/v1/resources`** — alternating ON/OFF blocks in one session so the polling delta is isolated from machine load; (3) post-telemetry, **under comparable load to arm 2.** *(Arm 2 captured by @fc8b5d97 in response to this criterion; it becomes unrecoverable the moment instrumentation lands.)*
  **⚠️ CONTENTION CAVEAT, self-reported:** HEAD moved `f55e459`→`9f3f5d9` mid-run and a Time Machine backup restarted. Verified zero Rust/Cargo files changed and the binary under test predates those commits, so the baseline is a valid clean-Rust reference — **but it was taken under real CPU contention and is likely a MILD UNDER-ESTIMATE.** Run the "after" under comparable conditions, ideally both quiet.
  Use `max_tokens=512`, blocks of 15; **do NOT use interleaved paired A/B** (tested on a null pair: noise is per-request jitter, not drift, so pairing DOUBLES the variance of the delta). Baseline must record the **git SHA and `git status --short`** so a reviewer can verify the trees differed by exactly the telemetry change.
- [ ] **AC34** Tests cover every newly-measured field, including the assertion that documented zeros are zero **for the documented reason**.
- [ ] **AC35** Debug/admin endpoints stay behind their existing gates; the demo degrades honestly when they are off.
- [ ] **AC36** **Model identity panel** sources `model_dir`, plus `loaded` and `is_default`, from **`GET /v1/models` (ungated, works with no flags)** — a real improvement, since the card degrades far less often. **🔴 It MUST NOT bind `created`** (see §3.2: it is `now_unix()`, a live-ticking fabrication). Shows `model_dir` and **`decode_backend`** (`PipelineEngine::decode_backend()`, `pipeline/mod.rs:423` — already exists, simply unrouted). **Execution provider degrades to `unavailable` with an honest reason** — EP is resolved inside `SessionOptions` and never surfaced to the server layer. `decode_backend` is strictly more valuable: **it tells the dashboard WHICH OF THE TWO MUTUALLY-EXCLUSIVE MODES IT IS WATCHING, verified at runtime rather than asserted in a README.**
- [ ] **AC37** Debug endpoints absent ⇒ the panel says so specifically (**404, not 403** — the route is never registered).
- [ ] **AC38** No telemetry work runs on the decode hot path (Architect invariant I3).

### Delivery & two-mode integrity
- [ ] **AC39 — Same-origin static serving at `GET /demo/`, ON *BOTH* ORIGINS.** ⚠️ **"The server" is not one server.** Under the ruled two-server topology the mode switch is a **page navigation**, so **every origin the demo can navigate to MUST serve `GET /demo/` itself** — scatter *and* dynamic. It is the same handler in the same binary, so this is a **launch/config requirement, not extra code**: both instances must be started with the demo assets resolvable. **If only one serves it, Mode B navigates to a 404 and the second half of the demo is unreachable** — while every test on the working origin passes. This is also what makes invariant **I6 ("same-origin only, no CORS ever")** achievable rather than aspirational.  No CORS layer is added.
  **🔒 REQUIREMENT (not a mechanism): ASSETS ARE READ FROM DISK AT REQUEST TIME AND NEVER BAKED INTO THE BINARY.** `ServeDir` satisfies it; so does **hand-rolled file serving, which is what we ship** — `tower-http`'s `fs` feature pulls `mime_guess`, `tokio-util` and `http-range-header`, **none of which are in `Cargo.lock` or the local cargo cache**, making it a network fetch for ~10 lines of convenience. `include_str!` is the one option that is **BANNED**, because The same-origin property is identical either way, so this is a **team-velocity ruling, not an architectural one**: `include_str!` bakes the assets into the binary, so **every HTML/CSS/JS tweak forces a full server rebuild — and each rebuild takes the EXCLUSIVE CARGO LOCK while three front-end devs are iterating.** It would convert a one-second edit-refresh loop into a serialised queue behind the slowest build in the project.
  **⚠️ THE NAVIGATION MUST CARRY THE SELECTED SCENARIO IN THE URL** (e.g. `/demo?scenario=paged-kv`), or the visitor lands on the destination origin's *default* view and has to re-select — **which reads as the click having failed.** *(Raised by @732c7548.)* **Bonus: a full page load makes AC42 true BY CONSTRUCTION** — no in-memory value can survive to bleed across profiles.

  🔴 **STALE-VERIFICATION DEFECT, FOUND BY @0837fdf9 AND FIXED HERE.** This AC carried *"Verified today: the current binary returns 404 on `/demo` … and 405 to an OPTIONS preflight"* — **true when written, FALSE AT HEAD since `demo_assets.rs` shipped, and phrased in the PRESENT TENSE as a live verification.** Struck.
  **THE GENERAL FAULT, and it is AC86 aimed at this document: A DATED OBSERVATION WRITTEN IN THE PRESENT TENSE HAS NO EXPIRY, AND A REWRITE RE-STAMPS IT WITH A FRESH TIMESTAMP IT DID NOT EARN.** AC39 is on the never-cut list, making it the **worst possible row to carry a dead fact.** **BINDING for this file: an observation is written as `OBSERVED <when>: …`, never as `the current binary does X`.**
  🔴 **AND THE URL IS `/demo/` WITH THE TRAILING SLASH, EVERYWHERE, BYTE-EXACT.** Without it, relative module imports resolve against `/`, **every `<script type="module">` 404s, and it presents as A BLANK PAGE WHOSE ONLY SYMPTOM IS A CONSOLE ERROR** — indistinguishable from a crash, and invisible to a status-code assertion (AC100).

- [ ] **AC40** `README.md` documents both servers, both ports, and the exact commands — **no hardcoded ports in code.** ⚠️ **`--model` takes a model DIRECTORY and NEVER a config file.** `onnx-genai generate ./m/genai_config.json` works only because `resolve_model_dir()` (`cli/src/lib.rs:674`) coerces a file to its parent — **that helper is CLI-ONLY and the server has no equivalent**, so `--model ./m/genai_config.json` fails. **No server command in any document, UI string, or copy button may show a `genai_config.json` path.** The launch command must be **byte-identical everywhere it appears** — README, `ui/launch-command.js`, and error states — because a visitor who copies one and reads another has no way to tell which is right. **Supported: @d7cf9b84's self-correcting server error** (*"`--model` expects a model DIRECTORY… Try: `--model <parent>`"*) — **an error that tells you the fix converts a dead end into a five-second recovery**, and this is the single most likely first-run mistake.
- [ ] **AC41** Every panel states **which mode/server it is showing.** Source attribution is never ambiguous.
- [ ] **AC42** **No cross-mode bleed.** ⚠️ **Under one server this stops being structural and becomes a discipline requirement enforced by AC47** — see §2. A value measured on one origin is never rendered while the other mode is displayed — including across a navigation.
- [ ] **AC43 — CANONICAL COPY, adopted from @0837fdf9 substantially verbatim because I cannot improve it:** *“continuous batching takes KV out of the pageable pool and puts it in fixed decode rows; paged KV keeps it in a managed page table for sharing and eviction; this runtime implements both, and today they are alternatives rather than a composition.”* **True, instructive, and nothing else in this space says it.** This is the strongest available instance of *the honesty is disarming*, and it is more memorable than any panel we could have shown instead. **We are not disclosing a limitation; we are teaching the most interesting thing we know.** A visible component explains **why the two modes are separate** — i.e. that continuous batching and paged KV are mutually exclusive execution paths. This is the demo's most interesting insight and it gets first-class UI, not a footnote.
- [ ] **AC44 — No row in `provenance-audit.md` may be false at HEAD.** The audit governs what every panel may bind to, so a stale audit is not documentation debt — it is an **active instruction to render a fabricated absence.** Requirements: (a) updated **in the same commit** that makes any row false, (b) carries a **commit SHA or timestamp**, (c) **lives in the repo** alongside the demo. **Test:** pick any three rows at review — one 🟢, one 🔴, one "measured but misnamed" — and verify each against source at HEAD. *(Raised by @c0de4c2e; the gap was in my own §3 wording.)*
  **🔴 (d) NAMED OWNER, AND A RULE FOR THE MID-STREAM CASE — requirements (a) and (c) CONFLICT during an active review and nobody had noticed.** Once the audit lives in the repo, reviewers diff it. But *“updated in the same commit that makes a row false”* and *“reviewed at HEAD”* **cannot both hold while telemetry is still landing**: the reviewer reads a HEAD that moves underneath them, and **the failure is silent — they verify a row that was true when they started and is false by the time they finish, then approve it.**
  **RULE: `provenance-audit.md` is owned by @d7cf9b84 (server telemetry) for as long as telemetry is in flight, and by the reviewer thereafter. A review MUST record the SHA it reviewed AT, and if HEAD has moved past that SHA when the review completes, ONLY THE ROWS TOUCHED BY THE INTERVENING COMMITS ARE RE-CHECKED — not the whole document.** That keeps the audit cheap enough to actually redo, which is the only property that makes a freshness guarantee survive contact with a deadline. *(Raised by @c7a654ed.)*
- [ ] **AC45 — A DISPLAYED VALUE MUST CARRY ITS AGE THE MOMENT IT STOPS BEING LIVE.** 🔴 **A frozen chart and a saturated one are pixel-identical.** The store already preserves the original `observedAtMs` across failed polls and the designer already specced the treatment (`41 · 12s old`, age **in words** — colour alone fails the grayscale gate), but **nothing REQUIRED a panel to surface it**: a designed capability with no criterion forcing its use. Binding: (a) any field in `stale` renders its **age in text**; (b) past a **staleness ceiling** it stops rendering as a number at all — an unbounded age suffix is still a number on screen, and past some point "last known" is indistinguishable from fiction; (c) **the ceiling is per-panel, not global** — a 4 Hz occupancy sparkline is worthless at 3 s old while `kv_pages_total` is fine at 30 s; (d) a **whole-origin stall marks EVERY panel on that origin**, not just the one that happened to poll. **This is AC6 arriving through the TRANSPORT layer instead of through a panel** — every panel can be individually honest and the page still lies, because honesty was enforced per-field while the failure is per-connection. **Especially load-bearing under two origins: one server can die while the other stays live, and the dead half keeps displaying its last good frame indefinitely.** *(Gap found by @c0de4c2e — correctly identified as a missing AC rather than a design detail.)*
- [ ] **AC50 — THE HERO IS THE MECHANISM, NOT THE RATIO. THE HEADLINE MUST BE A COUNT THE VISITOR CAN VERIFY IN THE ROOM.** The headline is **"four sequences decoded in a single forward pass; onnxruntime-genai decodes one."** It is a **count**, directly observed on screen: the batch-composition panel shows the rows, the visitor counts them. **No arithmetic, no baseline, no second machine, no model provenance, nothing the audience must take on trust.**
  **🔻 THE PRIOR HEADLINE — `2.46x batching speedup` — IS WITHDRAWN BY ME, ITS AUTHOR. IT WAS WRONG IN A WAY THAT MATTERS AND THE REASON IS RECORDED HERE RATHER THAN DELETED, BECAUSE A NUMBER THAT VANISHES WITHOUT A REASON GETS RE-DERIVED BY THE NEXT PERSON WHO FINDS THE BASELINE.**
  **⛔ REASON 1 — THE PRECISION WAS TWENTY TIMES BEYOND THE INSTRUMENT'S RESOLUTION, AND THIS DOCUMENT ALREADY CONTAINED THE PROOF, 970 LINES BELOW THE CLAIM (see AC33):**
  ```
  claimed        82.130 / 33.415  =  2.458      <- shipped as "2.46x, never round it"
  same numerator, re-run denominator (@fc8b5d97, byte-identical binary, 75 min later)
                 82.130 / 30.151  =  2.724
  denominator moved  -9.77%  from BACKGROUND LOAD ALONE  ->  ratio moved  10.83%
  three significant figures require < 0.5% per arm.  We demonstrated 9.77%.  20x short.
  ```
  **☠️ SO `NEVER ROUND IT` WAS EXACTLY BACKWARDS. ROUNDING WAS THE ONLY HONEST OPTION AVAILABLE, AND EVEN `~2.5×` OVERSTATES WHAT THIS INSTRUMENT CAN RESOLVE.** @0837fdf9's design rule — *never `~2.5×`, rounding looks like modesty* — was **correct about typography and inverted by the measurement**: here the rounding was not modesty, it was the truth, and the three-figure form was the overclaim. **Both of us were reasoning about how the number would READ. Neither of us asked what the instrument could RESOLVE.**
  **⛔ REASON 2, AND IT IS THE WORSE ONE — THERE WERE ALWAYS TWO RATIOS IN THE SAME PARAGRAPH, BOTH TRUE, DIFFERING BY 56%, AND WE HEADLINED THE LARGER WITHOUT EVER RECORDING THAT A CHOICE WAS MADE:**
  ```
  4-concurrent AGGREGATE  82.130 / 33.415  =  2.46x   <- the one we shipped
  4-concurrent WALL       52.506 / 33.415  =  1.57x   <- same paragraph, same run, unshipped
  ```
  **AGGREGATE SUMS WHAT EACH STREAM SEES. WALL IS WHAT THE CLOCK ON THE WALL SEES — AND THE CLOCK ON THE WALL IS WHAT A USER EXPERIENCES.** I am not ruling which is methodologically correct; that is @fc8b5d97's lane and it requires reading the harness. **I am ruling that the spec recorded the chosen number and the unchosen number IN THE SAME NOTATION, with nothing marking that a selection occurred — so no reviewer could see there was a decision to audit.** *(This is @086345a5's law arriving in a measurement: an exemption list records decisions and non-decisions in the same notation. So did this paragraph.)*
  **🔑 AND THE GENERAL RULE, WHICH IS THE PART I WANT TO SURVIVE THIS DOCUMENT: A RATIO IS A CONSEQUENCE OF THE CAPABILITY; THE COUNT IS THE CAPABILITY. A ratio needs two arms, and every arm is a place to choose. A count has no denominator to pick, no baseline to age, no harness to trust, and no second machine to disagree with it. **A number an observer can move by observing harder is not a measurement — and a number with two defensible values is not a headline.**
  **✅ WHAT SURVIVES THE WITHDRAWAL AND IS NOW MORE LOAD-BEARING, NOT LESS: every conditions-and-labelling requirement below. They were written to protect a ratio; they protect the count at least as well, and the count can actually satisfy them.**
  **🔴 AND IT NEVER SHIPS ALONE. PER-STREAM THROUGHPUT FALLS TO ~0.62x OF SOLO (~20.7 tok/s). BOTH NUMBERS, TOGETHER, EVERYWHERE — README, model card, honesty footer, and ANY panel showing the speedup.**
  **BATCHING MAKES NO SINGLE REQUEST FASTER. It trades per-stream latency for total throughput.** **This survives the ratio's withdrawal completely and is arguably the more important half now** — reporting only the win, in ANY notation (ratio or count), would be **technically accurate and substantively misleading**, and the omitted half is **precisely what an engineer in the audience most needs to understand.** **Four rows decoded in one pass is a true statement that still owes the audience the sentence "and each of those four is slower than it would have been alone."**
  **A TRADEOFF PRESENTED AS A PURE WIN IS A LIE TOLD WITH TRUE NUMBERS** — the same class as AC53's false continuity, and the closest we have come to shipping one in the headline itself.
  **And omitting it would not even have FLATTERED us: anyone who has run a server knows this tradeoff exists. Hiding it wouldn't look impressive — it would look naive.** Showing it is what proves we understand our own system. *(Caught by @732c7548.)*
  Also required: label these **one CPU machine's numbers, explicitly not a performance claim**, and record **why 512-token generations** were used (CV 4.95% at 128; two runs of an identical binary differed 1.98%).
  **🟢 THE RISK I NAMED HERE AND THEN IGNORED FOR SIX HOURS, NOW RESOLVED BY THE WITHDRAWAL RATHER THAN BY MITIGATION: THE VISITOR WILL NOT REPRODUCE A RATIO.** They run on different hardware, and — per AC33 arm 2 — **our own dashboard polling at 4 Hz measurably perturbs the very number it displays.** So the page would show a live figure that **disagrees with our headline**, and the natural conclusion is that **we cherry-picked**. That single inference costs more trust than any em-dash we have argued about all session.
  **☠️ THIS PARAGRAPH IS THE MOST SELF-IMPLICATING THING IN THIS DOCUMENT AND I AM LEAVING IT INTACT. I WROTE THE RISK, FILED IT RED, MARKED IT `NOBODY HAS NAMED`, AND THEN MADE THE NUMBER THE BINDING HEADLINE IN THE SAME ACCEPTANCE CRITERION.** Every sentence in it was true when written and stayed true all night. **It was not a measurement error and no instrument could have caught it — it was a bad CHOICE, made in full view of a correct analysis, and we have no guard anywhere in this repository that audits choices.** *(The count has none of this exposure: a visitor who counts four rows on their own hardware gets four.)*
  Required: (a) the hero number is **labelled with its hardware and settings** wherever it appears; (b) the **live** figure is presented as *your run*, distinct from *our reference run*; (c) **when they differ, the page says why** — different hardware, and the dashboard's own polling overhead — **rather than leaving the visitor to guess.** **We are the only ones who can see both numbers; explaining the gap is not a disclaimer, it is the demo proving it measures honestly even when the result is inconvenient.**

- [ ] **AC51 — AN EXPLANATION MUST BE RE-DERIVED, NEVER INHERITED. A REASON STRING IS A MEASUREMENT AND IS GOVERNED BY EVERY RULE THAT GOVERNS ONE.**
  Every human-readable `reason` shown to the visitor — tooltip, footer, empty-state, blocking screen — **MUST be computed from the CURRENT evaluation** of the current server response. It **MUST NOT** persist across a state transition, and it **MUST NOT** be cached alongside a field whose availability has since changed.
  **Why this is its own criterion:** @bb2ee824 found, in a real browser, that after a `no-model` → `unreachable` transition, hovering `queue.depth` explained *“the server has no model loaded”* — **a confident, specific, WRONG explanation, inherited from a state that had ended.** This is strictly worse than showing nothing: **an absent explanation makes a visitor ask; a wrong one makes them stop asking.** We spent this entire spec ensuring no number lies, and the mechanism built to explain that honesty was itself lying.
  It also composes with the server-computed availability rule (§3): `reason` originates in the server's `unavailable` map **per request**, and the client may not substitute a remembered one. **A stale-but-labelled reason is acceptable; an unlabelled inherited one is not.**
  **Test the TRANSITIONS, not the states:** `ok→unavailable`, `no-model→unreachable`, `unreachable→ok`, and origin-switch (Mode A→B). This bug did not exist in any single state.

- [ ] **AC52 — “I VERIFIED IT IN A REAL BROWSER” IS THE ACCEPTANCE STANDARD FOR EVERY VISITOR-FACING PANEL. READING THE CODE DOES NOT SATISFY ANY CRITERION IN §5.**
  A panel is **not done** when its code looks right. It is done when it has been **driven in a real browser against a server (or a fake serving the real response shapes), through its state transitions, with the console visible.**
  **The console is part of the product.** A visitor who opens DevTools — and the technical audience this demo targets **will** — sees our error handling as directly as they see the UI. Per AC21 the console must be clean; a 4 Hz retry against a dead endpoint producing a scrolling wall of red is a **product defect**, not a logging detail. Backoff and cached-failure replay are required behaviour.
  **Non-negotiable path, because a live demo WILL have the server die at the worst possible moment:** kill the server mid-scenario → blocking state with the server's verbatim message and a copy-pasteable command → restart → **full recovery with NO manual refresh.** Self-healing is the difference between a pause and a disaster, and it is the single highest-value robustness behaviour in the build.
  **First-paint counts.** A page that throws before render (e.g. a TDZ crash) fails **every** criterion simultaneously, and is invisible to code review.
- [ ] **AC49 — AN UNRECOGNISED FIELD STATE MUST HARD-FAIL, NEVER FALL THROUGH TO RENDERING THE VALUE.** `formatFieldText` currently checks `unavailable`, checks `pending`, then **falls through to `return format(field.value)`** — so **any unrecognised state string renders its value as if it were measured.** During an enum migration that is not hypothetical: a module built against the older shape passing `state: 'ok'` renders a **live-looking number**. **A default branch that renders as fine is how AC6 dies quietly.** Required: unknown state **throws** (or renders `—` plus `console.error`). **Fail loudly in our own faces rather than silently in the visitor's.**
  **Corollary — `stale` must not share a rendering path with measured:** returning the bare number puts staleness **only in the hover**, so a visitor sees a live-looking number and must hover to learn it is twelve seconds old. See AC45. *(Both defects found by @0837fdf9 reading @bb2ee824's shipped code — exactly the failure the Field object exists to prevent, sitting inside the Field object.)*
- [ ] **AC48 — PREFIX-CACHE PAYOFF: A WARM-PREFIX REQUEST'S TTFT IS ≤50% OF COLD — SPECIFIED AGAINST THE *DYNAMIC* PROFILE ONLY.** ⚠️ **A QA run of this on the scatter model is NOT a failure and must not be filed as one** — it is a category error, because the batching path bypasses the prefix cache by construction. The run must state the profile it was measured on. Measured **client-side via TTFT delta**, never read from `/v1/status` (see §2, Scenario B: the counters are process-global with no model dimension).
  *(Substance flagged by @0837fdf9 as "AC14" — see the reconstruction banner: that number now belongs to the batch-occupancy sparkline. **The criterion was real and is restored here; only its number changed.**)*
- [ ] **AC47 — NO TELEMETRY VALUE MAY BE DISPLAYED WITHOUT THE MODEL ID THAT PRODUCED IT.** Every telemetry request carries an explicit `?model=`, and **every telemetry payload echoes the RESOLVED model id**, which the panel renders. **This is a provenance requirement, not a UI nicety** — it is AC7 applied to the one dimension that has no other defence. Today every telemetry endpoint calls `registry.resolve("")`, which resolves to `default_id` (`registry.rs:329-341`) = `specs[0]` after an **alphabetical** sort (`models_config.rs:151`). ~~**`qwen2.5-0.5b` sorts ahead of `qwen2.5-0.5b-scatter-v2`, so the dynamic model silently wins the default and the KV/batch panels would describe the WRONG MODEL during the batching scenario.**~~ **🔒 CORRECTED IN PLACE (D131): THE ALPHABETICAL SORT LIVES IN `from_models_dir` ONLY, AND `run-demo.sh:233-247` LAUNCHES WITH `--model` + `--model-id qwen-scatter` / `qwen-dynamic` — ONE MODEL PER PROCESS, SO `specs[0]` IS THE ONLY MODEL AND `resolve("")` IS UNAMBIGUOUS. THE ALPHABETICAL TRAP CANNOT FIRE ON THE SHIPPING PATH; THE IDs `qwen2.5-0.5b*` ARE NOT THE SERVED IDs EITHER.
