THE ARGUMENT SURVIVES WITHOUT IT — SEE *"A CORRECT DEFAULT IS STILL AN UNLABELLED ONE."***  The numbers would be real, internally consistent, plausible, and **about something else** — the exact failure this document calls *a real measurement of the wrong quantity*, which is more dangerous than a stub because a stub is greppable and this is not. **The echoed id is what makes the mistake VISIBLE rather than merely absent.** Under two servers the origin implied the model; under one server nothing does. **AC47 is therefore a hard precondition of the one-server topology, not an enhancement of it.** *(Found and being fixed by @d7cf9b84; alphabetical-default hazard verified independently by me.)*
- [ ] **AC46 — AN UNCOMPUTED FIELD MUST BE `null` ON THE WIRE, NEVER `0`.** Every field in the DOCUMENTED_ZERO set becomes nullable (`kv_usage: Option<f32>` and peers); the server emits `null` for anything it does not genuinely compute. **This is the Lead's own principle applied to the transport: THE HONESTY BAR IS ENFORCED BY THE API SHAPE, NOT BY DEVELOPER DISCIPLINE.** Today `0.0` is indistinguishable from a real zero **on the wire**, so every client must carry a hardcoded distrust-list — **a mirror of server state that changes underneath it.** The failure is guaranteed and silent: **the moment the KV plumbing succeeds, every stub-list-based client starts em-dashing a field that is now REAL** — the work lands and the dashboard hides it. Nullability makes the §3.2 "bind from `provenance-audit.md`" rule **age correctly instead of expiring on success**, and it **deletes** the stub list rather than maintaining it. **A `null` cannot be accidentally summed, averaged or plotted; a `0.0` silently can.** *(Ruling from @d7cf9b84; recorded here because it is the structural version of the rule this whole document exists to enforce.)*

---

  🔴 **AMENDMENT (@12e42da8, from @c0de4c2e's seam analysis) — THE BROWSER MUST BE LOADING THE ASSEMBLED PAGE FROM `GET /demo/`.** Not a test harness, not a `file://` path, not a module opened by URL, not a component sandbox. **Both front-end devs verified their own halves rigorously, in real browsers, and the defect lived in the seam between them: 21 KB of `panels.css` linked from nothing — silent, no 404, nothing in DevTools, both suites green.** A stylesheet that is never referenced is never requested, so **there is no signal to find.**
  **THE ONE CONFIGURATION THAT WOULD HAVE CAUGHT IT IS THE ONE NOBODY COULD RUN YET** — which is the whole argument for why `/demo` serving is the gate and not a convenience. **BINDING: no §5 criterion is satisfied by a component-level browser check. The artifact under test is the page the visitor receives, assembled by the server, at the documented URL, on BOTH origins (AC103).**
  **AND THE ORDERING FACT THAT MAKES THIS URGENT: the first browser bug found was a TDZ crash on FIRST PAINT — the page did not render at all. Everything downstream of it was UNVERIFIABLE, and every reading-based review still passed.** Console cleanliness, first paint, and **kill → restart → recover with no refresh** are criteria, not polish.

- [ ] **AC53 — THE PAGE MUST NEVER LET A VISITOR CONCLUDE THAT ONE SERVER DOES BOTH. WE CAN MISLEAD USING ONLY TRUE STATEMENTS, AND THIS IS THE COSTLIEST WAY TO FAIL BECAUSE IT SURVIVES REVIEW.**
  @0837fdf9's §14 risk, and it is the sharpest unnamed threat on the board: a visitor sees batch occupancy **4/4**, clicks a tab, sees a **live KV block table** — and reasonably concludes **one server did both.** It cannot; the two modes are **mutually exclusive by construction.** **Nobody will have said anything false.** **Tabs imply continuity of subject, so the visitor supplies the continuity themselves.**
  **This is the same failure class as interpolating across a data gap: nothing is fabricated, everything is JOINED, and THE JOIN IS THE LIE.** Every panel passes AC6 individually while the page as a whole misleads — which is exactly why a per-field honesty rule cannot catch it and it needs its own criterion.
  **Required, all three (they are cheap and they compose):**
  1. **Persistent server identity in the HERO, not the header** — `● SERVER 1 · qwen2.5-0.5b-scatter-v2 · static KV` with capability ticks (`batching ✓  paged KV ✗  prefix ✗`). A later em-dash then reads as **confirmation, not surprise.**
  2. **Narrate the switch** — one dismissible line stating the next scenario is a **second server** and that the two **cannot coexist.** **An expert who spots a hidden seam concludes we were hiding it; one who is told concludes we understand our own system. Same fact, opposite inference, decided entirely by who says it first.**
  3. **The URL bar does the heavy lifting** — per the navigation ruling the origin visibly changes (AC39). That is necessary but **NOT sufficient**: it is passive, off-page, and most visitors will not look. Items 1 and 2 are what make it land.

- [ ] **AC54 — NO HAND-MAINTAINED HONESTY TABLE. EVERY "WHAT'S REAL" SURFACE IS GENERATED FROM THE PROVENANCE REGISTRY, PROFILE-KEYED.**
  The honesty footer, the model card, the panel legend and any prose listing what is real **MUST be rendered from the same `(field, profile)` registry the panels read.** No parallel list, no hardcoded copy.
  **The evidence is unusually strong because it is self-reported:** @0837fdf9's §4.6 footer listed *prefix hits/looks* under WHAT'S REAL — **true on the dynamic profile, false on the scatter one.** They noted it is the **second** drift the registry decision caught **that they themselves had introduced.**
  **A hand-maintained list of what is trustworthy is the single most dangerous document in the project: it is believed exactly in proportion to how authoritative it looks, and it goes stale silently, at the speed the code moves.** A generated one cannot disagree with the panels, because it *is* the panels' source.

- [ ] **AC55 — THE MODE SWITCH IS A PAGE LOAD. IN-FLIGHT WORK MUST STOP CLEANLY BEFORE NAVIGATING, AND THE RESET MUST BE DESIGNED FOR RATHER THAN DISCOVERED.**
  Navigation is the right mechanism (AC39) but it has consequences we accept **deliberately**, and every one of them is a visitor-facing behaviour someone has to build:
  1. **In-flight scenarios STOP CLEANLY before navigating** — abort open SSE streams, cancel timers, halt the 4 Hz poller. An abandoned stream at unload produces **console noise (AC52) and a server-side session that outlives the page that owned it.**
  2. **Client-observed timeline state RESETS at the boundary** — it is held in the page, and the page is being destroyed. This is **correct and unavoidable**; what is not acceptable is a visitor watching a running scenario vanish with no warning. **If a scenario is mid-flight, say so before navigating.**
  3. **The switch must READ as entering a different mode** — not as a tab change that happened to blank the screen. A full repaint with no framing looks like a crash, and per AC52 **a page that looks broken IS broken.**
  4. **Cross-mode side-by-side is OUT** — an explicit non-goal, not a missing feature. Do not reintroduce it via a proxy or a CORS layer.

- [ ] **AC56 — SAME-ORIGIN MUST BE STRUCTURAL, NOT MERELY CURRENT. NO CLIENT-SIDE ORIGIN-SWAPPING FETCH LAYER, EVER.**
  Every request the page makes goes to **its own origin**, by construction. A helper that takes a target origin — `fetch(originFor(mode) + path)` — is **forbidden even if it is currently only ever called with the page's own origin**, because it makes the cross-origin bug **one line away, permanently.**
  **Why this earns a criterion rather than a code-review note:** such a layer **passes every unit test and fails the first time it meets two real servers.** Unit tests, Node harnesses and `curl` do not implement the same-origin policy — **only a browser does.**
  **And the failure is worse than a broken request, which is the part that is easy to miss:** at the fetch layer **a CORS block is INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM AN UNREACHABLE SERVER.** Our missing-server state would tell a visitor *“not detected — run `<command>`”* **about a server that is already running**, and the copy-pasteable fix would not work. **Confidently worded, actively false, and unactionable — inside the error state we built specifically to be trustworthy.** Detection of *why* a fetch failed must never be inferred from the failure alone.

- [ ] **AC57 — SCENARIOS B AND C MUST BE DRIVEN BY SEQUENTIAL SHARED-PREFIX SESSIONS, NEVER BY RAISING CONCURRENCY. THE DYNAMIC SERVER SERIALISES GENERATIONS.**
  **Verified from source (@d7cf9b84, independently confirmed by me):** on the non-batching path `driver.rs:696` calls `run_fallback_generation(...)` **INLINE inside the command match**, so the driver thread is blocked for the whole generation. **Concurrent requests to the dynamic server DO NOT OVERLAP — THEY QUEUE.**
  **The trap this criterion exists to prevent:** the obvious way to demonstrate block sharing and memory pressure is to turn a concurrency dial up. On Mode B **that produces a queue, not a busier allocator** — so the panel never moves, and we would debug the *panel* for an hour before suspecting the *engine*. **A correct panel fed by a workload that cannot exercise it is indistinguishable from a broken panel.**
  **Correct driver:** **sequential requests sharing long prefixes across sessions.** Prefix reuse and copy-on-write sharing are **per-sequence-history phenomena, not per-concurrency ones.** This is also the **more honest and more relatable** workload — it is exactly what multi-turn chat looks like: many turns sharing one long system prompt.
  **Concurrency remains the correct driver for Scenario A (scatter/batching) only**, which is where the hero mechanism is demonstrated and where batching is genuinely exercised. *(This line previously cited the withdrawn `2.46x` as the thing concurrency produces; the mechanism it drives is unchanged — see AC50.)*

- [ ] **AC58 — A PANEL THAT CAN ONLY CHANGE BETWEEN REQUESTS MUST NOT BE PRESENTED AS LIVE. POLL RATE IS NOT REFRESH RATE, AND THE VISITOR READS IT AS ONE.**
  On Mode B's serial driver loop, **a telemetry read queued behind a generation is not serviced until that generation FINISHES** — the same second-order effect already documented on the batching path (`driver.rs:592` parks non-`Generate` commands until the batch goes idle). So the KV block table can update **only BETWEEN requests**, while **paged-KV allocation is inherently a DURING-generation phenomenon.**
  **A 4 Hz poll against a value that cannot change at 4 Hz LOOKS live and is not** — the visitor infers refresh rate from motion, and a smoothly-polling panel showing a value frozen for the duration of every generation is **a fabricated impression built entirely from true numbers** (same class as AC53).
  **✅ RESOLVED — THE PREFERRED OPTION IS APPROVED AND IN FLIGHT.** @d7cf9b84 owns an engine accessor returning `Vec<PageBlock { id, ref_count, filled_slots, tier, owner }>` plus `Option<Arc<KvTelemetry>>` atomics, so **the block table is LIVE DURING generation, not sampled between requests.** Three agents reached this design independently — by reading the drain logic, by reading the fallback loop, and by measuring a 15-second stall. **The `sampled between requests` fallback below is retained ONLY as the degradation path if the accessor slips.**
  **Two outcomes, preferred one now ruled:**
  - **PREFERRED — make it genuinely live:** ~20–30 lines in `crates/onnx-genai-engine`, an `Arc` of atomics written after each decode step — **wait-free, and CHEAPER in the decode loop than servicing a channel.** The KV block table then animates during generation, which is **the single most visually compelling thing in the demo.**
  - **ACCEPTABLE — label it:** the panel ships **badged `sampled between requests`, explicitly NOT live**, with the reason in hover. Honest, and a slideshow.
  **What is NOT acceptable is shipping it unlabelled at 4 Hz.** Note this is the *same architectural cause* on both execution paths — which is the argument for fixing it once rather than labelling it twice.

- [ ] **AC59 — `onnx_genai_batch_size_current` IS NOT THE BATCH SIZE. IT MUST NEVER BE LABELLED "BATCH SIZE" IN ANY UI COPY, TOOLTIP, README LINE OR AXIS TITLE.**
  **Verified by @bb2ee824 and independently by me:** the gauge is `fetch_add(1)` in `GenerationMetrics::start()` (`metrics.rs:111`) and decremented in `Drop` (`metrics.rs:145`). **It counts GENERATION REQUESTS IN FLIGHT AT THE HTTP LAYER — it has no connection to `ContinuousBatchManager`.** With `max_batch` hardcoded to 4, **firing 8 concurrent requests makes this gauge read 8 while the engine's real batch is 4.**
  **This is the most dangerous field we have found, because it is REAL, LIVE, MOVES BEAUTIFULLY, AND IS NAMED AFTER THE EXACT THING OUR DEMO EXISTS TO PROVE.** Every previous trap was a static zero — visibly suspicious. This one responds to load, so it would have sailed through review and shipped as our headline evidence. **The upstream metric name did the lying for us; we would only have had to repeat it.**
  **REQUIRED BINDING — and the gauge is genuinely VALUABLE once named honestly:**
  - **`batch.in_flight`** — `MEASURED`, straight from the gauge.
  - **`batch.queued`** — `DERIVED`, `max(0, in_flight - max_batch)`, naming its inputs.
  - **the engine's TRUE batch size** — **`unavailable`**, because nothing exposes it.
  **This is what finally makes the QUEUEING-vs-BATCHING distinction expressible**, which is the single most important idea in Scenario A: **a visitor must be able to see that eight simultaneous requests are NOT eight simultaneous decodes.** A gauge reading 8 labelled "batch size" would have taught the exact opposite of the truth.
  **GENERAL RULE, and this is the fourth instance today: A METRIC'S NAME IS NOT EVIDENCE OF WHAT IT MEASURES.** Trace it to the line that writes it — not to the struct that declares it, and never to the string that names it.

- [ ] **AC60 — ⚠️ CORRECTED. `/metrics` IS REAL AND DEFAULT-ON, BUT IT MUST NOT BE POLLED AT 4 Hz — IT BLOCKS FOR THE ENTIRE GENERATION.**
  **🔴 MEASURED BY @fc8b5d97 ON A CLEAN TREE — this REVERSES the ‘prefer `/metrics`’ instruction I wrote earlier in this same section:**

  | endpoint | idle | during a 384-token generation |
  |---|---|---|
  | `/metrics` | 0.8 ms | **14,784 ms** (5 polls completed) |
  | `/v1/resources` | 0.8 ms | **14,785 ms** (5 polls completed) |
  | `/v1/status` | 0.9 ms | **1.8 ms** (61 polls, clean 4 Hz) |
  | `/v1/debug/kv` | 0.8 ms | **1.9 ms** (61 polls, clean 4 Hz) |
  | `/health` | 1.6 ms | 1.8 ms |

  **Root cause:** `routes/admin.rs:396` awaits `engine.resource_snapshot()` → `driver.rs:383` sends a `DriverCommand` and awaits a oneshot; **the driver is inside the decode loop for the whole generation.** The Prometheus encode is atomic and fast — **the round-trip is the cost, not the encoding.**
  **🔒 POLL AT 4 Hz: `/v1/status`, `/v1/debug/kv`, `/health` ONLY.** `/metrics` and `/v1/resources` may be read **on demand or while idle**, and only become 4 Hz-safe if `resource_snapshot()` is changed to read a cached/atomic snapshot instead of round-tripping the driver.
  **WHY THIS ONE WOULD HAVE HUMILIATED US: the dashboard freezes for 15 seconds EXACTLY WHEN A GENERATION IS RUNNING — the only moment anyone is looking.** It idles perfectly, so it passes every calm test, and it fails only under the demo's own headline scenario. **A stall is also indistinguishable from a hang: the visitor sees a frozen page and concludes the SERVER died, when it is happily generating.**
  **🔴 SAME ROOT CAUSE, SAME BAN — DO NOT ADD `DriverCommand::KvSnapshot`.** @e00032a4 self-corrected on this and @fc8b5d97 measured it: routing `/v1/status` or `/v1/debug/kv` through a driver round-trip would **convert our two FASTEST endpoints into 15-second stalls.** Publish **write-side from the decode loop** into atomics / an `ArcSwap` that handlers read lock-free — the pattern `metrics.rs:74-101` already uses, and the reason `encode_prometheus()` is 0.8 ms. **This is the same architectural fact as AC58, now confirmed by measurement from a third direction.**
  ✅ **And the polling budget is SAFE:** 4 Hz polling costs **+1.45% median / +0.46% mean, SEM ±1.53% — indistinguishable from zero.** AC33 will not false-fail on polling load.

  **The original finding still stands and still removes em-dashes:**
  `default = ["metrics"]` (`onnx-genai-server/Cargo.toml`), so it needs **no flag** — which makes it strictly better than any gated source for a first-run visitor. Genuinely measured and confirmed against a live server: `time_to_first_token_seconds` and `e2e_request_latency_seconds` (**real histograms**), `tokens_generated_total`, `prompt_tokens_total`, `completion_tokens_total`, `requests_waiting`, `sessions_active`, `prefix_cache_{hits,lookups}_total`. **🔴 `/v1/resources` IS REAL ONLY ON THE DYNAMIC SERVER. ON SCATTER IT IS DEGENERATE — `total_pages: 359128175` with 16-byte pages. NO SCENARIO A PANEL MAY BIND TO IT.** That is **confident, precise garbage**: a number with nine significant figures that a visitor has every reason to believe, produced by dividing a budget by a page size that has no meaning on a path with no pages. **Precision is not accuracy, and a wrong number rendered to nine digits is more persuasive than a wrong number rendered to two.**
  **Consequence — two headline numbers move from `unavailable` to `MEASURED`, so DO NOT ship them as em-dashes:** **tokens/sec** = differentiate `tokens_generated_total` between two polls (`DERIVED`, naming its inputs and its interval); **TTFT** = histogram `sum/count` (`DERIVED`, and it is a **running mean over all requests**, which must be said — it is not the latest request's TTFT and will not match a stopwatch).
  **`prefix_cache_lookups_total > 0` WITH `hits_total = 0` ON THE SCATTER SERVER IS CORRECT AND MUST NOT BE DEBUGGED.** Lookups happen; hits are **structurally impossible** on the static-cache path. Per §5.6 of `ARCHITECTURE.md`, the paged KV cache owns **both** the page table **and** the prefix trie, so bypassing the cache bypasses both — **one cause, two symptoms.** Render it `not-applicable`, never `0`.

- [ ] **AC61 — 🔴 SCENARIO C FILLS THE POOL WITH PROMPT LENGTH AND ACCUMULATED SESSIONS, **NOT** WITH CONCURRENCY. RAISING CONCURRENCY ON THE DYNAMIC SERVER DOES NOTHING.**
  **This corrects the one mechanism named in the Scenario C ruling, and it is a direct collision with a verified fact (AC57).** The ruling says *“raise CONCURRENCY and PROMPT LENGTH until the pool genuinely fills.”* **Prompt length works. Concurrency does not — on the ONLY server that has a paged KV pool.**
  Scenario C requires **paged KV**, which exists only on the **dynamic** server. The dynamic server runs the **non-batching** path, where `driver.rs:696` executes `run_fallback_generation(...)` **inline** — the driver thread blocks for the whole generation. **Concurrent requests queue; they do not co-reside.** So concurrency **cannot** raise the number of simultaneously-resident sequences, which is the only thing that fills a KV pool.
  **The cruel part: concurrency is the mechanism that DOES work — on the scatter server, which has NO paged KV pool to fill.** The two halves of the ruling are each correct about a different server. **Nothing would error; the pool simply never fills, and we would tune a scenario for an hour against an engine that cannot respond to the dial we are turning.**
  **CORRECT DRIVERS for Scenario C, all sequential:** **long prompts** (pages scale with sequence length — the strongest single lever), **many accumulated sessions** whose KV stays resident between turns, and **long generations** (pages are appended as tokens are produced). **`allocation_failures` (`page_table.rs:564-576`) is the honest “pool under real pressure” signal**, with `filled_slots`/`slot_capacity` showing **fragmentation — the actual cost of paging.** Combined with the **small KV budget** (capacity in the low hundreds), real pressure is reachable in under a minute.
  **Everything else in the ruling stands and is right:** the vram knob is dead (`reconfigure_lower_reports_overage_without_evicting` says so in the repo's own test name), pressure is **real**, no `simulated` badge, no privileged endpoint, and **`--enable-admin-endpoints` stays OUT of the launch command — a permission granted for a feature must not outlive the feature.**

- [ ] **AC62 — THE LAUNCH COMMAND MUST CARRY `--enable-debug-endpoints`. "ZERO FLAGS" IS FALSE AND MUST NOT BE WRITTEN ANYWHERE.**
  Verified on disk by @732c7548: the store polls **five** endpoints, and **`/v1/debug/config` and `/v1/debug/kv` are GATED.** `GET /demo` itself is ungated (@d7cf9b84, with a test), and so is `/v1/models` — **but the dashboard is not**, and the phrase *“works with no flags”* is true only of the page, never of the panels.
  **🔴 THE FAILURE IS UNIQUELY CRUEL AND THIS IS WHY IT EARNS AN AC: WITH THE GATE OFF THE SERVER RETURNS `404`, NOT `403` (`routes/admin.rs:93-100`).** A `403` says *“you need permission”* and is self-diagnosing. **A `404` says “this endpoint does not exist,” so a visitor who omits the flag concludes THE URL IS WRONG, OR THE BUILD IS OLD, OR THE DEMO IS BROKEN — anything except “add a flag.”** They will re-read the README looking for the right path, and the README will look correct, because it is.
  **Required:** the flag is in **every** copy-pasteable launch command, **identical in all four places** it appears, mechanically enforced by `check-launch-command.test.js`.
  **🔴 ALSO REQUIRED: `--demo-assets-dir`, EXPLICITLY.** `demo_assets.rs:30` resolves `./examples/serving-dashboard` **relative to the CURRENT WORKING DIRECTORY** — so the launch command works for us, standing in the repo root, and **fails for every visitor who runs it from anywhere else.** A path-dependent command in a copy-pasteable README is a defect that is **invisible to its own authors by construction.** *(Caught by @732c7548 against their own script.)* **And `--enable-admin-endpoints` stays OUT** (AC61) — the two flags are not a pair, and granting the second **has no demonstrated consumer** on an unauthenticated server.
  **If a gated endpoint 404s, the panel MUST say “this needs `--enable-debug-endpoints`” and show the full command** — never a bare em-dash, and never *“server not detected.”* Per AC56, **a failure whose cause cannot be inferred from the failure itself must be explained, not guessed at.**

- [ ] **AC63 — THREE FIELDS ARE REAL, LIVE, AND MEAN SOMETHING OTHER THAN THEIR NAMES. NONE MAY BE BOUND UNDER ITS OBVIOUS LABEL.**
  Companion to AC59. **A REAL MEASUREMENT OF THE WRONG QUANTITY IS MORE DANGEROUS THAN A STUB: a stub is discoverable — someone greps and finds the literal — but a correctly-computed number under a wrong name LOOKS PERFECT FOREVER.**
  | Field | What the name implies | **What it actually measures** |
  |---|---|---|
  | `vram.used` | GPU memory in use | **KV BYTE-BUDGET ACCOUNTING.** Not GPU memory. Label it *KV budget used*. |
  | `host_ram.used` | this server's memory | **WHOLE-MACHINE RAM**, every other process included — browsers, editors, our second server. Never attribute it to the demo. |
  | `active_sessions` | concurrent requests | **PERSISTENT `X-Session-Id` SESSIONS.** 🔴 **A demo firing 4 concurrent requests with no session header DISPLAYS 0.** |
  **`active_sessions` is the one that would have burned us in Scenario A specifically:** the visitor fires 4 concurrent requests, watches tokens interleave on screen, and a panel labelled *active sessions* reads **0**. **The page would contradict its own visible evidence** — and the number is perfectly correct. Bind `batch.in_flight` (AC59) for concurrency; bind `active_sessions` only where sessions are genuinely used, which is **Scenario B** (AC57's sequential shared-prefix workload), where it is both correct and interesting.
  **REVIEW CRITERION, binding on AC44: for every displayed field, verify the NAME against the INCREMENTING CODE. “It's a real number” is NOT sufficient.**

## §6 — Non-Goals

| Not doing | Why |
|---|---|
| **Fake numbers** — any hardcoded, formula-derived, or cherry-picked metric presented as measured | **Instant PR rejection, no discussion.** |
| **The demo is a pure client** — no model-loading logic, no loader refactor, no model-dir flag | The server serves it statically at `GET /demo`; no CORS. (§4, AC36–AC40) |
| A simulated static-batching baseline | §1 |
| A build step, bundler, or TypeScript toolchain | AC1, AC3 |
| Server-sourced per-request state (`sessions[].state`) | It is a literal `"unknown"` (`admin.rs:77`); the swimlane is client-observed |
| Any use of `POST /v1/admin/resources/vram-limit` | Dead endpoint (§0.1) |

**Failure modes we are explicitly designing against:**
1. A number on screen that nobody can trace.
2. An em-dash over a real measurement.
3. A stale value rendered as live.
4. A structurally-empty panel that reads as broken.
5. **CORS failure** — opening `index.html` via `file://` and getting nothing. If we ship a demo that only works if you already know to serve it, **we have shipped a bug-report generator.**


- [ ] **AC64 — THE MODE INDICATOR IS `continuous_batch_supported`, NEVER `decode_backend`. AND THE SERVER, NOT THE CLIENT, DECIDES WHICH FIELDS ARE `not-applicable`.**
  🔴 **`decode_backend` WOULD HAVE BEEN A CONFIDENTLY-WRONG FIELD ON THE MODEL CARD.** I verified the enum at `config.rs:516-525`: `Auto | Ort | Native`. That is the **native-vs-ONNX-Runtime decoder axis** — **completely orthogonal** to static-cache-vs-dynamic-cache, which is what actually separates Mode A from Mode B. A model can be `Ort` on either side of the mutual exclusion.
  **THIS WOULD HAVE BEEN WORSE THAN THE DOCUMENTED ZEROS WE ARE FIXING, AND IT IS THE SHARPEST EXAMPLE OF THE SESSION'S CENTRAL LESSON.** A hardcoded `0.0` is inert and greppable. **A REAL, CORRECTLY-COMPUTED VALUE UNDER A LABEL DESCRIBING A DIFFERENT AXIS IS A MEASUREMENT THAT ACTIVELY MISLEADS — and it PASSES EVERY PROVENANCE CHECK WE HAVE, because the field genuinely IS measured.** It would have been believed by a visitor *and by us, while debugging*, precisely because it was labelled "verified at runtime". **Provenance is not "is this field computed?" — it is "does this field MEAN what its name says?"**
  ✅ **THE REAL DISCRIMINATOR ALREADY EXISTS AND IS FREE. I verified it at source, `driver.rs:415`:** `let continuous_batch_supported = engine.continuous_batch_manager(max_batch).is_ok();` — and `:416-421` is the literal `if/else` that forks the whole system. `true` → Mode A (batching; no paged KV, no prefix cache, no preemption). `false` → Mode B. Already computed at startup, already logged. **Ship it as `execution_mode: "continuous-batch" | "per-request"`.** Ship `decode_backend` too if wanted, but labelled exactly what it is — *"decoder runtime: ort"* — **never as the mode.**
  🔑 **AND THIS FULFILS AC54 AT THE RIGHT LAYER: WHICH FIELDS ARE `not-applicable` IS A PURE FUNCTION OF `continuous_batch_supported`, AND ONLY THE SERVER KNOWS IT AUTHORITATIVELY.** So the server computes the state per-request and **the client never holds a mode→field table at all** — it renders whatever `state` arrives. This makes the `0% hit rate` bug **UNREPRESENTABLE rather than merely discouraged**, which is the entire thesis of the Field envelope applied one layer down. *(Architect @e00032a4; premise re-verified independently by me at all three file:line citations.)*

- [ ] **AC65 — `preempted_total` IS DROPPED. SCENARIO C's PAYOFF IS ADMISSION BACKPRESSURE, WHICH IS REAL, LIVE AND ALREADY MEASURED.**
  🔴 **FOUR INDEPENDENT STRUCTURAL BLOCKERS, ANY ONE SUFFICIENT — and I formally withdraw my earlier "build it".** Mode A: (1) `batched.rs:759` hardcodes `PreemptionPolicy::Disabled`, structural per `:713-717`; (2) **stronger — I verified `ContinuousBatchManager` (`batched.rs:101-110`) has NO Scheduler field at all.** It is `{ decode, tokenizer, metadata_max_context, static_max_len, queue, rows, events, next_handle }`. **Preemption isn't disabled — THE COMPONENT THAT COULD PREEMPT IS NOT PRESENT. Deleting line 759 changes nothing.** Mode B: (3) the server's path is `enqueue_generate_request`/`drive_next_fcfs_result` (`runtime.rs:394-430`), while the preemption machinery lives on a **different entry point** the server never calls (`:544`/`:607`); (4) the fallback driver runs generation **inline** (`driver.rs:696`), so **there is never a second sequence to preempt.**
  **A COUNTER STRUCTURALLY PINNED AT ZERO IS THE FABRICATED-ZERO CLASS THIS ENTIRE PROJECT EXISTS TO ELIMINATE — and it is the INVISIBLE kind, because "0 preemptions" reads as A HEALTHY SYSTEM rather than as a field that cannot move.** No `preempted` lane; no `sessions.paused` binding; **the swimlane is 4-state, fully client-observed, and that is the honest maximum.**
  ✅ **THE REPLACEMENT IS BETTER, NOT A CONSOLATION:** under sessions + repeated prefixes + long prompts (AC57/AC61), `queue.depth` climbs, `admission.slots_available` falls to zero, and `admission.rejections` increments — **all genuinely server-measured, no privileged endpoint, no `simulated` badge, no engine change.** A truthful Scenario C rather than a staged one.
  **🔴 THIS IS THE MUTUAL-EXCLUSIVITY PATTERN FOR THE FOURTH TIME, WHICH MAKES IT PREDICTIVE RATHER THAN COINCIDENTAL: a capability is well-implemented in the engine, and the server's chosen execution path routes around it.** Standing rule, generalised one final notch: **"the engine has X" is ZERO evidence that "the server can observe X" — trace to a handler that COMPUTES it. Never a struct that DECLARES it, and never a function that IMPLEMENTS it.** *(@d7cf9b84; blockers 2 and 4 re-verified by me at source.)*


- [ ] **AC66 — `prefix_cache_hit_rate` IS A REAL, LIVE, MOVING NUMBER THAT IS NOT A HIT RATE. IT MUST NEVER SHIP UNDER THAT NAME.**
  🔴 **THE HARDEST TRAP FOUND THIS SESSION, BECAUSE EVERY SAFEGUARD WE BUILT CERTIFIES IT AS HEALTHY.** Verified at `metrics.rs:132-136`:
  ```rust
  REGISTRY.prefix_cache_lookups.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);              // UNCONDITIONAL — once per GENERATION
  if prefix_cache_hit_len > 0 { REGISTRY.prefix_cache_hits.fetch_add(1, ..); } // BINARY — once per GENERATION
  ```
  **BOTH counters are per-GENERATION, and the numerator is BINARY:** a generation hitting on twelve blocks counts identically to one hitting on a single block. So the quantity at `admin.rs:149-152` is **"the fraction of generations that got ANY prefix hit"** — ~~genuinely useful, genuinely honest,~~ **🔒 STRUCK IN PLACE — THAT RELABEL IS NOW EXPLICITLY FORBIDDEN. SEE *"NO PREFIX REUSE NUMBER SHIPS IN ANY FORM, ON ANY ORIGIN, UNDER ANY LABEL"* BELOW.** I called this relabel honest because it accurately describes **what the code counts**; @12e42da8 forbade it because **the underlying reuse does not happen**, so a faithful description of a meaningless computation is still a false claim about the system. **A metric can be perfectly-named and still assert something untrue.** *(Retained, not deleted, so the reversal stays visible — a doc that silently loses the overruled version is indistinguishable from one that never held it.)* — **and not a cache hit rate.** The two diverge the moment any generation performs more or fewer than one lookup.
  ~~**THE MISNOMER IS BAKED INTO THE METRIC'S OWN DOCUMENTATION:**~~ **🔒 CORRECTED IN PLACE — THIS IS NOW FALSE, AND MY *EVIDENCE FOR THE CORRECTION* WAS ALSO WRONG, WHICH IS THE MORE INSTRUCTIVE HALF.** I originally cited `grep -rn "prefix_cache_hit_rate Prefix" crates/` **RETURNING ZERO** and concluded *the misleading HELP string was removed.* **THE CONCLUSION IS TRUE. THE WARRANT WAS NOT, AND IT COULD NOT HAVE BEEN.**
  **⛔ THE HELP STRING WAS NEVER REMOVED — IT IS AT `metrics.rs:357` RIGHT NOW, AND MY GREP CANNOT SEE IT BECAUSE IT IS SPLIT ACROSS A RUST LINE CONTINUATION AT EXACTLY THE POINT MY PATTERN SPANS:**
  ```rust
  "# HELP onnx_genai_prefix_cache_hit_rate \        <- :357  my pattern needs the next
   Fraction of completed generations that reused a cached prefix.\n",   <- :358  word, which is HERE
  ```
  **A LINE-ORIENTED GREP OVER A MULTI-LINE STRING LITERAL RETURNS ZERO FOR *REMOVED* AND ZERO FOR *REFLOWED*, AND THE SOURCE IS REFLOWED.** *(This is @086345a5's concatenation-boundary false zero — which they found in our JS prose — arriving in Rust source, and it cost me a correct finding rather than a wrong one.)*
  **✅ THE CORRECT EVIDENCE IS POSITIVE, NOT NEGATIVE, AND IT VINDICATES THE RETRACTION ON BETTER GROUNDS: read the joined string and the HELP text now says *"Fraction of completed generations that reused a cached prefix"* — which is an ACCURATE description of a binary-per-generation counter, not a hit ratio. Somebody did not delete the documentation; they FIXED it, and they fixed it to say the true thing.** **I proved a fix by the absence of the defect; the honest proof is the presence of the correction — and only the second one can tell you the author understood the problem.**
  The argument never needed this step: the counter is binary-per-generation at `metrics.rs:129-142`, which is the part that matters. **HISTORICAL FORM: it once read** `# HELP onnx_genai_prefix_cache_hit_rate Prefix-cache hit ratio.` — **so a reader who did the diligent thing and checked the HELP string was CONFIRMED IN THE WRONG BELIEF.** That is no longer true.
  🔴 **AND THE SECOND DEFECT, WHICH SEALS IT:** `admin.rs:145` resolves a model handle via `resolve("")` and then computes the rate from `crate::metrics::snapshot()` — **the PROCESS-WIDE registry. The resolved handle contributes NOTHING to the numbers.** `/v1/debug/kv` therefore **names one model and reports another's data blended in.** ~~Default model is `specs[0]` after an **alphabetical** sort (`models_config.rs:151`), so `qwen2.5-0.5b` silently outranks `qwen2.5-0.5b-scatter-v2`.~~ **🔒 CORRECTED IN PLACE (D131): THE ALPHABETICAL SORT LIVES IN `from_models_dir` ONLY, AND `run-demo.sh:233-247` LAUNCHES WITH `--model` + `--model-id qwen-scatter` / `qwen-dynamic` — ONE MODEL PER PROCESS, SO `specs[0]` IS THE ONLY MODEL AND `resolve("")` IS UNAMBIGUOUS. THE ALPHABETICAL TRAP CANNOT FIRE ON THE SHIPPING PATH; THE IDs `qwen2.5-0.5b*` ARE NOT THE SERVED IDs EITHER. THE ARGUMENT SURVIVES WITHOUT IT — SEE *"A CORRECT DEFAULT IS STILL AN UNLABELLED ONE."*** 
  **WHY NOTHING WE BUILT CATCHES IT: live numerator, live denominator, a plausible bounded value, AND IT MOVES WHEN YOU EXERCISE THE CACHE.** No greppable literal, no stuck value, correct `source: 'server'`, correct `state: 'measured'`. **This is AC64's `decode_backend` failure occurring in a field we had already told everyone to trust — which is why the rule must be stated as a QUESTION ABOUT MEANING, not about computation: provenance is not "is this field computed?" but "does this field MEAN what its name says?"**
  **BINDING:** ship it as `prefix_hit_generations / completed_generations`, labelled **"generations with a prefix hit"**, with the binary-numerator caveat on the panel. **Never "hit rate"; never rendered as a cache-efficiency percentage.**
  ✅ **AND IT REINFORCES THE TWO-SERVER RULING FROM A NEW DIRECTION:** `static` means per-process, so **two processes = two registries, and cross-model blending does not get MITIGATED — IT CEASES TO EXIST.** Every global counter becomes per-model for free, with zero hot-path risk. *(Found by @c0de4c2e as QA-PLAN §5.4; both defects re-verified at source by me, and the `resolve("")` misattribution is an extension.)*


- [ ] **AC67 — AN HONEST RENDERER CANNOT RESCUE A DISHONEST FIELD. PROVENANCE MUST BE CORRECT AT THE SOURCE.**
  🔴 **THIS IS THE MOST IMPORTANT THING WE LEARNED, AND IT DEFEATED EVERY RULE IN THIS DOCUMENT.** A throughput panel bound to `throughput.tokens_per_second` — **a DOCUMENTED_ZERO** — rendered a confident `0.0` while carrying `state: 'measured'`.
  **READING STATE BEFORE VALUE DOES NOT SAVE YOU, BECAUSE THE FABRICATION HAPPENED ON THE SERVER, NOT IN THE RENDER.** Our entire apparatus — the Field envelope, state-before-value, `numericValueOf` returning `null`, the five treatments — **defends the boundary between the STORE and the SCREEN. This lie was already INSIDE the envelope before the envelope was sealed.** Every client-side safeguard we built was, by construction, incapable of catching it.
  **BINDING:** `state`, `source`, `server` and a **machine-readable `reason_code`** are emitted **BY THE SERVER**. The client-side provenance table is a **mirror that can drift**, never the authority. **Server owns truth; client owns voice.**
  ✅ **AND THE REPAIR PATTERN IS ITSELF THE RULE — WHEN YOU REMOVE A TRAP, LEAVE A TRIPWIRE.** The fix was not merely "rebind the panel": the value is now **derived client-side from the token counter that IS genuinely measured, badged `derived`, with a TEST ASSERTING THE MODULE NEVER NAMES THAT FIELD AGAIN.** A removed trap with no tripwire is a trap that returns the first time someone refactors in good faith. *(Found and fixed by @c8d9a40e; rule stated by @12e42da8.)*

- [ ] **AC68 — A RATIO INVENTS A NUMERATOR. NAME BOTH TERMS AND CONFIRM BOTH ARE MEASURED BEFORE DRAWING ANY PERCENTAGE.**
  🔴 **`/v1/resources` PUBLISHES CEILINGS ONLY — THERE IS NO CONSUMPTION TERM ANYWHERE IN THE PAYLOAD.** Verified at `routes/mod.rs:187-208`: `configured_limits` (strings), `resolved_limits` (`vram_bytes`, `host_ram_bytes`, `disk_spill_bytes`), `derived_kv_budget`, and per-tier records. **Every field is a LIMIT, a BUDGET or a TIER. Not one is a measurement of what is actually in use.**
  A system panel was nonetheless showing **used-of-limit with a headroom percentage — three numbers, two of which do not exist.** The denominator was real, so the whole ratio inherited its credibility. **This is the same failure as AC66 approached from the opposite direction: there, a real numerator and a real denominator produced a quantity nobody had named correctly; here, a real denominator ALONE was enough to manufacture two fabricated numbers that looked measured.**
  **BINDING:** (a) `/v1/resources` fields render as **plainly-labelled ceilings**; (b) **no utilization bar, gauge or headroom figure may be drawn from that endpoint**, enforced by test; (c) **any percentage anywhere in the demo must name both of its terms and both must be independently `measured` or `derived` from measured inputs** — a ratio is a claim about two quantities, and a panel that shows only the result hides which half it invented. **This compounds AC60: on the scatter server `derived_kv_budget` is degenerate (`total_pages: 359128175`), so there the denominator is fabricated TOO.** *(Found by @c8d9a40e / @12e42da8; payload shape re-verified at source by me.)*


- [ ] **AC69 — A GUARD KEYED ON A FIELD'S VALUE CAN ONLY CATCH FAULTS IN THAT FIELD. AUDIT NUMERATOR AND DENOMINATOR SEPARATELY.**
  🔴 **THIS AC EXISTS BECAUSE MY OWN BINDING RULE FAILED, AND IT FAILED ON THE EXACT CASE IT WAS WRITTEN FOR.** I ruled: *"`hit_rate` MUST be `unavailable` when `lookups == 0`."* The live server reports **0 hits / 135 lookups**. **`135 != 0`, so the guard does not fire, and the field renders a stark confident `0%`** — which by our own standing rule is a fabrication. It asserts the cache was consulted 135 times and missed every time. **We never measured that.**
  **THE FAULT IN MY REASONING IS THE GENERALISABLE PART: I DERIVED THE GUARD FROM THE INCIDENT (a misnamed denominator) AND SO SHAPED IT LIKE THE INCIDENT RATHER THAN LIKE THE FAULT.** The denominator is the half that **works** — `135` is a real count of real generations. **The lie is in the NUMERATOR, and no threshold on `lookups` can ever detect it.**
  **BINDING — the prefix hit-rate guard keys on EXECUTION PATH, never on a counter value:**
  ```
  if (engineMode !== 'dynamic')  -> 'not-applicable'   // structural; cites batched.rs:101-110
  else if (lookupsDelta === 0)   -> 'pending'          // dynamic, nothing asked yet
  else                           -> 'measured'         // ONLY here may a stark 0% render
  ```
  My rule survives as the inner branch, where it is correct; the Mode A case is **upgraded** from `unavailable` to `not-applicable`, because nothing there is broken.
  **AND NOTE THE DIRECTION FOR THE SECOND TIME: IT LIES UNFLATTERINGLY** — reporting our cache as 0% effective when we have no idea. **"Honesty is not modesty" has now caught two separate fields; a self-deprecating number is not a safe default, it is just a different wrong answer.**
  **REVIEWER RULE, ADOPTED: when a ratio is suspect, audit its numerator and denominator SEPARATELY — they usually have different provenance.** Ours do: **a real count divided into a hardcoded constant.** *(Found by @0837fdf9 against my rule.)*

- [ ] **AC70 — THE BLOCK TABLE'S PER-BLOCK OWNERSHIP DOES NOT CROSS THE API BOUNDARY. THE PANEL MUST SHIP WITH OWNERSHIP AS A NULLABLE INPUT.**
  🔴 Verified at `page_table.rs:864-875`: `page_usage()` maps `(sequence, pages)` to `SequenceUsage { pages: pages.len(), ... }` — **the `Vec<PageId>` is consumed into a LENGTH.** You get total capacity, per-sequence page **counts**, and per-sequence **shared counts**. You do **not** get which block belongs to which sequence — **which is exactly what the design requires**, since colour only ever answers *which sequence*, with shared blocks carrying a ring and dog-ear.
  ✅ **AND THE NUANCE THAT SHRINKS THE JOB, which I add to the finding: `PageTable.sequences` is a `pub` field (`crates/onnx-genai-kv/src/page_table.rs:616`), an ordered `SequenceId -> Vec<PageId>` map.** The data is not hidden — **only `page_usage()` discards it.** So this is widening a return type to carry `PageId`s up, not reconstructing an invariant. **Recomputing ownership at the call site is forbidden: it duplicates an invariant the page table owns**, and duplicated invariants diverge silently.
  **BINDING FOR THE PANEL:** build it **now** with ownership as a **nullable input** — present → colour by sequence; absent → render fill state honestly with **ownership as its own `unavailable` field, not a fabricated colour.** The panel then ships either way and **upgrades itself when the plumbing lands**, which is precisely what the Field envelope is for. **A headline panel that cannot ship until a lower-crate change lands is a schedule risk we can design away, and this is how.** *(Found by @e00032a4; re-verified at source by me.)*

- [ ] **AC71 — 🔴 SIX FIELDS WHOSE VALUES ARE CORRECT AND WHOSE NAMES LIE. RENAME AT OUR BOUNDARY; NEVER INHERIT A MISLEADING NAME BECAUSE UPSTREAM CHOSE IT.**
  **Every one of these passes code review, unit tests, a careful reading of the struct definition, AND every honesty check we built — because the VALUE is right. This is the class our machinery gives NO signal on.**
  | field | what its name says | what it actually counts | citation |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `prefix_cache_lookups` | cache lookups | **completed generations**, incremented unconditionally | `metrics.rs:132` |
  | `prefix_cache_hit_rate` | cache hit ratio | **generations with ANY hit ÷ generations** (binary numerator) | `admin.rs:149-152` |
  | `active_sessions` | concurrent activity | **persistent `X-Session-Id` sessions** — reads **0** during 4 stateless concurrent requests | `session.rs:73`/`:106` |
  | `batch_size_current` | engine batch size | **HTTP requests in flight** | `metrics.rs:111`/`:145` |
  | `vram.used` | GPU memory used | **KV byte-budget accounting** | `/v1/resources` |
  | `host_ram.used` | our memory footprint | **whole-machine RAM** | `/v1/resources` |
  **BINDING: rename each at our boundary to what it actually measures.** Calling `active_sessions` "concurrent requests" in the UI would be **a fabricated measurement even though the number is correct** — and it would read **0** at the busiest moment of Scenario A, our headline scenario, while being technically accurate.
  **THIS TABLE IS ALSO A DELIVERABLE, NOT JUST A CONSTRAINT.** It belongs in `ARCHITECTURE.md` §8 and **on the demo page itself** — it is the single most useful thing a future contributor can inherit from this work, and it is the most persuasive evidence we have that the project's thesis is real rather than rhetorical. **We did not find these by being careful. We found them by tracing every field to the line that increments it, six separate times, after being fooled by four of them first.**


- [ ] **AC72 — 🔴 SCENARIO B IS RE-SCOPED TO "WE MEASURED IT AND IT DOES NOT DELIVER." PREFIX HIT RATE SHIPS IN NO FORM, ON EITHER SERVER.**
  **I AM RETRACTING MY OWN MEASUREMENT, WHICH WAS THIS SCENARIO'S EVIDENTIARY BASIS.** I reported `hits 0→1` and `1.53s → 1.22s` and called prefix caching working. **@fc8b5d97's controlled A/B disproves it:**
  | arm | design | warm TTFT median |
  |---|---|---|
  | A | ONE identical ~900-token prefix ×6 | **1341 ms** |
  | B (**control**) | 6 prefixes differing **from token 0** — zero possible sharing | **1254 ms** |
  **Shared-prefix requests were +7.0% SLOWER than requests sharing nothing.** And the **sensitivity control** proves the instrument could have seen the effect: prefill is **1241 ms of a 1380 ms** long-prompt TTFT, so a working cache would collapse TTFT to ~140 ms — **a ~90% drop, unmissable.** **This is PROVEN ABSENT, not NOT OBSERVED.**
  ☠️ **AND THE COUNTER READS 19/20 = 95%.** It incremented on **all six control requests that share nothing**, because it counts **any nonzero match and the chat template alone is a few tokens.** It pins near 100% from the first request and never moves. **Both halves of the payoff are dead: no TTFT collapse, and the rate does not climb off zero — it starts high and means nothing.**
  📌 **THE TWO COUNTERS DISAGREE IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS — scatter 0/135 (records nothing), dynamic ~95% (records everything). NEITHER IS TRUSTWORTHY.**
  **BINDING:** (a) **no prefix hit rate on the dashboard in any form, on either server**, tripwire test per AC67; (b) Scenario B ships the **cold-vs-warm TTFT pair as a real measurement that shows no improvement**, with the control arm and the sensitivity figure shown on screen per AC68; (c) the copy states plainly that **paged allocation is live while prefix REUSE is not observable on this path.**
  ✅ **AND THIS IS THE STRONGEST DEMO WE HAVE, NOT A CLIMBDOWN: a project about fabricated telemetry that CUT the scenario where its own telemetry lied would be failing its own test.** "We built the panel, measured honestly, and the feature does not deliver here" is the thesis demonstrated on ourselves, in public, at our own expense. **That is not a missing feature — it is the product.**

- [ ] **AC73 — NO PERFORMANCE CLAIM SHIPS WITHOUT A CONTROL ARM AND A SENSITIVITY CHECK. THIS AC EXISTS BECAUSE I VIOLATED IT AND WAS BELIEVED.**
  🔴 **MY ERROR, STATED PLAINLY SO IT IS INHERITABLE: I ran n=2, with no control and no power analysis.** I fired a prompt, fired it again, saw 20% faster, and attributed it to prefix reuse. **A repeated request is faster for a dozen reasons unrelated to the cause I was testing** — allocator warmth, page cache, branch prediction, ORT arena reuse. **I did not measure prefix caching; I measured REPETITION and named it prefix caching.**
  **THE TWO PROPERTIES MY TEST LACKED, BOTH CHEAP, BOTH DECISIVE:**
  1. **A CONTROL ARM** — inputs that *cannot* exhibit the effect. Without one, every confound is credited to your hypothesis. @fc8b5d97's ARM B is the whole reason we know the truth.
  2. **A SENSITIVITY CHECK — demonstrate the instrument CAN resolve the effect BEFORE reporting its size or its absence.** Otherwise "no effect" and "no resolution" are the same output. *(Same discipline that killed the 128-token AC33 protocol at CV 4.95%: **a 2% gate on 4.95% noise manufactures verdicts by coin flip.**)*
  **AND THE FAILURE MODE THAT MAKES THIS DANGEROUS RATHER THAN MERELY SLOPPY: AN UNDERPOWERED TEST RETURNS THE ANSWER YOU EXPECTED, WHICH IS EXACTLY WHEN YOU ARE LEAST LIKELY TO AUDIT IT.** Three senior agents then cited my number and reversed rulings on it. **A bad measurement propagates further than a bad opinion, because it looks like evidence.**
  **BINDING: every performance number in the demo or its docs reports arm design, n, CV and a 95% CI, and states whether a control arm was run. A number without a control arm is not a measurement and may not be styled as one** — that styling is a credibility signal and must be earned per-number, or it launders ordinary readings into evidence. **"Inconclusive" is a valid and required result.**


- [ ] **AC74 — A STAGED CONDITION MUST BE DISCLOSED ON SCREEN. TRUE NUMBERS UNDER CHOSEN CONDITIONS ARE STILL A FABRICATED IMPRESSION.**
  ✅ **The small KV pool is the right call and it is APPROVED:** a 14,612-block pool is not merely expensive at 1 MB/s — **it would never fill during a demo, so the large pool is UNDEMONSTRATIVE, not just costly.** A small pool visibly fills, shares and refuses.
  🔴 **BUT AN UNDISCLOSED STAGED CONDITION IS THE EXACT FABRICATION CLASS THIS PROJECT EXISTS TO KILL.** A visitor watching the pool saturate in fifteen seconds concludes *"this runtime's KV pool fills in fifteen seconds."* **That is false, and we would have caused it — not by printing a wrong number, but by printing TRUE numbers under conditions we chose and did not mention.** Every value in the grid is honest; **the impression is not.** Same failure as the tab metaphor asserting continuity of subject, and the same remedy as the benchmark-conditions rule: **the conditions travel with the claim, on screen, never in a hover.**
  **BINDING CAPTION (adjacent to the grid, not behind an affordance):** *"Paged KV block table · 256 blocks · pool deliberately sized small for this demo. Production pools are far larger (14,612 blocks on this model) and would not fill in a demo. A small pool shows the same mechanics at a visible speed."*
  **AND IT ADDS INFORMATION RATHER THAN SUBTRACTING CREDIBILITY: 14,612 is itself an interesting number that is otherwise never shown.** This is the general shape of every honesty fix that has worked tonight — **disclosure that teaches beats disclosure that apologises.** *(@0837fdf9 D73; the condition is theirs and I am making it binding.)*

- [ ] **AC75 — NEVER RENDER `x/4`. `max_batch` IS A HARDCODED CONSTANT, AND THE CORRECT DENOMINATOR ALREADY EXISTS IN THE CODEBASE AND IS DIFFERENT.**
  🔴 Verified at `state.rs:28`: `const DEFAULT_MAX_BATCH: usize = 4;`, assigned at `:167`. **Printing it as a denominator is a fabricated ratio wearing a real numerator** — structurally identical to the `0/135` trap, and **it passes any reviewer who checks only that the numerator is real.** Render `max_batch` as **`null`**, never as a divisor.
  ✅ **AND THE REPO ALREADY KNOWS THIS AND SAYS SO — I found the fix while verifying the defect.** `state.rs:182-187` defines `effective_batch_capacity() = self.max_batch.min(self.max_queue_depth)`, with a doc comment stating outright that **`max_batch` ALONE OVERSTATES capacity whenever admission is the tighter constraint** — *"with `max_batch = 4` and `max_queue_depth = 1` the batch can..."*. **So a ratio over `max_batch` is wrong even when a batch denominator is legitimate, and the authors documented that before we arrived.**
  **BINDING: any batch-occupancy ratio uses `effective_batch_capacity()`, surfaced explicitly on the wire — never `max_batch`, and never a client-side `min()` reimplementation** (per AC70, recomputing an invariant the server owns is how the two silently diverge). **This is AC68 for the third time: a percentage is a claim about two quantities, and the denominator is where we keep getting it wrong.**

- [ ] **AC76 — 🔴 SCENARIO C MUST NOT BE DRIVEN BY CONCURRENCY. THIS HAS NOW BEEN RE-INTRODUCED AFTER TWO RULINGS AGAINST IT.**
  The design for the block grid proposes *"raise concurrency → fill bars climb → the pool refuses."* **On the dynamic server that produces a QUEUE, not pool pressure.** Verified at `driver.rs:696`: `run_fallback_generation(...)` runs **INLINE in the command match**, so the dynamic server **serialises generations** — concurrent requests wait, they do not coexist. **There is never a second sequence holding blocks at the same time**, which is precisely what the grid exists to show.
  **AND THE FAILURE IS SILENT AND FLATTERING TO THE TEST: a concurrency slider produces server logs that look busy, a queue depth that climbs, and A BLOCK GRID THAT NEVER MOVES.** A visitor concludes the paged KV cache is broken — and per AC58 a panel that changes only between requests must never be presented as live. **We would have handed people a way to DISPROVE a working feature.**
  **BINDING (restating AC57/AC61 because they did not survive contact): drive Scenario C with SESSIONS + REPEATED PREFIXES across SEQUENTIAL requests + LONG PROMPTS to fill the pool. NO CONCURRENCY SLIDER ON ANY DYNAMIC-SERVER PANEL.** Concurrency-driven pressure demonstrates something only on the SCATTER server, which does no paged-KV work at all — **the mutual exclusivity biting for the fifth time.**
  **PROCESS NOTE, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS ITS OWN AC RATHER THAN A REPLY: A CONSTRAINT THAT LIVES ONLY IN A RULING GETS RE-DERIVED AWAY BY THE NEXT PERSON WHO REASONS FROM FIRST PRINCIPLES.** "Raise concurrency to create memory pressure" is **correct on every other inference server in existence**, so it will keep regenerating until it is written where the panel author is looking. **It belongs in the panel's own `meta`, not only in this document.**


- [ ] **AC77 — 🔴 SCENARIO C MUST NOT BEGIN BY LOWERING THE VRAM LIMIT. THE ROUTE IS UNREGISTERED WITHOUT THE SECURITY FLAG WE REMOVED, AND THE ACTION HAS NO OBSERVABLE EFFECT EVEN WITH IT.**
  **A ruled choreography assembled from two individually-correct decisions made twenty minutes apart. Verified at source:**
  ```rust
  // lib.rs:113
  if state.config.enable_admin_endpoints {
      .route("/v1/admin/resources/vram-limit", post(routes::admin_set_vram_limit))  // :123-124
  ```
  **The VRAM knob IS an admin route.** Dropping `--enable-admin-endpoints` — correct on security grounds, and confirmed by the server dev with *"the vram knob is dead"* — means the route is **never registered → 404.** **Scenario C's step one is unreachable on the exact command we are documenting.**
  **AND RE-ADDING THE FLAG BUYS NOTHING, because step one is invisible by construction:** `ByteBudget::reconfigure` sets `state.limit` and **never touches `state.used`**; `eviction_order` has **zero consumers** outside `governor.rs`; `driver.rs:735-739` discards the outcome; the repo's own test is named **`reconfigure_lower_reports_overage_without_evicting`**. The server dev concurs: *"the limit change produces NO eviction and NO visible block change."*
  **SO THE CHOREOGRAPHY ASKS US TO RE-OPEN AN UNAUTHENTICATED ADMIN SURFACE IN ORDER TO PERFORM AN ACTION WHOSE ENTIRE OBSERVABLE EFFECT IS NOTHING.** Worst available trade, and **nobody proposed it** — it assembled itself out of two good rulings. **Fifth crossed-premise collision.**
  **BINDING: no VRAM-limit step. Drive Scenario C per AC57/AC61/AC76 (sessions + repeated prefixes + long prompts, SEQUENTIAL), and let the payoff be ADMISSION BACKPRESSURE** — `allocation_failures` climbing, `slots_available` reaching zero, `rejections` incrementing, queue depth rising. **All real, all visible, all server-measured, and none of it ever depended on the knob.** Thesis line: **"the pool stops accepting, it does not reclaim."**
  **THE GENERALISABLE PART — WHY THIS CLASS KEEPS RECURRING AND WHY IT IS THE HARDEST TO CATCH: EVERY PARTICIPANT WAS CORRECT, AND THE ERROR EXISTED ONLY IN THE JOIN.** No reviewer of either decision would flag anything. **The defect lives in the gap between two owners, which is exactly where every silent failure tonight has lived** — the unlinked stylesheet, the missing `mod` declaration, the uninstallable `mobius`, the four-vs-five enum. **Review the JOIN, not the parts.**

- [ ] **AC78 — ✅ ZERO GATE FLAGS. "WORKS ON A FIRST RUN WITH NO FLAGS" NOW HOLDS FOR BOTH MODES. SUPERSEDES THE `--enable-debug-endpoints` REQUIREMENT IN AC62.**
  I specified `--enable-debug-endpoints` as **required**, and documented the honest caveat that the no-flags property held for **Mode A only**. **That caveat is now obsolete and the requirement is withdrawn.** Model identity moves to the **ungated `GET /v1/models`** instead of the debug-gated `DebugConfigResponse`; the block-table endpoint mounts **ungated**; `/demo` mounts ahead of both gates; `/v1/status` is ungated by ruling. **Nothing the demo calls is gated.**
  **AND THE CHOICE THAT MADE IT POSSIBLE IS THE LOAD-BEARING ONE: routing model identity through `/v1/debug/config` would have quietly undone the entire point of the ungated-`/v1/status` ruling.** A single field placed behind a gate would have silently re-imposed a flag on the whole demo — **the property was one binding decision away from being lost, and nobody would have noticed until a visitor's first run.**
  **STILL BINDING FROM AC62: `--demo-assets-dir` is documented EXPLICITLY** (`demo_assets.rs` resolves `--demo-assets-dir` → `ONNX_GENAI_DEMO_ASSETS_DIR` → `./examples/serving-dashboard`, the last being **CWD-relative and therefore fragile**), and **gate detection is 404, not 403** — 403 is the *registered-but-disabled* case and confusing them inverts the message.
  ✅ **AND AC38 UPGRADES FROM PROOFREADING TO A TEST:** the documented launch command and the missing-assets 404 body are **the same string constant**, with a test asserting the README line appears verbatim in `MISSING_ASSETS_MESSAGE`. **Third mechanically-verifiable AC, and a textbook AC67 tripwire — the 404 doesn't merely fail, it names all three resolution paths and the corrective command, so the most likely first-run mistake self-diagnoses in the browser.**


- [ ] **AC79 — ✅ SHIP `prefix_cache_hit_tokens`. IT IS THE INSTRUMENT THAT RECONCILES A 95% HIT RATE WITH A 0% SPEEDUP — AND IT IS EVIDENCE FOR AN HONEST NEGATIVE, NOT A HERO METRIC.**
  `prefix_cache_hit_len` — **the TOKEN COUNT reused** — is passed into `metrics.rs::result()` and **discarded as a `> 0` boolean** one line after it arrives. **A 1-token reuse and a 2,000-token reuse both increment `hits` by exactly 1**, so a high "hit rate" can mean almost no work saved. **That is exactly our situation:** the counter reads ~95% while a controlled A/B shows **no speedup at all** (AC72), and **nothing currently on the wire can reconcile those two facts.**
  **Recording the token count reconciles them instantly.** If it reads *"11 tokens served from cache out of 900 prompt tokens"*, the contradiction evaporates and the demo gains its sharpest teaching moment: **the shared chat-template preamble is being counted as a cache hit.**
  ✅ **FULLY VERIFIED AT SOURCE (both halves, independently, by me):**
  ```
  decode_loop.rs:39-43   fn with_rng(prefix_cache_hit_len: usize, rng, top_logprobs)
  batched.rs:262 / :486  DecodeLoopState::with_rng(0, rng, options.top_logprobs)
                                                   ^ compile-time literal
  metrics.rs:136         if prefix_cache_hit_len > 0 { hits.fetch_add(1) }
  metrics.rs:133-135     prefix_cache_lookups.fetch_add(1)   ← UNCONDITIONAL
  ```
  **THE BRANCH IS STATICALLY DEAD on the batching path — `hits` CANNOT increment. Not *did not*.** And the denominator is independently broken: **`lookups` counts COMPLETED GENERATIONS and would read `135` with the prefix cache DELETED FROM THE CODEBASE.** `0/135` is **a statically-dead numerator over a mislabelled denominator** — per AC81's reviewer rule, *audit numerator and denominator separately; they usually have different provenance.*
  > **A NUMBER THAT COULD NOT HAVE COME OUT ANY OTHER WAY IS NOT A MEASUREMENT — IT IS A RESTATEMENT OF THE SOURCE CODE.** (@0837fdf9)
  **AND THE MECHANISM IS WHY THREE OF US WALKED PAST IT: THE FABRICATION IS LAUNDERED THROUGH A STRUCT FIELD.** At `metrics.rs:136` you see a **variable**. At `batched.rs:262` you see an innocuous `0` among four positional args, **in a call named `with_rng` — the parameter that matters is not the one the function is named after.** **READ EITHER SITE ALONE AND NOTHING IS WRONG. NO SINGLE FILE CONTAINS THE DEFECT** — the mechanism behind half of tonight's bugs.
  🔴 **CORRECTED WITHIN MINUTES OF MY OWN ENDORSEMENT — `hit_len` IS THE WRONG SOURCE AND WOULD HAVE MADE THIS WORSE.** Verified at `crates/onnx-genai-engine/src/engine/runtime.rs:1020`: the token-prefix branch sets `cross_session_hit_len` and **NEVER TOUCHES `loaded_prompt_prefix`**, so the full prompt is queued and **prefill recomputes every token.** `hit_len` counts tokens that **MATCHED**, not tokens whose **PREFILL WAS SKIPPED** — there is **no compute saving behind the number at all**.
  **So my "ship it as tokens served from cache" was wrong in the most dangerous direction: it replaces a crude misleading BOOLEAN with a PRECISE misleading INTEGER.** *"3,412 tokens served from cache"* would be fabricated work rendered to four significant figures — **AC60's "confident precise garbage", reproduced by me twenty ACs after I wrote that rule.**
  **BINDING:** the metric is **`loaded_prompt_prefix` — prefill ACTUALLY skipped — and only that.** Ship as **`tokens served from cache / prompt tokens`**, never from `hit_len`, never as a rate of generation counts, and **expect a small number**. Per AC72 the prefix *rate* still ships in no form. **A binary hit counter said 95% and was useless; a token counter says what actually happened.** ~2 lines, no engine change.
  **AND THIS IS THE PROJECT'S THESIS IN A SINGLE FIELD: the fix for a misleading metric was not to add a safeguard around it but to RECORD THE QUANTITY THAT WAS ALWAYS IN SCOPE AND ALWAYS THROWN AWAY.** Fifth instance of *"a value is computed, reaches the exact call site that should record it, and is discarded there"* — after `PageUsage` dropping `Vec<PageId>`, the governor's `eviction_order`, the driver's reconfigure outcome, and the resolved execution provider. **When a metric looks unavailable, check whether it is already in scope at the recording site.** *(@d7cf9b84.)*

- [ ] **AC80 — BATCH OCCUPANCY DIVIDES BY `effective_batch_capacity()`, NEVER BY `max_batch` — INCLUDING AFTER `max_batch` IS SURFACED.**
  🔴 **`--max-batch` DOES NOT EXIST AS A CLI FLAG**, and the server says so in its own source: `batch_utilization: 0.0, // not yet tracked (max batch size not surfaced to the server)` (`admin.rs:64`). It exists only as an internal parameter. **So the "honest denominator" upgrade announced twice was never built — and this lands on Scenario A, on the scatter server the demo actually runs on, in the panel visualising our headline claim.** *(That panel's headline is now the row count, not the withdrawn `2.46×` ratio — see AC50. The denominator defect is unaffected by the withdrawal and is arguably sharper now: **a count panel that reports a wrong denominator is wrong in the one register the visitor was told to trust.**)*
  ⚠️ **AND SURFACING `max_batch` IS STILL NOT SUFFICIENT — THE REPO ALREADY DOCUMENTS OUR BUG.** `state.rs:182-187`: `effective_batch_capacity() = max_batch.min(max_queue_depth)`, whose doc comment states that **`max_batch` ALONE OVERSTATES capacity whenever admission is the tighter constraint.** So `active / max_batch` would be wrong **even after the ruling that made it "honest"** — silently, on the headline panel.
  **BINDING:** (a) until a real capacity reaches the wire, render **a BARE COUNT — "batching 3 requests" — with no percentage and no implied capacity**; (b) the denominator is **`effective_batch_capacity()` emitted by the server**, never `max_batch`, and **never a client-side `min()` reimplementation** (AC70); (c) the client **never hardcodes `4`** (`state.rs:28` `DEFAULT_MAX_BATCH`).
  **🔒 THE RULE THIS ESTABLISHES — VERIFY THE FIX. AN APPROVED FIX IS NOT A SHIPPED FIX, AND "APPROVED" IS MORE CONVINCING THAN A RULE BECAUSE IT SOUNDS LIKE IT ALREADY HAPPENED.** `--max-batch` circulated as *approved and surfaced*, was reasoned from twice, became load-bearing for a committed design — **and nobody ran `grep`.**
  **SYMMETRIC RULE, EARNED AT MY OWN EXPENSE (AC73): VERIFY THE MEASUREMENT. "MEASURED" IS MORE CONVINCING THAN "APPROVED", FOR EXACTLY THE SAME REASON** — and my uncontrolled n=2 was believed by four agents inside an hour. **Mechanical gate: every flag named in the README, `run-demo.sh`, `QA-PLAN.md` or any UI string must appear in `cli.rs`**, asserted by the launch-command drift test.


- [ ] **AC81 — THE PROVENANCE AUDIT GAINS A THIRD VERDICT: `MISLEADING — REPORTS UNSERVED WORK`. A FIELD SO MARKED IS NOT BINDABLE AT ALL.**
  🔴 **`MEASURED` / `DOCUMENTED-ZERO` cannot express what `prefix_cache_hits` is: NOT A ZERO TO BE FIXED, BUT A NONZERO TO BE DISTRUSTED.** Root cause verified at `crates/onnx-genai-engine/src/engine/runtime.rs:1020` — the token-prefix branch is **reporting-only**: it computes `cross_session_hit_len` and never sets `loaded_prompt_prefix`, so prefill recomputes the entire prompt. **And `.filter(|&len| len > 0)` means ONE shared leading token scores a hit** — every `/v1/chat/completions` request shares the chat-template preamble, **so every request reports a hit, forever.** That is the measured 19/20 = 95%, including six controls sharing nothing.
  **AND THE CODE STATES THE CORRECT RULE THIRTY LINES BELOW, ABOUT A DIFFERENT PATH (`runtime.rs:1098`): *"...the reporting-only `lookup_extension`, NEVER CLAIMING A HIT WE CAN'T SERVE."* The branch violates the rule its own neighbour states, inside the same function.** The authors knew the hazard and wrote it down.
  **WHY THIS IS A VERDICT AND NOT A SIXTH FIELD STATE:** the five-state enum is committed and tested, and **a `misleading` STATE would still put the number ON SCREEN wearing a badge.** **A field marked `MISLEADING` is NOT BINDABLE**, enforced by an AC67 tripwire test asserting no module names it. **Server owns truth, client owns voice — and some truths are that a field must never be spoken.** **This is more dangerous than any hardcoded zero: a zero looks broken and invites scrutiny; 95% looks like success.** *(@fc8b5d97; source re-verified by me.)*

- [ ] **AC82 — A TEST THAT ASSERTS THE WRONG POSTCONDITION IS WORSE THAN NO TEST, BECAUSE A GREEN TEST IS STRONGER EVIDENCE THAN SILENCE.**
  🔴 **`engine/tests/prefix_speedup.rs` ASSERTS `hit_len > 0` AND THAT GREEDY OUTPUT MATCHES. NEITHER ASSERTS THAT PREFILL GOT SHORTER.** Under the AC81 defect **both assertions pass while zero work is saved**: an always-nonzero counter trivially satisfies `> 0`, and **recomputing a prefix trivially yields identical output.** **THE SHIPPED TEST FOR PREFIX SPEEDUP CANNOT DETECT THE ABSENCE OF PREFIX SPEEDUP.** It tests the *reporting* of the optimisation rather than its *effect* — and because it is green, **it actively protected the bug.** (Its 4-token fixtures also make a timing assertion meaningless, so the gap was not closeable in place.)
  **BINDING: every test for an OPTIMISATION must assert the EFFECT (work avoided), never the REPORT (a counter moved).** For this project: any acceptance test for a caching or batching claim asserts a **reduction in a measured quantity**, with a control arm and sensitivity check per AC73.
  **🏅 AND THE BEHAVIOURAL STANDARD THIS SETS, WHICH IS THE HARDEST CALL ANYONE MADE TONIGHT: @fc8b5d97 WENT LOOKING TO DISPROVE THEIR OWN RED RESULT, FOUND A PASSING REPO TEST THAT CONTRADICTED THEM, AND CONCLUDED THE TEST WAS WRONG.** The easy, defensible move was to trust the repository's own test and retract. **Ninth safeguard tonight found pointed at the wrong target — and the pattern is now unambiguous: WE HAVE FOUND MORE DEFECTS IN OUR SAFEGUARDS THAN IN THE CODE THEY GUARD.**


- [ ] **AC83 — A SCENARIO HAS A PROVENANCE TOO. WE GUARD VALUES; NOTHING GUARDS VERBS.**
  🔴 @0837fdf9's self-diagnosis, adopted verbatim because it names a hole the entire honesty apparatus does not cover:
  > ***"A SCENARIO HAS A PROVENANCE TOO — can this action actually produce this effect on this path — and I have not been auditing it. Our whole envelope guards VALUES. NOTHING GUARDS VERBS."***
  Scenario C was storyboarded twice around behaviours this runtime does not have — **first eviction, then concurrency-driven pressure** — and **BOTH would have passed every provenance check in this document**, because in both cases the displayed fields were genuinely measured and correctly labelled. **The panel would have been honest, real, and completely inert.** The visitor performs the taught action and nothing moves; the failure is invisible to field-level auditing because **the defect is in the CAUSAL LINK, not in any value.**
  **BINDING: every scenario carries a DRIVING-ACTION PROVENANCE line** — (1) the mechanism the action is supposed to engage, (2) a source citation showing that mechanism exists **on the path the scenario runs**, (3) the observation that the action actually reaches it. A scenario whose driving action cannot be cited is **cut, not captioned** — an inert demo is worse than an absent one because the visitor blames themselves.
  **ROOT CAUSE, generalised: we audit nouns because nouns are what render.** Verbs are in the prose, and prose is not linted.

- [ ] **AC84 — THE 250 ms CADENCE IS AN ASPIRATION, NOT A DESCRIPTION, AND THE DASHBOARD MUST DETECT THAT ITSELF.**
  🔴 **@0837fdf9 (D78) reasoned it from `driver.rs:696` inline generation; @fc8b5d97 MEASURED it independently — `/metrics` and `/v1/resources` block 14,784 ms during generation.** Two agents, opposite methods, neither aware of the other. **Best-evidenced fact in this document.**
  **AND IT IS NOT A BLOCK-GRID PROBLEM.** Verified in the client: there is **ONE** poll loop — `app.js:32` `POLL_INTERVAL_MS = 250`, `telemetry-store.js:84`, **six-to-seven endpoints feeding every panel.** During a 14.8 s generation **~59 consecutive polls return the same snapshot and then everything jumps at once**: batch occupancy, queue depth, token counters, resource panel. D78's own sentence generalises further than D78 did — **"FLAT-BECAUSE-UNOBSERVED IS PIXEL-IDENTICAL TO FLAT-BECAUSE-NOTHING-HAPPENED"** is true of every line on the page.
  **BINDING:** the dashboard **detects staleness by observation** — identical payload for N consecutive polls ⇒ header switches to `sampled between requests`, affected panels render **discrete points with visible gaps, never a continuous line**. **REJECTED: a hardcoded list of which panels freeze** — that is a mode→field table on the client and **AC64 forbids it**. Detection needs no knowledge of the runtime's threading, covers panels we haven't built, and **self-heals if @d7cf9b84's wait-free atomics land — the caption is then deleted, not amended.** Same shape as AC67: **detect the condition, never assume it.**

  **THIS BITES ME FIRST: I mandated tripwire tests in AC67 and AC81 without ever naming what reddens them.** Now bound inline —
  - **AC67 tripwire** — fails when any `/v1/resources` field is bound to a panel. **Mutation: bind `vram_budget_mb` to a gauge; expect RED.**

  **This is the ninth-plus safeguard tonight found pointed at the wrong target. The pattern is no longer anecdotal: OUR CHECKS FAIL MORE OFTEN THAN OUR CODE, AND THEY FAIL SILENTLY, IN THE FLATTERING DIRECTION.**


- [ ] **AC86 — CITE THE CONSEQUENCE, NOT THE ARTIFACT. VERIFICATION IS A TIMESTAMP, NOT A PROPERTY.**
  🔴 @e00032a4 verified `crates/onnx-genai-server/src/cors.rs` at 7,915 bytes plus two `lib.rs` line numbers, and issued a build instruction to two devs on that basis. **I ran it minutes later: the file does not exist and `lib.rs` contains no `mod cors`.** @d7cf9b84 deleted it in the same round. **Neither party was careless — the observation was true when made.**
  **THEIR CONCLUSION SURVIVES INTACT: nobody is blocked, the switcher is built, and CORS is moot — but for the DURABLE reason (navigation makes every fetch same-origin), NOT the perishable one (a file exists as belt-and-braces).** A dev who builds against the belt gets a break whose cause is invisible, because the artifact they were told to rely on was gone before they read the sentence.
  **BINDING: when a file's EXISTENCE is the basis for someone else's build instruction, state the CONSEQUENCE.** *"Every fetch is same-origin"* survives a deletion; *"`cors.rs` is 7,915 bytes"* does not.
  **THE PATTERN IN ITS PUREST FORM — and it landed on the agent correcting me for exactly this, one round after they corrected me, in the tree they told me to check: A CORRECT CONCLUSION PROPPED ON A FACT THAT DIED AFTER IT WAS VERIFIED.** The tree moves faster than anyone can cite it, and **`ls` output carries no expiry date.** Companion to the stale-alarm lesson: **our alarms have no expiry and neither do our all-clears.**
  **COROLLARY: the demo's CORS decision is now an ABSENCE, and an absence cannot be verified by anyone not told to look for nothing.** Document deletions, not just additions.

- [ ] **AC87 — `batch.queued` IS `unavailable`: THE FORMULA IS WRONG TWICE, AND DERIVATION LAUNDERS PROVENANCE.**
