  **BINDING:** `batch.queued` ships `unavailable`, reason naming `state.rs:28`. **`batch.in_flight` ships `measured`** — genuinely measured (`metrics.rs:112` `fetch_add` in `GenerationMetrics::start()`, `:145` `Drop`), correctly named, interesting alone. When the limit becomes reportable, `queued` derives from **`effective_batch_capacity()`, never `max_batch`.**
  > **REVIEWER CHECKLIST, verbatim (@0837fdf9): "A DERIVED FIELD IS ONLY AS HONEST AS ITS LEAST HONEST INPUT — AND DERIVATION LAUNDERS PROVENANCE. The output is a single clean number with NO VISIBLE SEAM where the constant went in."** Third instance today of one shape: `x/4` (§25), `0/135` (§22), and now this — **worse than both, because a ratio at least looks like an estimate, whereas `queued: 4` looks like a count the scheduler handed us.**

- [ ] **AC88 — TYPE SIZE IS A CLAIM ABOUT WHICH NUMBER MATTERS. LAYOUT CAN LIE ABOVE THE LAYER EVERY CHECK WE BUILT INSPECTS.**
  🟢 @0837fdf9 (D85/D86). **RESOLVED BY AC50's WITHDRAWAL, AND THEIR ANALYSIS IS WHAT MADE THE WITHDRAWAL THINKABLE.** Their finding was that the natural build sets the aggregate figure large with the per-stream figure small beside it, and **that still tells the lie, just more quietly: it reads as THE RESULT, WITH A CAVEAT — and the caveat is the half an engineer in the audience most needs.** ➡️ **The typographic problem dissolves once the hero is a count rather than a ratio: `four rows in one pass` and `each row slower than solo` are the SAME KIND of statement, so neither can be set as the exception to the other.** **A hierarchy problem in typography was a symptom of a hierarchy problem in the claim — and it could not be fixed in CSS because it was never a CSS defect.** *(@0837fdf9's `:2396` — "type size is a claim about importance" — is the rule that proves it, and it is theirs.)*
  > **We spent the entire session ensuring VALUES cannot lie. A LAYOUT THAT RANKS TWO TRUE VALUES REINSTATES THE LIE ABOVE THE VALUE LAYER, WHERE NO PROVENANCE CHECK REACHES. The field envelope has no opinion about font size.**
  **BINDING: identical type size, weight and colour; the SENTENCE is the hero and the numbers are its evidence** (*"Batching does not make any single request faster. It trades per-stream latency for total throughput."*). **No panel shows aggregate gain without per-stream cost ADJACENT — never a hover, never a footnote, never a second tab.** Conditions on screen per AC74.
  **AND THE ARITHMETIC IS EXPOSED DELIBERATELY: `0.62 × 4 ≈ 2.48`.** The figures are arithmetically linked, so a viewer can **check the relationship in their own head.** Same principle as `file:line` in the `not-applicable` hovers — **A DISPLAY A SKEPTIC CAN VERIFY BEATS ONE THEY MUST TRUST.**
  **ZERO-COST PROOF: the swimlane already draws this** — four decode segments advancing in lockstep, each slower than a solo request, finishing together. **The panel designed as the batching PROOF is simultaneously the per-stream-COST proof.**
  **COMPANION (D83): layout space is itself a claim about significance** — which is why `not-applicable` is a **treatment, not automatically a slot**, and why the absent preemption lane is one caption line citing `batched.rs:713-717, :757` rather than a greyed full-width band carrying the most visual weight on the page to say nothing happened.


- [ ] **AC89 — TWO VOCABULARIES, TWO AXES, BOTH NORMATIVE. THEY MAY NEVER BE QUERIED INTERCHANGEABLY.**
  @c0de4c2e flagged that the same idea is encoded twice: `telemetry-field.js:89-95` `FieldState` (`ok | pending | stale | unavailable | not-applicable`) and `telemetry-provenance.js:31` `Classification` (`MEASURED | DOCUMENTED_ZERO | NOT_PLUMBED | STRUCTURALLY_BYPASSED`, live at `:339, :369, :547, :582, :605`). **The proposed fix — collapse them — is WRONG, and the source answers it at `telemetry-provenance.js:28-29`: *"This is not a UI state — it is a statement about the server's code."***
  **THEY ARE ORTHOGONAL. `FieldState` = HOW A PANEL RENDERS. `Classification` = WHAT IS TRUE OF THE SERVER.** A field may be `DOCUMENTED_ZERO` in the audit and render `measured` with a genuine `0`. **Collapsing them destroys the distinction AC81 rests on — which is why `MISLEADING — reports unserved work` is a Classification and NOT a sixth render state.** (Independent confirmation that AC81 was filed in the right layer.)
  **THE REAL DEFECT IS THE NAME: `STRUCTURALLY_BYPASSED` READS LIKE A RENDER STATE**, which is how it got tangled with @0837fdf9's `Bypassed` and the ratified `not-applicable` — **four live names for one idea.** **BINDING: rename to `STRUCTURALLY_ABSENT`** (five call sites). *"Bypassed"* implies a path that exists and was skipped; **the truth is there is no path** — `crates/onnx-genai-engine/src/batched.rs:759`, physical pre-reserved rows.
  **AND I OWN THE COLLISION: I retired "BYPASSED" IN THIS SPEC AND NEVER CHECKED THE TREE. It is still there five times.** **A retirement that lands in a document but not in the code CREATES the collision it was written to remove** — the document and the tree now disagree, and both look authoritative.
  **ALSO RECORDED: `telemetry-field.js:19`'s four-state JSDoc IS ALREADY FIXED** — it now lists five and documents the `FIELD_STATES.MEASURED` → emits `'ok'` trap. Third stale citation in a single thread, from three agents including me.

- [ ] **AC90 — `/v1/status` HAS FIVE POPULATED FIELDS, AND `sessions[].id` IS REAL *AND* DELIBERATELY PARTIAL — A SHAPE THE ENVELOPE CANNOT CURRENTLY EXPRESS.**
  @e00032a4 via @c0de4c2e (QA-PLAN §7.1a): `sessions[].id` (`admin.rs:69`) is **genuinely measured and intentionally TRUNCATED, because full session ids are bearer credentials.**
  **A COMPLETE-LOOKING RENDER IMPLIES A COMPLETE VALUE.** Rendering a truncated identifier as though it were the whole thing is a small lie of exactly the class this document exists to prevent — and unlike our other cases, **the partiality is a FEATURE and must survive every future refactor.**
  **BINDING: `source: 'server'`, state `measured`, and the truncation stated IN THE LABEL — never in a hover. A HOVER IS WHERE A SECURITY PROPERTY GOES TO DIE:** it is invisible in screenshots, absent on touch devices, and the first thing dropped when a panel gets cramped. Anything a reader must know to avoid *misusing* a value belongs in the always-visible layer; hovers carry provenance, not semantics.


- [ ] **AC91 — THE BLOCK GRID POLLS LIVE. NO "SAMPLED BETWEEN REQUESTS" CAPTION. A FALSE ADMISSION OF IGNORANCE IS STILL A FALSE STATEMENT.**
  🔴 **CORRECTS D78 *AND* MY OWN AC84.** D78 asserted *"the engine services telemetry on the same thread that decodes, so the allocator can only be read between generations."* D88, from the same author twenty minutes later, reports @fc8b5d97's numbers: `/metrics` + `/v1/resources` **14,784 ms** under load, but **`/v1/status`, `/v1/debug/kv`, `/health` stay ~1.8 ms DURING THE SAME GENERATION.** `/v1/debug/kv` **is** the block table.
  **VERIFIED — D88 WINS:** `grep -n resource_snapshot crates/onnx-genai-server/src/routes/*.rs` → **`admin.rs:205` and `admin.rs:448`, and nothing else.** `debug_kv` (`lib.rs:105`) never calls it. **The stall is a property of TWO HANDLERS, not of the runtime's threading.**
  **WHY THE CAPTION IS THE DANGEROUS PART:** it would print a **false claim about our own architecture** as the most technically confident sentence on the page, and render **discrete points with visible gaps WHERE CONTINUOUS DATA EXISTS — fabricating an absence.** Every other rule here guards against overstating what we know. **This is the mirror image and nothing covered it: A FALSE ADMISSION OF IGNORANCE IS STILL A FALSE STATEMENT — and it is MORE credible than a false number, because self-deprecation reads as rigour.**
  **AND I AMPLIFIED IT:** AC84 generalised D78 to every panel on the strength of the single 250 ms loop. **The loop is real; the mechanism I attached to it was not.**
  **✅ BUT AC84's REMEDY SURVIVES AND IS VINDICATED BY THE ERROR THAT PRODUCED IT.** I ruled **against a hardcoded list of frozen panels** and **for observation-based detection**. Under D78 a hardcoded list would have shipped **permanent gaps in a panel that updates fine.** **Detection does not require anyone to have understood the mechanism correctly — and neither of us did.** *A rule that self-corrects under a wrong premise is worth more than a rule that is merely right today.*
  **BINDING: normal cadence for `/v1/status`, `/v1/debug/kv`, `/health`; `/metrics` and `/v1/resources` OFF the loop entirely (D88 — excluded, not slowed, per AC24's one-cycle-in-flight rule). Staleness rendering fires only when OBSERVED, never asserted.** @d7cf9b84's wait-free atomics are **not needed for this purpose.**
  > **STANDING: an endpoint's idle latency tells you nothing about its latency under load — AND ONE ENDPOINT'S LOAD BEHAVIOUR TELLS YOU NOTHING ABOUT ANOTHER'S ON THE SAME SERVER.** 18,000× apart, same process, same second.

Layout answers questions decoration cannot.** The panel keeps header and frame and **replaces its body with the explanation — no em-dash anywhere**, so there is no row of absences to misread as breakage, and it survives screenshots, touch, and grayscale.

It was, and it stalled 14,784 ms anyway.** Three agents reasoned correctly to a conclusion a stopwatch demolished. **A 3-line patch reviewed by two architects is precisely the artifact we would all wave through, and tonight's entire lesson is that OUR REVIEWS PASS WHAT OUR MEASUREMENTS CATCH.**

- [ ] **AC96 — STALENESS MEASURES THE AGE OF THE *SAMPLE*, NOT THE AGE OF THE *VALUE*. PER-PANEL CEILINGS BELOW.**
  @0837fdf9's ceilings, adopted (a design call, theirs):
  | panel | ceiling |
  |---|---|
  | swimlane · throughput sparkline | **3 s** (4 Hz sources; 3 s is 12 missed frames) |
  | batch occupancy · queue depth | **5 s** |
  | block grid | **EXEMPT — see below** |
  | `kv_pages_total` · pool capacity | **30 s** (structural, changes rarely) |
  | model card fields | **never** (immutable for process lifetime) |
  **Past the ceiling the value STOPS RENDERING AS A NUMBER.** An unbounded age suffix is still a number on screen, and ***"20.7 tok/s · 4m old"* is read by every human as *20.7 tok/s*.**
  **🔴 THE EXEMPTION'S STATED REASON IS WRONG AND ITS CONCLUSION IS RIGHT.** D78's *"event-sampled, not time-polled"* is dead per AC91 — `debug_kv` never calls `resource_snapshot()` and clocked **1.9 ms across 61 clean polls during a 384-token generation.** The grid polls live. **But a wall-clock ceiling would still mark it stale, because its CONTENT legitimately does not change during a generation while its SAMPLE is fresh every 250 ms.**
  **BINDING — the distinction nothing in this document had: `sampledAt` (when we last successfully READ) is the staleness input; `changedAt` (when the value last MOVED) is never it.** A panel that is polled successfully and reports an unchanged value is **healthy, not stale**. Conflating the two marks correct panels broken and — inverted — would mark a frozen feed fresh. The grid's staleness test is *"no successful sample"*, never *"no change."*

- [ ] **AC97 — A WHOLE-ORIGIN STALL MUST MARK EVERY PANEL ON THAT ORIGIN. AC6 ARRIVES THROUGH THE TRANSPORT LAYER.**
  🔴 **Every panel individually honest, and the page still lies — because honesty was enforced PER-FIELD while the failure is PER-CONNECTION.** No field-level check can see this: each panel holds a value that was genuinely measured, correctly labelled, and correctly sourced. **The defect is that the connection under all of them died.**
  **AND THE TWO-ORIGIN TOPOLOGY MAKES IT WORSE, NOT BETTER: one server dies while the other stays live, and the dead half keeps rendering its last good frame FOREVER — BESIDE A LIVE HALF. The working panels ACT AS CORROBORATION for the frozen ones.** A wholly-dead page reads as broken and invites a refresh; **a half-dead page reads as a working dashboard reporting a quiet system.**
  **BINDING: liveness is tracked PER ORIGIN, and a failed or timed-out cycle marks every field sourced from that origin at once** — not by decorating each panel, but at the panel-group level per AC92's scope rule. **Companion to AC91: staleness is rendered only when OBSERVED — and a dead socket is an observation.**
  > **THE GENERAL LESSON, and it is the last of tonight's family: EVERY FAILURE ON THIS PROJECT HAS BEEN SILENT AND HAS POINTED THE WRONG WAY.** A fabricated zero looks like a measurement. A CORS-blocked GET looks healthy in the logs. An idle endpoint looks fast. An empty spec looks permissive. **And a frozen origin looks like a calm one.**

AC15 already carried the `page_table.rs:867-875` unsatisfiability warning verbatim. **A PARAPHRASE PRESERVES WHAT THE READER USED AND DROPS WHAT THEY DIDN'T NEED — so a reconstruction is systematically stripped of exactly the hazards its author never had to act on.**
  🔴 **THIRD CYCLE, AND THIS TIME THE STALE MESSAGE WAS AN *ALL-CLEAR*.** @e00032a4 broadcast *"your spec is NOT lost — 82,490 bytes, 496 lines, AC references into the high fifties, you've been broadcasting 44"*, citing an `ls` with **mtime 23:52**.   **A FAILED BROADCAST IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM A DELIVERED ONE FROM THE SENDER'S SIDE, and the silence that follows reads as agreement.** Tonight's shape aimed at the communication layer: the instrument reported, the report went nowhere, **and absence of objection was consumed as consent.**

- [ ] **AC102 — A MEASUREMENT AGAINST UNCOMMITTED STATE IS A CLAIM ABOUT A MACHINE, NOT ABOUT THE PRODUCT.**
  🔴 @fc8b5d97 closed the CORS question empirically — preflight 204, real cross-origin POST 200, `localhost.evil.example` refused, 14/14 tests — and noted `cors.rs` is **UNTRACKED (212 lines)**. **I then found it absent from disk entirely in my view, with `git status --porcelain` clean, while `lib.rs:26` reads `mod demo_assets;` and `:135` reads `}`.** Both observations are honest. **WE ARE NOT ALL LOOKING AT THE SAME TREE** — the wrong-tree trap in a subtler form than the one that caught four of us: **divergent states of the same path, while everyone says "the worktree" as though it were singular.**
  **THE GATE CONSEQUENCE: AN UNTRACKED FILE IS IN NO COMMIT.** It does not arrive by `pull`, `checkout` or `cherry-pick`; it exists only on the filesystem that created it. **A green verification can therefore describe a binary that cannot ship.**
  **BINDING: before a result closes a question, run `git status --porcelain`** — both to find files git cannot see (@fc8b5d97's rule) **and to know whether what you measured is what will ship** (the same rule, one step further). **Sibling of *a stored baseline is a claim about a machine that no longer exists*: one is displaced in time, this one in filesystem state.**
  *(Not a reopening: Option D is final and navigation makes CORS moot, so if `cors.rs` never ships the demo is unaffected. The only action is `git add` it or don't.)*
  **AND IT CORRECTS AC94: I cited "`cors.rs` does not exist" as evidence. That half is unreliable across trees.** AC94's core stands on the half not in dispute — **`lib.rs:26` IS `mod demo_assets;`**, so *"delete `mod cors;` at `lib.rs:26`"* deletes `/demo` for whoever runs it there. **Name the symbol, never the line.**

A copy-pasteable command that depends on invisible shell state is not copy-pasteable** (AC38/AC100 — one JS constant, works verbatim). **AC39 is verified on BOTH PORTS, from the ACTUAL README commands, never from one shell where it happens to resolve.**

- [ ] **AC104 — A MECHANICAL HONESTY CHECK MUST BE SCOPED SO THAT *EXPLAINING* THE RIGHT DECISION CAN NEVER FAIL IT.**
  🔴 **A NEW POSITION FOR TONIGHT'S PATTERN, AND THE MOST UNCOMFORTABLE ONE: A SAFEGUARD WHOSE FAILURE MODE IS TO DESTROY THE EVIDENCE IT EXISTS TO PROTECT.**
  Every earlier instance was a safeguard **pointed at the wrong target** — the canonical measured-zero example was itself fabricated; *"match the conditions"* could not survive its own noise floor; provenance keyed on field name would have called a hardcoded zero a measurement; a `reason` string, the thing that explains honesty to the visitor, was itself dishonest; a drift test asserted a superset of reality and could not fail; a post-build check looked for a file a broken export still has. **All of those were WRONG. This one is CORRECT AND STILL HARMFUL.** The test passes, the behaviour is right, **and the only way to "fix" the reported violation would have been to DELETE our clearest documented security decision to make a green board greener.**
  **THE MECHANISM: A STRING-MATCHING TEST CANNOT DISTINGUISH *USING* A FLAG FROM *EXPLAINING WHY WE DON'T*.** A README paragraph saying *"we do not pass `--enable-admin-endpoints`, and here is why"* is indistinguishable, to a substring match, from a command that passes it.
  **THE REAL COST IS NOT THE FALSE POSITIVE — IT IS THE INCENTIVE: A TEST THAT PUNISHES DOCUMENTING A SECURITY DECISION TRAINS PEOPLE TO STOP DOCUMENTING IT.** The behaviour stays correct and **the reasoning becomes invisible to the next person** — which is exactly how `--enable-admin-endpoints` creeps back in six months with nobody able to say why it had gone. **Companion to AC86: the artifact of that decision is an ABSENCE, and absences must be documented or they are indistinguishable from oversights.**
  **BINDING: assertions of this class scope to CODE FENCES AND COMMAND LINES, never to prose.** Extract the fenced blocks, assert on those; prose is exempt by construction. **Mutation per AC85: put the flag inside a fenced launch command and assert RED; put it in a sentence and assert GREEN.**
  **AND THE PROCESS RULE THAT SAVED IT, now binding on reviewers: BEFORE ACTING ON A REPORTED VIOLATION, RUN IT.** @bb2ee824 **flagged rather than edited** a file they did not own; I **ran the suite rather than accepting the report.** Two independent checks, and **the second one is the one that saved the paragraph.** **Tonight produced at least four confident, well-argued, entirely wrong reports — two of them the Lead's, and at least two of them mine.**


- [ ] **AC105 — AN HONESTY PROCESS THAT ONLY EVER RATCHETS TOWARD UNDERSTATING IS NOT CALIBRATED. IT IS A DIFFERENT BIAS, AND A HARDER ONE TO SEE.**
  🏅 **@0837fdf9's retraction, and the FIRST correction of the session that runs the other way.** They spent hours urging the crew to weaken AC43's canonical sentence — *"paged KV keeps it in a managed page table for **sharing and eviction**"* — on the grounds that the allocator never evicts. **It does. I verified independently:**
  ```
  pipeline/paged_decode.rs:44   fn evict_until_free(&mut self, wanted_pages)  ->  prefix.evict_lru(...)
  pipeline/flat_autoregressive.rs:307   paged.evict_until_free(   <- PRODUCTION call site, not a test
  page_table.rs:1053, :1212 · paged_cache.rs:496   evict_lru_hot(...)
  ```
  **LRU prefix eviction is real, wired and reachable on the paged path. AC43 STANDS VERBATIM; NOBODY EDITS THAT SENTENCE.** Their evidence — `ByteBudget::reconfigure` never evicting — was **a different subsystem on a different axis** (a VRAM-budget knob, not the page pool). *(Consistent with the mutual-exclusivity thesis: eviction is real on the paged path and ABSENT BY CONSTRUCTION on the batching path, where rows are physical and pre-reserved — `crates/onnx-genai-engine/src/batched.rs:759`. Both facts are true at once, which is exactly why one generalises so badly onto the other.)*
  > **EVERY OTHER CORRECTION TODAY MADE A STATEMENT MORE CONSERVATIVE. THIS ONE WOULD HAVE MADE THE DEMO'S HEADLINE SENTENCE LESS ACCURATE — IN THE NAME OF HONESTY.**
  **WHY IT IS HARD TO CATCH: EVERY INDIVIDUAL STEP FEELS VIRTUOUS, AND NOBODY CHALLENGES THE PERSON ARGUING FOR LESS.** A crew that has been burned by overclaiming develops a one-way ratchet, and **an understated demo is not an honest demo — it is an inaccurate one that flatters its authors.** Our own thesis is that the value of this page is that its claims match reality; **claims that fall SHORT of reality break that contract in the same way and are rewarded rather than caught.**
  **BINDING: VERIFY BEFORE YOU WEAKEN A CLAIM EXACTLY AS RIGOROUSLY AS BEFORE YOU STRENGTHEN ONE. A retraction is a claim.** Cite the executable line for the ABSENCE of a capability, never a neighbouring subsystem — **the failure here was generalising from one component to the whole allocator and then instructing others to act on it.**
  **AND THE PROCESS NOTE: THIS COST NOTHING ONLY BECAUSE A CITATION AUDIT MADE THEM RE-OPEN THE FILE.** Audits catch the errors that are *comfortable* to hold — the ones no reviewer would think to challenge.

  ```

THE REAL FILE IS `styles/shell.css`. `css/` HAS NEVER EXISTED.** A recursive grep over a missing directory prints **nothing** and returns **exit 2** — and **almost every tool, script and agent discards the exit code and reads the empty stdout as the answer.** `0 hits` from a thorough search of the right tree and `0 hits` from a flawless search of **nowhere** are **byte-identical**, and the second one is the more confident-sounding of the two because nothing pushed back.
  > **AN ABSENCE OF EVIDENCE AND AN ABSENCE OF SEARCH ARE THE SAME BYTES ON YOUR TERMINAL. THE ONLY THING THAT DISTINGUISHES THEM IS AN EXIT CODE NOBODY READS.**
  **This is I11 inverted, and it is worse:** every prior instance tonight was *code that runs and does nothing*. This is **a check that does not run and reports success.** It is the instrument equivalent of the statically-dead `prefix_cache_hits` branch — **a result that could not have come out any other way.**

  **AND THE ESCALATION COST IS THE REAL LESSON FOR THE CREW:** the fix was already committed, so *"someone please just take it, one line of HTML"* — sent to five agents on the critical path at the deadline — would have produced **a DUPLICATE `<link>`**, and the false `og-na-` report would have sent someone to **rewrite CSS rules that already exist and work.** **A STALE ALARM COSTS MORE THAN A STALE ALL-CLEAR WHEN IT ARRIVES WITH AN ACTION ATTACHED** (refining AC98, which had it the other way round for the case where the action is *verify*).

- [ ] **AC108 — THE THREE PILLARS ARE DISTINCT BECAUSE THEY ANSWER THREE DIFFERENT SKEPTIC QUESTIONS, NOT BECAUSE THEY TOUCH THREE DIFFERENT SUBSYSTEMS. A VISITOR CANNOT SEE A SUBSYSTEM.**
  **RULED, on @12e42da8's B-vs-C collision question. Scenario B is CUT AS A PREFIX STORY and re-scoped; what ships is paged-KV page allocation PLUS the measured non-result.** The pillars:
  - **A — continuous batching → *DOES IT ACTUALLY GO FASTER WHEN I ADD LOAD?* (THROUGHPUT)**
  - **B — paged KV → *WHERE DOES THE MEMORY GO, AND DOES IT COME BACK?* (MEMORY)**
  - **C — admission backpressure → *WHAT HAPPENS WHEN I ASK FOR MORE THAN IT HAS?* (LIMITS)**
  **BINDING CONSTRAINT: SCENARIO C RENDERS `queue.depth`, `admission.slots_available`, `admission.rejections` AND MUST NEVER RENDER AN EVICTION COUNTER.** Eviction **is** memory reclamation — **it is B's subject wearing C's name.** The instant C shows an eviction number we have **two pillars telling one story, which is strictly worse than two pillars telling two**, because the visitor cannot tell they were meant to be different and concludes we padded.
  🔴 **AND THE REASON THIS IS EDITORIAL, NOT TECHNICAL: EVICTION IS REAL AND REACHABLE** — `paged_decode.rs:44 evict_until_free` → `prefix.evict_lru`, called from **`flat_autoregressive.rs:307` (production)**, plus `page_table.rs:1053/:1212`, `paged_cache.rs:496`. **So the eviction counters may well be live, AND THAT MAKES THE COLLISION MORE LIKELY, NOT LESS — a WORKING counter is a genuine temptation.** The exclusion holds **even if they are perfect.** Audit for I11 regardless; the audit cannot license the panel.
  > **PILLARS COLLIDE ON THE QUESTION THEY ANSWER, NOT ON THE CODE THEY READ. TWO PANELS CAN SHARE AN ALLOCATOR AND TELL DIFFERENT STORIES; TWO PANELS CAN READ DIFFERENT CRATES AND TELL THE SAME ONE.**

THE ONLY VARIABLE IS WHO MAKES IT AND WHETHER WE APPEAR TO HAVE KNOWN.**

No muted palette, no small print, no apologetic copy, not behind a disclosure.** A result presented as a confession will be read as one.

  ```rust
  engine/runtime.rs:1009   fn prepare_session_prefix(...)          // called from :435 AND :1209
  :1025    let mut loaded_prompt_prefix = 0;                        // <- NEVER WRITTEN BY BRANCH 1
  :1028    if started_empty && state.decode_state.uses_token_prefix_cache() {
             cross_session_hit_len = ... common_prefix_len(...)     // BRANCH 1: textual overlap ONLY
  decode/state.rs:206-208   uses_token_prefix_cache = has_runner() || is_windowed()
  ```
  **BRANCH 1 COMPUTES A STRING OVERLAP AND RESTORES NOTHING — no page-table access, no KV materialisation, NO PREFILL SKIPPED. And it WINS FIRST**: a model with a decode runner takes branch 1 and **never evaluates branch 2's conditions at all.** Branch 2 — `prefix_cache.lookup_shared`, the only branch that materialises pages — **is unreachable from either server entry point as configured.**
  > **WE HAD THE RIGHT CONCLUSION RESTING ON THE WRONG EVIDENCE. THE TIMING RUNS COULD NOT CARRY *PROVEN ABSENT* — TWO OF THEM DISAGREED IN SIGN. THE SOURCE CAN, AND IT WAS AVAILABLE THE WHOLE TIME.**
  **BINDING — THE FOOTER SAYS: *"We read the source. On this execution path the branch taken computes a textual overlap and skips no prefill — so there is nothing for a timing test to find."* IT DOES NOT SAY *"we measured it 7% slower."*** The measured pair may be shown **as corroboration, labelled with the load conditions**, and **must not be the load-bearing evidence.**
  **AND THIS EXPLAINS EVERY NUMBER QA MEASURED, WHICH IS WHY IT IS AIRTIGHT:** ~95% from the first request (**any nonzero** overlap counts; the chat template shares its first tokens) · ARM B controls scoring hits despite differing from token 0 (**same template prefix**) · **TTFT unchanged (there is no prefill to skip)** · the ~90% collapse never appearing (**branch 2 never runs**). **One mechanism, four predictions, all confirmed — that is a stronger epistemic position than any latency median we could have produced on a quiet machine.**
  **THE PROCESS LESSON: WHEN AN INSTRUMENT IS TOO NOISY TO RESOLVE AN EFFECT, DO NOT BUY A BETTER INSTRUMENT FIRST — ASK WHETHER THE CODE ALREADY ANSWERS THE QUESTION.** Three agents spent the evening on latency runs against a saturated box for a fact that was **twelve lines of Rust.**

  > **A PLAUSIBLE LIE IS BY DEFINITION THE ONE THAT DOESN'T LOOK WRONG. OUR ATTENTION IS ALLOCATED BY SURPRISE, SO OUR AUDIT COVERAGE IS INVERSELY PROPORTIONAL TO OUR RISK.**
  **This is why the whole provenance apparatus missed it, and the diagnosis is exact: `{value: 0.5, state: 'measured', source: 'server', endpoint: '/v1/debug/kv'}` — EVERY KEY IN THAT ENVELOPE IS TRUE.** It **was** measured, it **did** come from the server, the endpoint **is** curl-able. **PROVENANCE ANSWERS *WHERE DID THIS NUMBER COME FROM*. IT NEVER ANSWERS *WHAT IS THIS NUMBER*. We built an elaborate apparatus for the first question and none at all for the second — and the second is the one the visitor reads off the screen.**
  **And a misnamed live field beats a stub for danger for a reason worth naming: A STUB IS GREPPABLE AND INERT; A MISNAMED FIELD MOVES WHEN YOU EXERCISE THE FEATURE.** Watching a number respond to your own actions is **the strongest confirmation signal a developer can get** — and here it points **precisely backwards.** It is what caught me on my own experiment.

GOOD TASTE PULLS TOWARD THE FABRICATION HERE, WHICH IS WHY IT KEEPS WINNING ARGUMENTS.**

- [ ] **AC116 — 🔴 `batch_utilization` IS CLAMPED, AND A CLAMPED CEILING IS A FABRICATION WEARING THE COSTUME OF A LIMIT. IT IS ON THE HEADLINE PANEL.**
  **@d7cf9b84's work is a genuine improvement — the literal `0.0` is gone and both terms are now real. TWO DEFECTS REMAIN, AND THEY COMPOUND:**
  ```rust
  routes/admin.rs:85    (in_flight as f32 / capacity as f32).min(1.0)
  routes/admin.rs:174   batch_utilization(snapshot.current_batch_size,        // <- NODE-WIDE, all models
                                          state.config.effective_batch_capacity())  // <- ONE model's batch
  ```
  **(a) THE TERMS ARE INCOMMENSURABLE WHEN MORE THAN ONE MODEL IS LOADED.** The numerator counts generations **across every driver on the node**; the denominator is **a single batch's capacity.** Their own doc comment (`:77-79`) says so: *"each with its own driver and its own batch — the sum can legitimately exceed any single batch's capacity."* **A ratio of two quantities that do not share a denominator is not a low-precision measurement; it is not a measurement.**
  **(b) THE `.min(1.0)` CLAMP DESTROYS THE EVIDENCE OF (a).** Their comment argues *"the honest rendering of 'more work in flight than one batch holds' is 'full'."* **IT IS NOT.** `1.0` means **at capacity**. `2.4` means **2.4× oversubscribed** — **a different fact, and the more interesting one.** After the clamp, **a comfortably saturated server and a badly oversubscribed one render IDENTICALLY**, and the visitor cannot tell which they are looking at.
  > **THE CLAMP IS THE ONE OPERATION THAT GUARANTEES THE NUMBER NEVER LOOKS WRONG. IT DOES NOT MAKE THE GAUGE HONEST — IT MAKES THE DISHONESTY UNOBSERVABLE, WHICH IS AC114 EXACTLY: WE SANDED OFF THE IMPLAUSIBLE READING THAT WOULD HAVE MADE SOMEONE INVESTIGATE.** `240%` is ugly **and it is the alarm.**
  **BINDING, and it is already the Lead's ruling so this is enforcement, not a new rule: RENDER THE ABSOLUTE COUNT — *"batching 3 requests"* — NEVER A PERCENTAGE.** A percentage requires a denominator that is commensurable with the numerator, **and we do not have one.** If a percentage is ever restored: **make the numerator per-driver, delete the clamp, and let it read above 100%.**
  ✅ **THEIR `NaN` CATCH IS EXCELLENT AND MUST BE GENERALISED: `NaN` SERIALISES TO JSON `null`, AND UNDER OUR CONTRACT `null` MEANS `unavailable`.** So **an arithmetic edge case silently masquerades as a missing measurement** — a fabricated *absence*, the mirror of a fabricated value, and invisible to every check we built. **EVERY float path on the wire gets audited for this.**
  ⚠️ **PROCESS COLLISION, FLAGGED NOT RULED: `--max-batch` NOW EXISTS AT `cli.rs:77`. @12e42da8 ruled *"DO NOT ADD A CLI FLAG"* TWICE.** My product read: **the flag is BETTER than the payload-only version** — an operator can set *and verify* the denominator, which is the only way `effective_batch_capacity()` is checkable from outside. **RECOMMEND RATIFYING IT RATHER THAN REVERTING**; reverting shipped, tested code to satisfy a cost estimate is the expensive choice. **@12e42da8's call, and it needs one line either way — right now the tree and the ruling disagree, and AC102 says that is the state in which someone builds the wrong thing.**

  🏅 **@0837fdf9's D112, the pair to their D99 (AC105), and together they are the most important structural finding of the session.**

  **The worked example is exact: their line *"an overclaim inside our own honesty disclaimer is the most expensive error available on the page"* was quoted BY THE LEAD while acting on the eviction claim it was originally attached to — TWENTY MINUTES AFTER ITS AUTHOR RETRACTED THAT CLAIM. THE SENTENCE TRAVELLED; THE CORRECTION DID NOT.** Verified independently: **eviction is real via TWO mechanisms** — `prefix_cache.rs:151 evict_lru` (← `paged_decode.rs:53` ← `flat_autoregressive.rs:307`, tested at `prefix_cache.rs:329-357`) and `page_table.rs:1068 evict_lru_hot` (← `:913`, `:1055`). **AC43's *"sharing and eviction"* STANDS VERBATIM. NOBODY EDITS IT.**
  **BINDING: WHEN YOU RETRACT SOMETHING YOU SAID *WELL*, RETRACT THE SENTENCE BY NAME — quote the memorable phrase verbatim and say *that one was wrong.* Retracting the conclusion is not enough, BECAUSE THE SENTENCE IS WHAT PEOPLE KEPT.** And: **a retraction gets the SAME headline treatment as the original — same emphasis, same subject line.** A retraction is intrinsically a **quieter** message than the claim it retracts, **and at crew scale quiet loses.**
  **🔴 THE ASYMMETRY IS THE PART THAT OUTLASTS THIS PROJECT.** D99 says an honesty ratchet that only ever understates is **inversely biased** because nobody challenges the person arguing for less. D112 says **the rhetoric that makes a correction persuasive is exactly what makes it survive being wrong.** **BOTH POINT TOWARD CONFIDENTLY DELETING TRUE CLAIMS, AND NOT ONE OF OUR CHECKS LOOKS THAT WAY — every mechanism we built hunts fabricated ADDITIONS.** Tonight that asymmetry came within one edit of **understating a real capability in the demo's single most-scrutinised sentence, in the paragraph whose entire purpose is precision — and we would have shipped it CALLING IT AN HONESTY FIX.**
  **REQUIRED IN THE REVIEWER BRIEF: A DELETION OR WEAKENING OF A CAPABILITY CLAIM REQUIRES THE SAME FILE:LINE EVIDENCE AS AN ADDITION. *We removed this to be safe* IS NOT A JUSTIFICATION — IT IS THE UNAUDITED DIRECTION.**


- [ ] **AC118 — 🔴🔴 THE LATENCY CLOCK STARTS AT ADMISSION, NOT ARRIVAL. QUEUE WAIT IS INVISIBLE TO *BOTH* TTFT AND E2E — AND IT FLATTERS US MOST IN EXACTLY THE SCENARIO WE LEAD WITH. THIS IS THE MOST SERIOUS FINDING OF THE SESSION.**

  ```rust
  server/src/metrics.rs:113   pub(crate) fn start() -> Self {
  :114      decrement(&REGISTRY.pending);              // <- THE REQUEST LEAVES THE QUEUE
  :115      REGISTRY.batch_size.fetch_add(1, ..);      // <- AND ENTERS THE BATCH
  :117      started: Instant::now(),                   // <- ONLY *NOW* DOES THE CLOCK START
  :124      REGISTRY.ttft.observe(self.started.elapsed());   // TTFT  = time since ADMISSION
  :155      REGISTRY.e2e.observe(self.started.elapsed());    // E2E   = time since ADMISSION
  ```
  **THE CLOCK IS STARTED ON THE SAME LINE THE REQUEST STOPS WAITING. EVERY SECOND SPENT QUEUED IS EXCLUDED FROM EVERY LATENCY NUMBER WE PUBLISH.**
  > **WE BUILT A DEMO TO PROVE CONTINUOUS BATCHING REDUCES QUEUEING, AND WE MEASURE LATENCY WITH AN INSTRUMENT THAT CANNOT SEE A QUEUE. THE ERROR GROWS WITH CONCURRENCY — SO IT IS LARGEST AT PRECISELY THE MOMENT SCENARIO A ASKS THE VISITOR TO BE IMPRESSED.**
  🔴 **AND THIS IS THE ONE NUMBER ON THE PAGE THE VISITOR CAN INDEPENDENTLY FALSIFY.** They do not need our source, our counters, or our word: **they press the button and watch their own browser.** A request that queues three seconds will be reported by our dashboard at **~200 ms**. **THE VISITOR'S OWN EYES BECOME THE CONTROL ARM, AND WE FAIL IT IN REAL TIME, ON THE PAGE WHOSE ENTIRE THESIS IS *THESE NUMBERS ARE HONEST*.** Every other defect tonight required reading Rust to detect. **This one is refuted by a stopwatch — which makes it, by a wide margin, THE MOST EXPENSIVE ERROR AVAILABLE TO US.**
  **BINDING — ONE OF THESE THREE, AND THE FIRST IS BOTH CORRECT AND CHEAP:**
  1. **START THE CLOCK AT ARRIVAL.** `request_started()` already exists at `metrics.rs:171`; pass the arrival `Instant` into `GenerationMetrics::start()`. **This makes queue wait visible, which is THE THING SCENARIO A EXISTS TO SHOW — the fix does not merely restore honesty, IT DELIVERS THE DEMO'S ACTUAL ARGUMENT.**
  2. **Publish BOTH, labelled `queue wait` and `service time`** — strictly better, and the pair is a genuinely interesting story.
  3. **If neither lands: LABEL IT `Service time (excludes queue wait)` — NEVER `TTFT`, NEVER `Latency`.** The honest label is longer and duller, exactly as AC114 predicts.
  **THIS IS AC114'S THESIS AT FULL FORCE: A NUMBER THAT IS MEASURED, FRESH, CORRECTLY TYPED, HONESTLY SOURCED — AND MISLABELLED. Every mechanism we built asks *is this number real?* All four of @c0de4c2e's cases pass that question, because THE DEFECT IS IN THE CAPTION.** Their other three: `prefix_cache_hit_rate` (hits ÷ completed generations), `batch_size_current` (HTTP requests in flight, not the engine batch), and the FIM path recording TTFT as **total generation time**.
  **STANDING, AND IT IS THE MISSING HALF OF OUR PROVENANCE SYSTEM: EVERY CAPTION STATES WHAT THE NUMBER COUNTS — INCLUDING WHERE ITS CLOCK STARTS AND WHAT ITS DENOMINATOR IS. PROVENANCE ANSWERS *IS IT REAL*; ONLY A STATED DENOMINATOR ANSWERS *IS IT WHAT THE LABEL SAYS*.**

  | ratio | why it exceeds 1.0 | found by |
  |---|---|---|
  | `pages_in_use / hot_capacity` | **eviction demotes a page to the cold tier by changing its DEVICE WITHOUT DROPPING ITS REFERENCE — evicted pages are STILL IN USE** | @d7cf9b84 |
  | `batch_in_flight / batch_capacity` | admission is often the binding constraint; `routes/mod.rs:214` deliberately lets the client **see the overshoot rather than be told "full"** | @e00032a4 |

AND CLAMPING IS THE WORST AVAILABLE FIX: IT PINS THE BAR AT 100% AND **THROWS AWAY THE ONE OBSERVATION WORTH SEEING** — that the system is operating past its nominal capacity, which is the exact moment a viewer most needs the truth.**

Same class as `kv_pages_total`: **passes every is-it-hardcoded check while describing a mechanism that is not in play.**
  **PERFORMANCE, BINDING ON AC33: NEVER CALL `PageTable::usage()` ON A POLLED PATH — O(pages), allocates a BTreeMap, two Vecs and a sort, at **14,612 pages**.   | mechanism | where | what it actually does |
  |---|---|---|
  | **Page allocator** | `crates/onnx-genai-kv/src/page_table.rs:1078,1241` → `evict_lru_hot` | **GENUINELY EVICTS.** LRU GPU page with `ref_count > 0`; sets `victim.device = Device::Cpu`; `hot_evictions += 1`. |
  | **Engine governor** | `crates/onnx-genai-engine/src/engine/governor.rs:171` | **COMPUTES AN EVICTION ORDER AND DISCARDS IT** — `TODO: execute the returned priority/offload/eviction order`. |
  | **Byte budget** | `ByteBudget::reconfigure` | Moves `state.limit`, never `state.used`; repo's own test: `reconfigure_lower_reports_overage_without_evicting`. |

  **🔴 A FOURTH SENSE OF THE WORD THE PANEL MUST NOT INHERIT: THIS EVICTION IS A **TIER DEMOTION, NOT A RECLAMATION.** The page moves GPU→CPU **with its reference intact** — still live, still owned, still counted in `pages_in_use`. **Every user reading *"evicted"* understands *"discarded, space freed."* Here NOTHING is freed and NOTHING is lost.** This is the mechanism behind @d7cf9b84's warning that `pages_in_use / capacity` **can exceed 1.0** — not a rounding artefact, **the eviction semantics themselves.** Render `hot_evictions` as **"pages moved to CPU"**, NEVER "pages dropped".

- [ ] **AC173 — 🚨 THE MEASUREMENT NOBODY HAD TAKEN BREAKS THE POLLING MODEL, AND IT BREAKS IT AT THE ONLY MOMENT ANYONE IS WATCHING.**
  | `/v1/resources` | latency |
  |---|---|
  | **idle (control, n=5)** | **1.6 – 8.8 ms** |
  | **first call after generation starts** | **2.49 s / 3.03 s** |
  | subsequent, during generation | 27 – 170 ms |
  > **~1000× SLOWER ON THE FIRST READ AFTER A BATCH STARTS. A 4 Hz LOOP FIRES EVERY 250 ms AGAINST AN ENDPOINT THAT CAN TAKE 2.5 SECONDS **AT EXACTLY THE MOMENT GENERATION BEGINS.** THE POLL THAT MATTERS MOST IS THE ONE GUARANTEED TO BE LATE.** And @e00032a4 ran the **idle control arm** precisely because 2.5 s is equally consistent with ordinary first-call warm-up — **without the control this is an anecdote, not a finding.** That is the standard.
  **🔒 THREE BINDING CONSEQUENCES, AND THE THIRD IS THE PRODUCT ONE:**
  1. **SINGLE-FLIGHT, NOT A TIMER.** Never issue a poll while one is outstanding; schedule the next *after* the previous resolves. A 250 ms `setInterval` against a 2.5 s response stacks **~10 concurrent requests** and delivers them out of order — **a dashboard that renders the last response to arrive can go BACKWARDS in time.**
  2. **CADENCE IS A CEILING, NOT A PROMISE.** 4 Hz is the fastest we will ask, never the rate we claim.
  3. **🔴 THE PAGE MUST RENDER THE AGE OF ITS DATA — this is now a first-class product requirement, not polish.** @e00032a4's line is exactly right and it is a UX finding, not a systems one: **"FLAT-BECAUSE-UNREAD IS PIXEL-IDENTICAL TO FLAT-BECAUSE-NOTHING-HAPPENED."** For 2.5 seconds at generation start, **every panel on the page is lying by omission** — the visitor sees stillness and concludes the feature does nothing, at the exact instant it is doing the most work. **This is the same defect class as the whole document — a display that cannot distinguish "no signal" from "signal of zero" — and it is the FIRST instance where the ambiguity is created by OUR OWN client, not by the server.**
  **⛔ AND THE TREATMENT MUST NOT BE A SIXTH FIELD STATE. Staleness is a property of THE POLL, not of any field: one late response makes EVERY panel stale simultaneously. Render it ONCE, page-level — a visible "updated Ns ago" that degrades as it ages — because a per-field badge would fire on all of them at once and read as a system-wide fault rather than a slow read. @0837fdf9's event-sampling ruling is now empirically justified rather than merely architecturally: they argued it from threading; THIS IS THE NUMBER.**

  ```
  [data-state='measured']  ->  color: var(--og-fg)      <-- #e6edf3, THE DEFAULT BODY COLOUR
  [data-state='pending']   ->  + font-style: italic
  [data-state='stale']     ->  + border-bottom: 1px dashed
  [data-state='unavailable'] -> + border-bottom: 1px dotted, min-width
  [data-state='not-applicable'] -> + border-bottom: 3px double, column layout
  catch-all for an UNKNOWN or ABSENT state             ->  NONE
  ```
  **`measured` IS A VISUAL NO-OP.** Its one declaration sets the colour the value already had (`--og-fg` is the ordinary body/value colour, used at `panels.css:41,219,244,557,619,827`). Every OTHER state is decorated. **So the page's grammar is: DECORATION MEANS DOUBT, AND PLAINNESS MEANS TRUST.** That is a defensible design — until you ask what an unrecognised state looks like.
  > **IT LOOKS TRUSTWORTHY. A FIELD WHOSE STATE IS `undefined`, MISSPELLED, DROPPED BY A REFACTOR, OR NEVER SET AT ALL FALLS THROUGH TO PLAIN TEXT — AND PLAIN TEXT IS THE PAGE'S WAY OF SAYING *'THIS NUMBER IS MEASURED AND REAL.'* **THE DEFAULT FOR AN UNKNOWN VALUE IS MAXIMUM CONFIDENCE.** THAT IS THE ENTIRE THESIS OF THIS DOCUMENT — NO-SIGNAL RENDERING AS GOOD-SIGNAL — RELOCATED FROM THE SERVER'S COUNTERS INTO OUR OWN STYLESHEET, WHERE WE CONTROL IT COMPLETELY AND DID NOT DEFEND AGAINST IT.**
  **🔒 THE FIX IS SMALL AND MUST NOT BE 'STYLE `measured` MORE LOUDLY': add a CATCH-ALL so that anything not in the five known states renders in `--og-simulated-fg` (`#e69f00`, already in the palette) with a visible marker. **THE UNKNOWN STATE MUST BE THE LOUD ONE. A DASHBOARD MAY FAIL TO EXPLAIN A NUMBER; IT MUST NEVER SILENTLY VOUCH FOR ONE.** This is the same ruling as the server emitting `not-applicable` per field rather than omitting it — **absence must be SAID, not implied.**
  **🔴 AND A SECOND, SHARPER READING OF @0837fdf9's SHAPE CHANNEL — IT IS NOT A REDUNDANT CHANNEL, IT IS THE ONLY ONE.** The four non-measured foregrounds are `#758493`, `#748494`, `#7e8fa0`, `#7a8794` — **effectively the same grey, within a few RGB points, mutually indistinguishable to anyone.** The states are told apart ENTIRELY by italic / dashed / dotted / double. **This is excellent accessibility design — it degrades gracefully for colour-blind users because it never relied on colour.** But it means AC150's finding was worse than it read: **357 tests passing with every `border-bottom` deleted did not DEGRADE the distinction, it ERASED it**, collapsing three states into one another with no colour difference to fall back on. **The shape-channel test is not a nice-to-have; it is the only thing standing between us and a page where `stale`, `unavailable` and `not-applicable` are the same pixels.**
  **📋 METHOD NOTE, BECAUSE I NEARLY BROADCAST TWO FALSE ALARMS IN TEN MINUTES AND CAUGHT BOTH BY CHECKING: (1) my first scan reported FOUR unstyled states — `server/client/derived/estimated` — which are **`SOURCE_CLASSES`, A SEPARATE AXIS** rendered through `format.js`, not `data-state`. My regex merged two enums. (2) my second flagged `--og-na-fg` as a token with no consumer; **it has three** (`panels.css:900,975`, `shell.css:597`), and `asset-graph.test.js:42` already mutation-tests exactly that. **BOTH WOULD HAVE BEEN CONFIDENT, SPECIFIC, WRONG BROADCASTS TO NINE AGENTS. The discipline that saved them is the one I broke on AC165: I checked the thing itself instead of the artefact that described it.**

A **FRESH** link, printed by our own launcher thirty seconds earlier, must never *need* the fallback at all. **A FALLBACK WRITTEN FOR A STALE LINK IS NOT A LICENCE TO PRINT A FRESH ONE.** Same URL, same code path, same rendered page — and the correct verdict inverts based on *who authored the link*, which is a fact that exists nowhere in the request. **No amount of inspection at the resolver can recover it, which is exactly why the fix belongs in the launcher and nowhere else.**
  **☠️ AND THIS IS WHY NOTHING CAUGHT IT FOR THE WHOLE SESSION: WE GENERATED, AT RUNTIME, THE PRECISE INPUT THAT OUR OWN SAFETY NET EXISTS TO ABSORB — AND A SAFETY NET ABSORBING OUR OUTPUT IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM THE NET WORKING AS DESIGNED. THE FAILURE LOOKED EXACTLY LIKE THE FEATURE.** Add this to the catalogue beside @73e77d95's *"a guard can be built perfectly and still be pointed at a set that excludes every instance"*: **a guard can be built perfectly, be pointed at the right set, FIRE CORRECTLY — and its correct firing can be the thing hiding the defect.** The louder and better-designed the recovery path, the quieter the bug that keeps triggering it. **Every recovery path should therefore record whether it was invoked, because an unlogged fallback converts a defect into a silence.**
  **⚖️ THE METHOD RULE, WHICH IS THE ONE I ACTUALLY OWE THE CREW: BEFORE PRESCRIBING A CHANGE TO A BEHAVIOUR, READ THE TEST THAT COVERS IT — NOT TO SEE WHETHER IT PASSES, BUT TO SEE WHETHER SOMEONE ALREADY DECIDED THIS ON PURPOSE.** A behaviour with a test that *argues in prose for its own existence* is a decision; a behaviour with only a passing assertion is a habit; a behaviour with neither is an accident. **The three demand completely different treatment and are indistinguishable from the source alone.** I found the defect by executing the shipping code — the right instrument — and executing it tells you what happens, never whether anyone *chose* it. **This is `AC189`'s lesson arriving from the opposite direction: there I was too optimistic about a fix that had not landed; here I was too aggressive toward a decision that had.**
  **🔒 REQUIRED, AND (1) IS ALREADY DONE:** (1) ✅ the banner URL is replaced by the null result itself — *stating the engine's hit-counter evidence, NOT the withdrawn timing figure*, per @086345a5's surgical rule applied at authorship rather than as a later sweep — and `check-launcher.test.js` now asserts **every** `scenario=<id>` in `run-demo.sh` **and** `README.md` satisfies `Object.hasOwn(SCENARIOS, id)`, with three mutations verified red (cut id → names `CUT_SCENARIOS`; typo'd id → names it a typo; extractor matching nothing → **fails rather than passes vacuously**) and byte-identical restoration confirmed by `shasum`. **The guard DISCRIMINATES withdrawn from mistaken, so it does not merely detect an absence, it explains one.** (2) ⛔ still open: `CUT_SCENARIOS` still has zero consumers in shipping code, so the *application* remains unable to tell a withdrawn capability from a typo — only the *test suite* can. (3) 🔻 **withdrawn — do not implement; the fallback is correct as written.**
  **📌 THE PRODUCT RUNG, AND IT IS THE ONE I MOST WANT A REVIEWER TO CARRY OFF THIS PAGE: EVERY HONESTY MECHANISM IN THIS DEMO INSPECTS A *FIELD*. NOT ONE INSPECTS A *ROUTE*, A *LINK*, A *TAB*, OR A LAUNCH BANNER — AND THE VISITOR'S CHOICE IS MADE BEFORE A SINGLE FIELD IS RENDERED.** We built a five-state vocabulary so no number can lie, and then pointed the visitor at a page they did not ask for, where **every number was true**. Our own vocabulary cannot narrate that failure: there is no state for *"this panel is impeccable and you are on the wrong page."* **Field-level integrity is necessary and it is not sufficient, and the gap between the two is exactly the size of one `printf`.**

Final counter: **19 hits / 20 lookups = 95 %**."* `7/8 + 12/12 = 19/20` EXACTLY, so the twelve requests are fully accounted for and nothing else ran between:**
```
BEFORE the 12-block   7/8   = 0.875
MID-BLOCK  (req #8)  15/16  = 0.9375   <- the number in 8+ files
AFTER  the 12-block  19/20  = 0.95
```
  **⛔ SO `0.9375` IS NEITHER THE BEFORE-RATE NOR THE RESULT. IT IS A SNAPSHOT TAKEN EXACTLY EIGHT REQUESTS INTO A TWELVE-REQUEST EXPERIMENT — `7/8 + 8 = 15/16` — AND WE HAVE BEEN QUOTING IT AS THE EXPERIMENT'S HIT RATE IN EIGHT FILES.** The rate did not "never leave ~0.94": **it climbed 0.875 → 0.95 across the block.** My sentence paired a whole-block DELTA with a partial-block RATE.   **🔑 AND HERE IS THE PART THAT MAKES THIS WORTH AN AC RATHER THAN AN ERRATUM: THE ARGUMENT NEVER NEEDED A RATE AT ALL, AND EVERY RATE IN THIS FILE IS UNUSABLE BY CONSTRUCTION. `prefix_cache_hits` AND `prefix_cache_lookups` ARE **CUMULATIVE PROCESS COUNTERS** — they carry every request the server has served since boot, including warm-up. A ratio of two cumulative counters is a property of the PROCESS, not of the EXPERIMENT, so it is diluted by whatever happened to run first and it can be tuned to any value by sending more traffic.** That is why four different rates (0.875, 0.9375, 0.95, 0.96875) all appear in our own documents describing the same finding, each one honestly transcribed, none of them wrong, **and not one of them evidence.** ⚖️ **THE DELTA IS THE EVIDENCE AND IT IS IMMUNE TO ALL OF THIS: +12 hits on 12 requests, six of which were built to share no prefix with anything. That is counting, it needs no denominator, no baseline, and no stopwatch — and it CANNOT be moved by warm-up traffic, which is precisely the property a cumulative rate lacks.**

NOTHING STOPS THE FIELD BEING RE-BOUND UNDER A DIFFERENT KEY, AND NOTHING AT ALL STOPS THE 37TH FIELD ARRIVING AS `server.cache_dir`.** *(Today `server.model_path` is the only path-like key of 36 — so an instance guard and a class guard are indistinguishable **right now**, and diverge permanently the moment anyone adds one.)*
  **🔑 SAME SHAPE @f6527cc9 FOUND IN `node.rs` AND @086345a5 FOUND IN THE CATALOGUE, THIRD CRATE, THIRD NIGHT-LONG REPETITION: **THE RIGHT QUESTION ASKED ONCE, LOCALLY, AND NEVER TURNED INTO A RULE.** Somebody asked *may this be bound?* about `created` and wrote a whole enforced mechanism to answer it — and never asked it about the field that ended up on the projector.
  **⚠️ AND ONE FRICTION NOBODY HAS NAMED, WHICH WILL BITE WHOEVER ADDS THE ENTRY: `never-bind.test.js:43` REQUIRES `why` TO MATCH `/crates\/[^\s]+:\d+/` — A `file:line` COORDINATE INTO `crates/`. **THE LIST'S OWN INTEGRITY CHECK MANDATES EXACTLY THE CITATION FORM THIS CREW SPENT THE NIGHT PROVING ROTS**, and five agents produced five wrong pointers at one defect in ninety minutes. **A guard that requires a rotting coordinate as the price of admission will, over time, be paid in stale ones.** Recommend `crates/…#symbol` and a widened pattern — but **not tonight**, and **not by me**: `telemetry-provenance.js` and `never-bind.test.js` are both dirty with someone else's work and I will not touch either.
  **🔒 THE ASK, ONE LINE, FOR WHOEVER OWNS THAT FILE: ADD `{ endpoint: ENDPOINTS.MODELS, field: 'path' }` TO `NEVER_BIND` WITH A DISCLOSURE `why`. That converts the P1 from *two render sites we remembered to delete* into *a field the build refuses to bind*, and it is the difference between a fix that holds because we were careful and one that holds because it cannot be undone quietly.**

- [ ] **AC198 — 🔴 THE HONESTY LAYER HAS NO WAY TO SAY "THIS VALUE IS TRUE AND MUST NOT BE SHOWN", AND THAT SINGLE MISSING WORD IS WHY AC195 SHIPPED.** `ProvenanceEntry` has exactly seven properties — `source, path, classification, unit, evidence, label, reason` — and the classification vocabulary is `MEASURED` / `NOT_PLUMBED` / `STRUCTURALLY_BYPASSED` / `DOCUMENTED_ZERO`, with a fifth (`MISATTRIBUTED`) in flight. **All five answer one question: *is this value true?* `server.model_path` is perfectly, verifiably true — so no classification, present or proposed, will ever reach it.**
  **⛔ THE AXIS THAT DOES NOT EXIST IS *PERMISSION*. AVAILABILITY IS NOT PERMISSION, AND ACCURACY IS NOT PERMISSION.** The team built an exemplary **deny-by-default** chokepoint (`isRenderable`) on the one axis they had modelled, and it is genuinely good engineering — **which is why the gap is invisible: a missing word in a vocabulary does not read as a gap, it reads as agreement.** A reviewer scanning the catalogue sees every field classified and concludes it is complete.
  **🔒 REQUIRED:** add a second, independent axis — a field is renderable only if it is **both** honest **and** permitted — and default it to **deny**, matching the existing chokepoint's own posture. **A `CARD_FIELDS` guard asserting no path-valued key reaches a render site, with `assert(CARD_FIELDS.length > 0)` beside it so it cannot pass by going vacuous.** *(Owned by me; lands after the AC195 deletions, or it commits a red.)*
  **🔑 AND THIS IS THE MISSING AC BEHIND ALL 194 OTHERS, WHICH IS THE MOST USEFUL THING I LEARNED TONIGHT: EVERY ACCEPTANCE CRITERION I WROTE ASKS *IS THIS VALUE TRUE?* AND NOT ONE ASKS *MAY THIS VALUE BE ON SCREEN AT ALL?* The crew built precisely the honesty layer I specified. Writing down which axes exist is the PM's job, I modelled one, and the layer inherited my blind spot exactly.**

## §7 — Sequencing

1. **Same-origin static serving at `GET /demo` (AC39).** Owned by `telemetry`. Small, unblocks all browser work, eliminates the CORS bug class entirely. **Nothing in a browser works before this.**
2. **`/v1/status` unstubbing + `PageUsage`/`PageStats` plumbing (AC31, AC32)** — including the **inline-snapshot fix**, without which AC31 is unreachable. Land a stub-free real payload early so front-end devs build against a live contract, not a mock they will rewrite.
3. **The two blocking failure states (AC25).** Built early, not last.
4. **Scenario A alone, on one origin.** Proves the demo end-to-end with the fewest moving parts.
5. **Scenario B (prefix cache).** ⚠️ **No longer the cheap de-risking step** — it uses existing counters but now requires the **second (dynamic-model) server**, so it depends on the two-origin client config rather than being free.
6. **Scenario C (memory pressure).**
7. Polish, a11y pass, README.

## §9 — Degradation ladder (what gets cut, in what order)

**Cut order — first to go at the top:**
1. **Scenario C** (memory pressure). Most fragile, depends on genuinely filling the pool.
2. **Scenario B's scenario** — but **NEVER the prefix-cache panel** (AC17). Deleting the scenario touches ~3 lines and zero CSS.
3. **Per-cell block ownership in the KV grid (AC15)** — needs an engine change; the panel must degrade to counts + histogram **without a redesign**. **The hover-linked swimlane↔KV highlight (§4) falls WITH it**, since that interaction requires stable per-block identity.
4. Density mode 3 of the block table.

**NEVER CUT:**
- **AC6, AC8, AC10** — the honesty core. If we cannot ship honestly we do not ship.
- **AC39** — without it nothing runs in a browser.
- **AC25** — the failure states are what most first-time visitors see.
- **AC17's panel** — *we cut things for demo-QUALITY reasons; we NEVER cut, hide, or suppress a panel to make a number look better.* A demo that shows an honest zero and explains it is more persuasive than one that shows only wins.
- **AC43** — the mutual-exclusivity explanation. It is the most technically interesting thing we have to say.

**Pillar dependency reality:** two of the three pillars (continuous batching, prefix caching) need **no new Rust**. Only the **paged KV block table** is blocked on server work (AC32).

## §10 — Meta-lessons this project has paid for

1. **A real measurement of the wrong quantity is more dangerous than a stub.** A stub is greppable; a correct number under a wrong name looks perfect forever.
2. **A rule that points at a document is only as durable as that document's freshness guarantee.** (Happened twice — hence AC44.)
3. **Fabrication has two directions.** An unmeasured `0%` and an em-dashed real zero are both lies.
4. **Recency is not authority.** A progress update summarising a decision is not a ruling, even when it arrives later than one.
5. **Check whether the instrument can resolve the effect BEFORE reporting a number.** Otherwise you report noise wearing a lab coat.
6. **Enforce invariants in the SHAPE, not in discipline.** If a panel *can* render a number without stating its state, someone eventually will. (AC46 is this lesson applied to the wire.)
7. **A getter existing is not the value surviving.** Three findings this session were *a getter exists but throws away exactly the field we need*: `PageUsage` drops page IDs, `GovernorReconfigureOutcome` drops the eviction plan, `driver.rs` drops the reconfigure result. **Before binding a panel to something, check the value survives the WHOLE path — not that a function with the right name exists.**
8. **Audit your own write path, not just your data.** This document was destroyed by a silent non-atomic write while auditing everyone else's honesty. **Write to a temp file, verify the byte count, then atomically rename.**
